## Case file: Wisconsin — the "ballots under the table" memory, and Wisconsin's real items
Tray: EXPLAINED: the clip people remember is Georgia's; the 3–4 a.m. jump was Milwaukee's absentee batch; the 2024 trucks clip; the 2022 Madison clip relabelled as Philadelphia. PROVEN as errors, found and fixed: 386 uncounted ballots found by the 2020 recount, and 31,000 ballots re-run in 2024.

## 61. There was no separate Wisconsin "ballots under a table" clip; that story is the Georgia footage
**UNRESOLVED.** Three targeted searches found no Wisconsin clip of ballots pulled from under a table, but an absence of results is not proof, so this stays off air as a flat statement. On air: *the clip most people remember was filmed in Georgia.* One real Wisconsin item (entry 63, ballots found underneath counted envelopes) is a plausible seed for the memory; that link is an inference, not a sourced fact.

## 62. Wisconsin's 2020 viral claim was "found 112k votes at 3 a.m." / "a 4 a.m. ballot dump"
**CONFIRMED.** It was Milwaukee's central count reporting its absentee ballots in one batch overnight. The state commission's own FAQ page on it returned an error and was not read.
- "The City of Milwaukee was still counting absentee ballots around 3 a.m., but that doesn’t mean that those ballots were" … "or appeared at that time." (PolitiFact)
- "It seems that these different states they found magical ballots at 4am." (the podcast video that pushed it, Nov 4, 2020)
- https://www.politifact.com/factchecks/2020/nov/04/facebook-posts/social-media-post-falsely-claims-wisconsin-found-1/ · https://leadstories.com/hoax-alert/2020/11/fact-check-episode-michigan-did-not-add-138000-votes-where-none-were-for-trump-and-there-was-not-a-4-am-ballot-dump-in-wisconsin.html

## 63. In the Nov 2020 Milwaukee recount, 386 uncounted absentee ballots were found underneath counted envelopes
**CONFIRMED.** Real, human error, found by the recount itself, and counted by a unanimous bipartisan canvass board.
- "Milwaukee election workers discover nearly 400 unopened, uncounted absentee ballots, found underneath opened, counted absentee ballot envelopes. The mistake was discovered on the fifth day of the recount."
- "not counting the 386 ballots on Nov. 3 was due to human error by new election inspectors."
- "The county board of canvassers agreed, voting unanimously to allow the ballots as part of the recount."
- https://www.fox6now.com/news/nearly-400-uncounted-ballots-found-in-wisconsin-recount

## 64. Nov 2024: a clip of trucks arriving overnight at Milwaukee's central count showed illegal votes
**CONFIRMED.** That it was fact-checked false: the city said the trucks carried legal absentee ballots, with media present. Something real happened the same night: 31,000 absentee ballots were recounted because tabulator doors had been left unlocked, with other safeguards ruling out tampering.
- "The footage shows nothing nefarious, however. There is no proof that any ballots delivered overnight were illegal and the trucks arrived with media present."
- "It is both expected and routine that absentee ballots—over 100,000 in this case—are counted and reported in the late hours of Election Night" (the city's election commission)
- "election workers count 31,000 absentee ballots all over again because some staff didn’t lock tabulator doors in the early morning" … "other security measures ensured there was no chance of tampering."
- https://dailycaller.com/2024/11/14/fact-check-no-this-video-does-not-show-illegal-votes-being-trucked-in-overnight-in-milwaukee (a syndicated copy of a Check Your Fact article) · https://votebeat.org/wisconsin/2024/11/06/election-day-madison-bomb-threat-milwaukee-absentee-ballot-recount

## 65. Nov 2022: a Madison poll worker initialling ballots was relabelled as "cheating in Philadelphia"
**CONFIRMED.** The clip was real; the place label was moved. A reverse-image search of the room identified the building. Initialling is a procedure state law requires.
- "AP’S ASSESSMENT: False. The video shows a poll worker in Madison, Wisconsin, on Tuesday. He was initialing ballots to be handed out to voters, a standard procedure mandated by state law"
- "The broadcast then switched to the clip of the poll worker and changed the location in the label to Madison. Social media versions of the video cropped out the location."
- "shall write their initials on the back of each ballot and deliver to each elector as he or she enters the voting booth."
- https://witf.org/2022/11/08/video-shows-wisconsin-poll-worker-not-cheating-in-philadelphia

## 66. Wisconsin's Legislative Audit Bureau (2021): 3 of 14,710 sampled absentee certificates lacked a voter signature
**CONFIRMED.** The same table: 8 lacked a witness signature, 15 a witness address, and 1,022 (6.9%) had partial witness addresses. 3 in 14,710 is about 1 in 4,900.
- "We reviewed 14,710 certificates for absentee ballots cast in 29 municipalities"
- "1,022 certificates (6.9 percent) had partial witness addresses;"
- "3 certificates (less than 0.1 percent) did not have a voter signature."
- https://legis.wisconsin.gov/lab/media/vxinij4f/21-19Summary.pdf

## 67. Eleven people who died before Nov 3, 2020 likely voted; their absentee ballots had already been mailed
**CONFIRMED.**
- "11 individuals who died before November 3, 2020, likely voted in the General Election. Clerks received potential data matches for all 11 individuals 10 days before the General Election. By that point in time, clerks had mailed absentee ballots to all 11 individuals."
- https://legis.wisconsin.gov/lab/media/rz1nj2dh/21-19full.pdf

## 68. The state commission's care-facility guidance did not comply with statutes
**CONFIRMED.** Guidance that broke statute is non-compliance, not fraud.
- "WEC issued written guidance directing clerks not to send or attempt to send special voting deputies to facilities and homes but to instead mail absentee ballots" … "This guidance did not comply with statutes."
- https://legis.wisconsin.gov/lab/media/vxinij4f/21-19Summary.pdf

## 69. "1 of 45 sworn complaints sustained"
**CORRECTED.** As of early June 2021, of the 34 complaints about officials' conduct or election-law violations: 25 were dismissed, 6 were not yet resolved, 2 were withdrawn, and 1 was resolved by directing an official to follow election law. "1 of 45 sustained" overstates the finality.
- "25 complaints were dismissed by WEC or its administrator" … "6 complaints were not yet resolved;" … "2 complaints were withdrawn by the individuals who had submitted them; and" … "1 complaint was resolved by the administrator in consultation with its chairperson. The decision directed an election official to follow election laws."
- https://legis.wisconsin.gov/lab/media/rz1nj2dh/21-19full.pdf (pp. 95–96)

## 70. The commission's rebuttal on care-facility ballots: 579 issued and 389 returned in 2020, against 810 and 633 in 2016
**CONFIRMED.**
- "November 2020: 579 ballots issued to SVD voters; 389 ballots returned" and "November 2016: 810 ballots issued to SVD voters; 633 ballots returned"
- https://urbanmilwaukee.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Special-Counsel-Report-Inaccuracies.pdf

## 71. The legislature's special counsel conceded that decertification "would not" change the result
**UNRESOLVED.** The special counsel's report itself was not opened, and the commission's rebuttal does not contain the phrase. Kept off air.
- Settled in the second top-up round: the report itself said decertification would not change who is president (AP, quoting the report; entry 419).

## Case file: Detroit (TCF Center, 2020) and Philadelphia — "observers locked out, windows covered, a 4 a.m. ballot dump"
Tray: EXPLAINED as a *ballot dump* or fraud. PROVEN as conduct problems on both sides (papered windows, challengers held at the door: "not always lawful or wise").

## 72. The overnight delivery was about 16,000 Election Day absentee ballots
**CONFIRMED.** As the Detroit adviser's estimate, in the Michigan Senate Oversight Committee's report (June 2021). Claims at the time ran from 30,000–45,000 to nearly 100,000.
- "estimated 16,000 ballots were delivered to the TFC Center around that time. Some other persons and media speculated it was nearly 100,000, but most reported about 30,000-45,000."
- "These ballots were submitted throughout Election Day at different locations, such as drop boxes, in the mail, and at the clerk’s main and satellite offices."
- https://misenategopcdn.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/99/doccuments/20210623/SMPO_2020ElectionReport.pdf (p. 26)

## 73. The van arrived about 3:30 a.m. and again about 4:30 a.m.; the "wagon" photo was a TV photographer's gear
**CONFIRMED.**
- "In the video, the van arrived around 3:30 a.m. and unloaded the absentee ballots."
- "The van arrived back once again around 4:30 a.m. to unload the final ballots."
- "it was a photo of a WXYZ-TV photographer hauling his equipment."
- https://misenategopcdn.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/99/doccuments/20210623/SMPO_2020ElectionReport.pdf (pp. 26–27)
- Detroit's elections director later explained that the late ballots were drop-box and satellite ballots collected after 8 p.m.: "And that is exactly what you experienced at 3 o’clock, 4 o’clock in the morning." https://www.kpbs.org/news/politics/2024/10/31/after-a-chaotic-vote-count-in-2020-heres-what-detroit-will-do-differently-this-year

## 74. The Republican-led Senate committee found no fraud, but called window-covering and removals by both sides "not always lawful or wise"
**CONFIRMED.**
- "This Committee found no evidence of widespread or systematic fraud in Michigan’s prosecution of the 2020 election."
- "such as covering windows, calling police, denying lawful challenges, and removing challengers. Those actions by both sides were not always lawful or wise"
- "the Committee found no evidence fraudulent activities were undertaken or that such actions led to irreparable harm to ballots or vote counting."
- https://misenategopcdn.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/99/doccuments/20210623/SMPO_2020ElectionReport.pdf (pp. 2, 13–14)

## 75. The committee recommended chain-of-custody manifests for ballots moved to the count
**CONFIRMED.**
- "the process of transferring ballots from the clerk’s office to other locations must be done with greater security and manifests so that there can be an accounting for each ballot sent and received between the two locations, establishing a chain of custody."
- https://misenategopcdn.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/99/doccuments/20210623/SMPO_2020ElectionReport.pdf (p. 27)

## 76. More than 570 challengers were in the room (227 Republican) against a cap of 134 per party, and late arrivals of both parties were held at the door
**CONFIRMED.** (secondary: PolitiFact relaying the Detroit Free Press and the city's lawyer).
- "At one point, there were more than 570 challengers in the counting room, the Detroit Free Press reported, including 227 Republican challengers."
- "election officials barred both Republican and Democratic challengers from reentering."
- "Election workers counting ballots asked security to place paper over the windows because they felt intimidated by Republican challengers banging on the doors and windows."
- https://www.politifact.com/factchecks/2020/nov/06/facebook-posts/over-100-republican-challengers-monitored-absentee/

## 77. The state audit reconciled Detroit's absentee count to within 21 of about 174,000
**CONFIRMED.** With the wording fixed: the net was 21 more names than ballots (more voters recorded than ballots), not 21 extra ballots. About 1 in 8,300.
- "Approximately 174,000 Absent voter ballots were cast in Detroit. Following the county canvass, 95 of 134 AV counting boards were out of balance."
- "The net number of ballots for the entire counting board was 21 more names than ballots, out of approximately 174,000 AV votes cast."
- https://www.michigan.gov/documents/sos/BOE_2020_Post_Election_Audit_Report_04_21_21_723005_7.pdf (p. 20)
- The March 2021 press release gave 17; the April 2021 report's 21 is the corrected figure (entry 305). How the counting boards fell out of balance, and how many, is in entries 295–309.

## 78. The audit found challenges were documented, not ignored
**CONFIRMED.**
- "This suggests that claims, made by some, that challenges were being ignored were not accurate."
- https://www.michigan.gov/documents/sos/BOE_2020_Post_Election_Audit_Report_04_21_21_723005_7.pdf (p. 19, fn. 16)

## 79. A Wayne County court (Nov 13, 2020) found the challengers' affidavits misread the process; no challenger affiant had attended the Oct 29 walk-through
**CONFIRMED.** (read via a public transcription of the order; the court's PDF is a scan). It was not a final order.
- "None of Plaintiff challengers affiants attended the session."
- "This is not a final order and does not close the case."
- "There is no evidentiary basis to attribute any evil activity by virtue of the city using a rental truck with out-of-state license plates."
- https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Costantino_v._City_of_Detroit

## 80. The related federal suit ended in sanctions on its lawyers, reduced on appeal
**CONFIRMED.** (Sixth Circuit, June 23, 2023). The sanctions are about the lawyers' conduct.
- "we reduce the City’s award to $132,810.62; and we reduce the state defendants’ fee award to $19,639.75."
- http://www.opn.ca6.uscourts.gov/opinions.pdf/23a0134p-06.pdf

## 81. Philadelphia 2020: Pennsylvania's Supreme Court held (Nov 17, 2020) that the election code sets no minimum distance for observers
**CONFIRMED.** Observers stood about 15–18 feet away, and the court held they could observe as the code prescribes.
- "these provisions do not set a minimum distance between authorized representatives and canvassing activities"
- "allowed candidate representatives to observe the Board conducting its activities as prescribed under the Election Code"
- "in each section by a distance of approximately 15-18 feet."
- https://www.pacourts.us/Storage/media/pdfs/20210604/015809-file-10581.pdf

## Case file: the New York-to-Pennsylvania "truck of completed ballots" (Oct 2020)
Tray: EXPLAINED.

## 82. The viral figure was 250,000–300,000 ballots; the driver's own sworn affidavit said 250 to 7,500
**CONFIRMED.** (USPS Office of Inspector General report; the text layer is an OCR of a redacted scan).
- "250,000 to 300,000 ballots across state lines to PA"
- "stated there could have been 250 to 7,500 ballots on trailer ID"
- https://www.oversight.gov/sites/default/files/oig-reports/USPS/21INV00781.pdf

## 83. A joint OIG, Postal Inspection Service and FBI investigation, with 20+ interviews, could not corroborate it; closed Feb 8, 2022
**CONFIRMED.**
- "conducted over twenty (20) witness interviews"
- "The above joint investigation could not corroborate" … "allegation of missing ballots. The investigation is complete and submitted for closure."
- https://www.oversight.gov/sites/default/files/oig-reports/USPS/21INV00781.pdf

## 84. The trailer held parcels, its seal matched, and every trailer is accounted for
**CONFIRMED.**
- "most, if not all the mail contents were packages, not letters."
- "The seal on the trailer matched the seal put on in Bethpage, NY."
- "This review did not reveal the presence of first class mail or ballots."
- https://www.oversight.gov/sites/default/files/oig-reports/USPS/21INV00781.pdf (pp. 2, 13, 19)

## 85. The comparator: the origin facility's election-mail log held about 150 ballot images for the whole season
**CONFIRMED.** The viral figure is about 2,000 times that (our arithmetic); even the driver's sworn upper bound is 50 times it.
- "Bethpage's log contained approximately one-hundred and fifty (150) images of ballots for the entire election season."
- "Multiple Postal Service employees recall seeing less than approximately one hundred (100) ballots the entire election season"
- https://www.oversight.gov/sites/default/files/oig-reports/USPS/21INV00781.pdf (pp. 2, 20)

## 86. The only large New York-to-Pennsylvania ballot flow found was printed general-election ballots from a Rochester printer
**CONFIRMED.** The OIG does not say this seeded the rumour; "may explain" is the most the episode says. The claim was launched at a Dec 1, 2020 press conference.
- "six-hundred and fifty thousand (650,000) ballots for PA. However, these ballots were either driven by Phoenix Graphics directly to respective election office in PA, or entered into the" (mail stream in Rochester, New York)
- https://www.oversight.gov/sites/default/files/oig-reports/USPS/21INV00781.pdf (pp. 1, 6)

## Case file: "2000 Mules" (2022) — drop-box video and phone tracking
Tray: EXPLAINED.

## 87. The state's bureau of investigation found the phone data "curious" but short of probable cause
**CONFIRMED.** (secondary: GPB quoting the bureau director's Sept 30, 2021 letter, which was not opened).
- "As it exists, the data, while curious, does not rise to the level of probable cause that a crime has been committed,"
- https://www.gpb.org/news/2022/06/14/evidence-gaps-in-2000-mules

## 88. The State Election Board dismissed three drop-box cases (May 17, 2022): household members returning family ballots
**CONFIRMED.** (secondary: GPB; the board's order was not opened).
- "in at least three cases so far, Georgia investigators say the video actually shows people legally dropping off ballots for eligible voters who are immediate family members living in their home."
- "I did not find anything to suggest a violation of Georgia code,"
- https://www.gpb.org/news/2022/06/14/evidence-gaps-in-2000-mules

## 89. The voter shown in the film returned ballots for himself and four family members
**CONFIRMED.**
- "returning absentee ballots for himself and four family members at a drop box before the 2020 election."
- "there were five legal electors living in the home, all family members." (GPB)
- https://www.ajc.com/politics/2026/08/filmmaker-dinesh-dsouza-settles-defamation-case-with-gwinnett-man-on-eve-of-trial/ · https://www.gpb.org/news/2022/06/14/evidence-gaps-in-2000-mules

## 90. The publisher apologised (May 31, 2024) and pulled the film and book
**CONFIRMED.**
- "has issued an apology and said it would halt distribution of the film and remove both the film and book from its platforms."
- https://www.npr.org/2024/05/31/g-s1-2298/publisher-of-2000-mules-election-conspiracy-theory-film-issues-apology

## 91. The film-maker's side conceded the drop-box videos were never linked to geolocation data
**CONFIRMED.** From the film-maker's own summary-judgment motion (Dec 20, 2024). The same motion insists the phone-data analysis itself has not been discredited.
- "and used in the film, were never linked to any geolocation data."
- "That part of the project is not at issue here, and has not been discredited"
- https://www.democracydocket.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/02/268-2024-12-20-DSouzas-motion-for-summary-judgment-WM.pdf (p. 2)

## 92. The court (Sept 30, 2025) recorded that the defendants did not contest that the "ballot mule" accusations were false; the voter was never geotracked
**CONFIRMED.** (the order itself).
- "are false." … "Such statements are defamatory as a matter of law."
- "Nor did the TTV Defendants ever geotrack"
- "do not dispute that the footage actually shows" [the voter] "legally depositing absentee ballots for himself and his family."
- https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.308676/gov.uscourts.gand.308676.337.0.pdf (pp. 8, 15–17)

## 93. The film-maker settled; "July 30" against "before an August 2026 trial"
**CONFIRMED.** Both are true. The parties told the court on July 30, 2026; the jury trial had been set for Aug 17, 2026; terms were not disclosed.
- "The parties have notified chambers via email that they have reached a settlement." … "The Pretrial Conference and Jury Trial are CANCELLED."
- "The jury trial of this case is scheduled to begin on August 17, 2026"
- "The settlement, details of which were not disclosed" (AJC)
- https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/65639379/ · https://www.ajc.com/politics/2026/08/filmmaker-dinesh-dsouza-settles-defamation-case-with-gwinnett-man-on-eve-of-trial/

## 94. The group behind the analysis told a court it had no names or documents, and the State Election Board dropped its enforcement suit (Feb 26, 2025)
**CONFIRMED.** (secondary: GPB/AP).
- "The organization said in a subsequent court filing it had no names, contact information or other documentary evidence to provide."
- https://www.gpb.org/news/2025/02/27/georgia-election-board-drops-suit-after-group-fails-produce-ballot-stuffing

## 95. Arizona's Attorney General's investigators also never received the group's data
**CONFIRMED.**
- "April 5, 2022 – TTV informed us their information would be used in a documentary 2000 Mules, we requested the information they purported to have"
- "As of this memorandum, TTV still ha s not provided any documentation or other information to support their allegations." [sic, an extraction gap]
- https://www.azag.gov/sites/default/files/2025-06/Election_Review_Summary.pdf (p. 6)

## 96. The film made over $9 million
**CONFIRMED.** (the court's order). A measure of how far it spread.
- "It made over $9 million."
- "It later debuted in theaters and was available online."
- https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.308676/gov.uscourts.gand.308676.337.0.pdf (p. 4)

## Case file: Bucks County, Pennsylvania — the "ripped-up mail ballots" video (Oct 2024)
Tray: EXPLAINED (fabricated).

## 97. US intelligence, the FBI and CISA said (Oct 25, 2024) that Russian actors made and amplified it, and local officials had already debunked it
**CONFIRMED.** The statement names Pennsylvania, not Bucks County. It states the basis only in general terms, so it is "the agencies said", not proof a viewer can check.
- "The IC assesses that Russian actors manufactured and amplified a recent video that falsely depicted an individual ripping up ballots in Pennsylvania, judging from information available to the IC and prior activities of other Russian influence actors, including videos and other disinformation activities. Local election officials have already debunked the video’s content."
- https://archive.dni.gov/index.php/newsroom/press-releases/press-releases-2024/4011-odni-pr-24-24 (read via a reader copy; the page refused a direct fetch)

## 98. The county board's bipartisan statement the same day: the video is fake
**CONFIRMED.**
- "This video is fake."
- "The envelope and materials depicted in this video are clearly not authentic materials belonging to or distributed by the Bucks County Board of Elections."
- https://www.buckscounty.gov/m/newsflash/home/detail/1151

## 99. "Later" the district attorney's office and Yardley Borough police said their investigation found it fabricated
**CORRECTED.** The same day, not later. The DA and police statement is dated Oct 24, 2024, the day the clip appeared.
- "Our investigation has concluded that this video was fabricated in an attempt to undermine confidence in the upcoming election."
- https://levittownnow.com/2024/10/24/video-showing-destruction-of-bucks-county-ballot-is-fake/ · https://politifact.com/factchecks/2024/oct/25/tweets/video-doesnt-show-an-election-worker-ripping-ballo/

## 100. The physical tells: glossy instead of matte, the wrong green, no return addresses, no punch hole; and Pennsylvania bars opening mail ballots before 7 a.m. on Election Day
**CONFIRMED.** With a source fix: the punch-hole detail is from the county spokesperson to PolitiFact, not from the AP/WHYY piece.
- "the dark green color on the left side of the outer envelopes — it is actually more of a kelly green — and the glossiness of the inner and outer envelopes, which in reality have a matte finish"
- "none of the envelopes in the video had voters’ return addresses written on them."
- "election officials must wait until 7 a.m. ET on Election Day before they can begin to process ballots cast by mail"
- "each return envelope has a punch hole so election workers can see whether the yellow secrecy envelope is inside."
- https://whyy.org/articles/russian-actors-fake-video-mail-in-ballots-bucks-county-elections-2024/ · https://politifact.com/factchecks/2024/oct/25/tweets/video-doesnt-show-an-election-worker-ripping-ballo/

## 101. It was debunked within three hours
**CONFIRMED.** As AP's reporting, attributed to local officials and law enforcement.
- "The video had taken off on social media Thursday but was debunked within three hours by local election officials and law enforcement after members of the public reported it."
- https://whyy.org/articles/russian-actors-fake-video-mail-in-ballots-bucks-county-elections-2024/

## 102. It was staged, not made with AI
**CONFIRMED.** With a source fix: the expert's quote is in PolitiFact, not AP.
- "There is nothing in this video that has the characteristics or tell-tale signs of AI-generation,"
- "I suspect that this is simply staged."
- https://politifact.com/factchecks/2024/oct/25/tweets/video-doesnt-show-an-election-worker-ripping-ballo/

## 103. It reached hundreds of thousands of views on X
**CONFIRMED.** (NPR).
- "where it racked up hundreds of thousands of views."
- https://www.npr.org/2024/10/25/nx-s1-5165593/russia-ballot-video-bucks-county

## 104. The "Storm-1516" label comes from an outside researcher, by account history
**CONFIRMED.** It is a researchers' label, not the government's.
- "The particular video about Bucks County came from an account that we were familiar with,"
- "It has originated Storm-1516 narratives before."
- https://www.npr.org/2024/10/25/nx-s1-5165593/russia-ballot-video-bucks-county

## Case file: the Georgia "Haitian voters" video (Oct 31, 2024) and the Arizona "overseas ballots" video (Nov 4, 2024)
Tray: EXPLAINED (fabricated).

## 105. US intelligence, the FBI and CISA said (Nov 1, 2024) that Russian influence actors made the Georgia video
**CONFIRMED.**
- "The IC assesses that Russian influence actors manufactured a recent video that falsely depicted individuals claiming to be from Haiti and voting illegally in multiple counties in Georgia."
- "The Georgia Secretary of State has already refuted the video’s claims as false."
- https://archive.dni.gov/index.php/newsroom/press-releases/press-releases-2024/4014-pr-28-24 (read via a reader copy)

## 106. No voter registration exists for the names shown; four licences carry the same photo and lack the state's red marks
**CONFIRMED.** As fact-checkers' findings, not official ones.
- "PolitiFact searched the names and birth dates visible on the IDs and found no voter registration records in the two Georgia counties mentioned."
- "The camera flips to show the man holding four driver’s licenses, with what appears to be the same photo in all of them."
- "marks in the top right corner, according to the Georgia Department of Driver Services website" (Lead Stories, on the missing red USA and GA marks)
- https://www.politifact.com/factchecks/2024/nov/01/tweets/video-shows-haitians-who-claim-theyre-voting-for-h/ · https://leadstories.com/hoax-alert/2024/10/fact-check-no-registration-on-file-for-haitians-who-claim-they-voted-in-georgia-for-kamala-harris.html

## 107. The Secretary of State's office called it false and probably foreign (Oct 31, 2024)
**CONFIRMED.** Via reporting; the office's statement page refused the fetch and no archived copy was found.
- "It is likely foreign interference attempting to sow discord and chaos on the eve of the 2024 Presidential election,"
- "This is obviously fake and part of a disinformation effort. Likely it is a production of Russian troll farms."
- https://www.politifact.com/factchecks/2024/nov/01/tweets/video-shows-haitians-who-claim-theyre-voting-for-h/ · https://www.wabe.org/?p=641115

## 108. The account that first posted it told a network it had been paid by an unknown source
**UNRESOLVED.** The network's report was not opened. Kept off air.

## 109. It was staged, not made with AI
**CONFIRMED.**
- "This is a cheap — and pretty lazy — fake."
- "This is likely to be a real video, but the story may not be,"
- https://www.politifact.com/factchecks/2024/nov/01/tweets/video-shows-haitians-who-claim-theyre-voting-for-h/

## 110. The video drew over half a million views
**CONFIRMED.** As one outlet's count; another outlet's "over 1 million" was not opened.
- "The video has garnered over half a million views"
- https://www.wabe.org/?p=641115

## 111. The Arizona "overseas ballots" video showed fake ballots
**CONFIRMED.** That it was a manufactured video, with a wording fix: it was a staged interview making claims, not footage of ballots.
- "Russian influence actors also manufactured and amplified a recent video that falsely depicted an interview with an individual claiming election fraud in Arizona, which involved creating fake overseas ballots and changing voter rolls"
- "The Arizona Secretary of State has already refuted the video’s claim as false."
- https://www.fbi.gov/news/press-releases/joint-odni-fbi-and-cisa-statement-110424

## Case file: "machines flipped votes" — Antrim County 2020, the machine maker's lawsuit, and the 2024 audits
Tray: EXPLAINED for flipping. PROVEN for Antrim's human card-loading error, and for the documented vulnerabilities (no exploitation shown).

## 112. Antrim County's hand count came "within 12 votes of 15,718"
**CORRECTED.** (the denominator). The net change of 12 votes is right, but 15,718 appears in no source read. The Senate committee's table puts total presidential votes at 15,969 certified and 15,982 in the hand recount: say *a 12-vote shift on about 16,000 votes*.
- "largely mirroring the machine-tabulation results from Nov. 3." (the Secretary of State, Dec 18, 2020)
- "7. Dec. 17 22,082 16,044 5,959 9,759 244 20 15,982 Hand Recount" (the committee's table)
- https://www.michigan.gov/sos/resources/news/2020/12/18/final-numbers-from-antrim-county-audit-continue-to-affirm-accuracy-of-election-results · https://misenategopcdn.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/99/doccuments/20210623/SMPO_2020ElectionReport.pdf (p. 15)

## 113. The scanners' own paper tapes were right on election night
**CONFIRMED.** In the committee's table, row 1, the tabulator tapes (the official results, not reported that night), shows 5,960 to 9,748 for the two leading candidates, close to the hand count; row 2, the clerk's computer (the unofficial results that were publicly reported), shows 7,769 to 4,509, the order reversed.
- "Tabulator tapes- official results (Not reported on election night)"
- "Clerk’s computer- unofficial results (publicly reported)"
- https://misenategopcdn.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/99/doccuments/20210623/SMPO_2020ElectionReport.pdf (p. 15)

## 114. The cause was human error in loading memory cards, not a security breach, and the county restated its results four times
**CONFIRMED.** (the state's forensic report). The one real residual error was a single vote omitted in a village ballot question.
- "The other 15 cards appeared to load normally, but 13 had not been updated after the ballot design changes."
- "the Antrim County incident was not caused by a security breach. There is also no credible evidence that it was caused deliberately."
- "the county restated its results four times to correct this and other errors"
- "the final reported outcome is potentially incorrect due to the omission of a single vote because of a separate human error."
- https://www.michigan.gov/documents/sos/Antrim_720623_7.pdf (pp. 3–5, 30)

## 115. The Senate committee called the Antrim manipulation claims "indefensible" and gave their promoters "zero credibility"
**CONFIRMED.** (now from the report itself).
- "manipulated the vote by hand or electronically are indefensible."
- "place all other statements and actions they make in a position of zero credibility."
- https://misenategopcdn.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/99/doccuments/20210623/SMPO_2020ElectionReport.pdf (pp. 16–17)

## 116. Wisconsin 2024: 327,230 ballots hand-counted, a 0.0% machine error rate, decided unanimously
**CONFIRMED.** From the commission's press release (read via the Internet Archive; the live page refused the fetch). The full staff audit memo was not opened, so the episode says "the Commission determined", not "the audit found". About 1 ballot in 10 statewide was counted by hand.
- "unanimously determined the error rate of the 2024 post-election voting equipment audit as 0.0%."
- "A total of 327,230 ballots, approximately 10 % of all ballots cast statewide, were counted by hand"
- "Conducting the voting equipment audit did uncover a small number of human errors"
- https://elections.wi.gov/news/audit-finds-no-election-machine-errors (read via the Internet Archive)

## 117. Georgia's 2024 ballot-image audit: zero QR-versus-text differences, and "86 intent differences plus 1 write-in error" on the hand-marked ballots
**CORRECTED.** (which ballots). The single write-in adjudication error was on the machine-printed (QR) ballots, not the hand-marked ones; the 86 voter-intent differences were on the hand-marked ballots. One slip in 5,025,863 machine-printed ballots.
- "A single discrepancy was identified in the audit with ImageCast X ballots and was traced back to a county adjudication mistake with a write-in vote (i.e. human error)."
- Table rows: "5,025,863 1 4" (machine-printed) and "271,399 86 0" (hand-marked)
- https://blog.citp.princeton.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/952/2026/04/georgia_ballot_image_audit_report_-_november_general_0.pdf (pp. 3–4)

## 118. Georgia's 2024 risk-limiting audit: 442 batches, 381 with no deviation at all
**CONFIRMED.** (the Secretary of State's release, read via the Internet Archive).
- "A risk limit of 5% was set"
- "Of the 442 batches, 381 (86.1%) had no deviation from the original candidate vote totals. Out of the other 61, all fell within an expected margin of error for a hand count."
- https://sos.ga.gov/news/georgias-2024-statewide-risk-limiting-audit-confirms-voting-system-accuracy (read via the Internet Archive)

## 119. Pennsylvania's audits found about six discrepancies in about 37,000 hand-counted ballots
**UNRESOLVED.** No Pennsylvania source was opened. Kept off air.
- Second top-up round: no 2020 Pennsylvania figure matching this was found either; Pennsylvania's 2020 audits are in entry 538.

## 120. The machine maker's suit against a cable network: summary judgment on falsity (Mar 31, 2023), then a $787.5 million settlement (Apr 18, 2023)
**CONFIRMED.**
- "The evidence developed in this civil proceeding demonstrates that is CRYSTAL clear that none of the Statements relating to Dominion about the 2020 election are true." (Delaware Superior Court)
- "The parties settled for $787,500,000 — about half of Dominion's original $1.6 billion ask." (NPR)
- https://courts.delaware.gov/Opinions/Download.aspx?id=345820 · https://www.npr.org/2023/04/18/1170339114/fox-news-dominion-defamation-trial-settle

## Case file: Arizona 2020 — "Sharpiegate" and the state Senate's contractor review
Tray: EXPLAINED for Sharpies and the contractor's fraud claims. PROVEN at small scale: one accidental deceased-voter instance, two double votes referred, 37 possible double votes forwarded, and chain-of-custody paperwork the Attorney General flagged.

## 121. Sharpies were officially issued, and the Attorney General's office said it was "now confident" they disenfranchised no one
**CONFIRMED.** (reporting of the office's Nov 5, 2020 statement). The county's ballots have offset columns, so bleed-through does not affect tabulation.
- "we are now confident that the use of Sharpie markers did not result in disenfranchisement for Arizona voters."
- "Those ballots have offset columns so if a voter marks the ballot and the ink bleeds through to the backside it would not impact tabulations." (the county spokesperson)

## Case file: the US Postal Service in 2020 — "sorting machines and mailboxes pulled to stop mail ballots" (the claim from the other side)
Tray: EXPLAINED as a plot against mail ballots. PROVEN as real machine and box removals and slower service, not shown to be aimed at ballots.

## 129. 712 sorting machines were removed from Oct 2019 to Aug 2020, against about 375 a year, 437 of them in June–August
**CONFIRMED.** (USPS Office of Inspector General).
- "removal of letter and flat processing machines across the country increased from an average of 375 removals a year from FY 2015 to FY 2019. Between October 2019 and August of 2020, 712 letter and flat processing machines were reduced – 437 (61 percent) of these machines were removed from June to August 2020"
- https://www.oversight.gov/sites/default/files/oig-reports/20-292-R21.pdf (p. 2)

## 130. The changes were made without a study of the impact on service, to hit financial targets
**CONFIRMED.** The OIG does not say the removals were aimed at ballots (its text was checked for any such finding).
- "it did not complete a study or analysis of the impact the changes would make on mail service prior to implementation."
- "were designed to achieve financial targets."
- https://www.oversight.gov/sites/default/files/oig-reports/20-292-R21.pdf (pp. 2, 15)

## 131. 776 of over 140,000 blue collection boxes were removed in summer 2020; the Western area jumped from 8 a month to 187 in August; removals were suspended Aug 18
**CONFIRMED.** Removals overall were below the yearly average; the spike was regional. About 1 box in 180.
- "The Postal Service has over 140,000 blue collection boxes"
- "an additional 776 boxes from June 15, 2020 through August 31, 2020."
- "collection box removals until after the 2020 election to avoid the appearance of any impact on Election Mail." (USPS's stated reason, not a finding)
- https://www.oversight.gov/sites/default/files/oig-reports/20-292-R21.pdf (pp. 4, 14–15)

## 132. On-time mail fell about 10 points in July 2020
**CONFIRMED.**
- "First-Class Single Piece declined from 90.1 to 79.7 (10.4 percentage points)."
- https://www.oversight.gov/sites/default/files/oig-reports/21-014-R21.pdf (p. 2)

## 133. Election mail then ran better than other mail: 93.8% on time against 88.1%, 98.1% in election week, and the 760 delayed pieces found were all delivered by Election Day
**CONFIRMED.** Only 52.9% of identifiable ballots could be measured.
- "Election Mail 133,772,172 93.8%" and "All Other First-Class Mail 6,395,986,390 88.1%"
- "Postal Service was only able to measure service performance for 71.5 million (or 52.9 percent) of the 135 million identifiable ballots"
- "During the week of the general election, 98.1 percent of identifiable ballots were processed in time to meet its service standard."
- "we only identified 760 delayed Election Mail mailpieces, all of which were delivered to election offices on or before election day."
- https://www.uspsoig.gov/sites/default/files/reports/2023-01/20-318-R21.pdf (pp. 2–6, 11)

## 134. USPS's own figure: 99.89% of ballots delivered within seven days
**CONFIRMED.** As USPS grading itself; paired on air with the OIG's figures.
- "99.89 percent of identified ballots mailed after September 4 were delivered within seven"
- https://about.usps.com/newsroom/national-releases/2021/0119-usps-releases-updated-2020-post-election-analysis-report.pdf

## Case file: 2024 from the other side — "20 million missing votes", Starlink, "statistical anomalies"
Tray: EXPLAINED (*20 million missing*, satellite internet, the anomalies model).

## 135. Certified totals: the losing 2024 ticket got 75,017,613 votes of 155,238,302; the winning 2020 ticket had 81,268,924 of 158,383,403, a gap of about 6.25 million
**CONFIRMED.** From the Federal Election Commission's certified results. 81,268,924 − 75,017,613 = 6,251,311; total turnout fell by 3,145,101; the other ticket rose by 3,086,426 (our arithmetic). The viral 20 million was more than three times the real gap.

## 136. The "20 million" came from partial counts, and the same number was used both ways
**CONFIRMED.** One camp used it as proof 2024 was stolen, the other as proof 2020 had 20 million fake votes.

- "Posts are using the comparison between the tallies as evidence of fraud during the 2020 election"
- "These claims are inaccurate. There have been no credible allegations of election fraud or evidence of votes that disappeared during the 2024 election."

## 138. Satellite internet connected pollbooks only in Tulare County, California
**CORRECTED.** (incomplete). Arizona's Secretary of State also deployed it in three counties to sync electronic pollbooks. The real thing underneath is pollbook connectivity, not tabulation, and some tabulators do briefly connect to transmit results.

- "Our voting machines are never connected to the Internet, not via Starlink or anything else." (Tulare County)

## 139. The "statistical anomalies" group has no confirming audit, recount or ruling
**CORRECTED.** There is a ruling. A federal judge in the Western District of Pennsylvania (June 12, 2026) dismissed with prejudice every request to recount, audit or disturb the certified 2024 results, on standing rather than the merits; refiled forward-looking claims were still pending in October 2026. Votebeat (Sept 3, 2026) found that the model behind the Pennsylvania claim flagged fraud in simulated data built to contain none.
- "All retrospective requests for relief, including any claim seeking to disturb, recount, or audit the certified results of the November 5, 2024 General Election, or to obtain a judicial determination of the accuracy of reported vote totals in that election, are dismissed with prejudice."
- "All said they doubt the model can accurately detect fraud in U.S. elections."
- "using simulated data built to contain no fraud. The model still found fraud at levels similar to"
- "The ETA’s leaders acknowledge that statistical analysis alone can’t prove fraud."
- https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/71883352/election-truth-alliance-v-schmidt/ · https://www.votebeat.org/national/2026/09/03/election-truth-alliance-walter-mebane-2024-election-fraud-model/
- The same researcher's 2020 paper used that model to call the 2020 Milwaukee and Allegheny estimates "negligble amounts that probably neither reﬂect nor result from bad acts." (sic). A model that reports fraud in clean data cannot settle a real election in either direction (entries 317–319). http://websites.umich.edu/~wmebane/inapB.pdf

**UNRESOLVED.** A single advocacy-adjacent secondary source, not opened. Kept off air.

## Case file: dead voters (Georgia 2020)
Tray: PROVEN at four ballots, all cast by relatives. EXPLAINED for *thousands*. OPEN — Homeland Security's 42,776 *deceased* on Georgia's rolls: (a) the method is unpublished and no count of ballots cast in those names exists; (b) the match method and list, and the state's own check, would settle it; (c) Homeland Security and Georgia's Secretary of State hold them. It is about rolls, not ballots.

## 141. Jan 6, 2021: 10,315 dead voters alleged; the state had found 2 potential cases
**CONFIRMED.**

**CONFIRMED.** The transcript names the dead voters and their relatives; those names are never used.

- "original allegation that there were 1200 dead" … "We found five that" … "were all voted by close family members" (a board member)

## 143. The final count of dead-voter ballots is unsettled: 2, 4 or 5
**CORRECTED.** It is four, by the Secretary of State's sworn testimony of June 21, 2022; the "five" was a board member's interim tally while cases were open. Four in 5,000,585 hand-counted ballots is about one in 1.25 million. No list of referrals or prosecution outcomes was found.
- "We had found 2 dead people when I wrote my letter to Congress that is dated January 6th, and subsequent to that we found 2 more. That is--1, 2, 3--4 people, not 4,000, but just a total of 4, not 10,000, not 5,000."

## 145. The "5,000" figure came from the then-President's own recorded call of Jan 2, 2021
**CONFIRMED.** (the call audio, played at the congressional hearing). On air: *the claim, as made on a recorded call to the Secretary of State*.
- "So, dead people voted. And I think the--the number is close to 5,000 people."
- https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CHRG-117hhrg49352/html/CHRG-117hhrg49352.htm

## 146. A federal letter now says citizenship checks found "more than 42,000 deceased registrants" on Georgia's 2026 rolls
**UNRESOLVED.** It is an assertion in a Sept 2, 2026 letter from the Homeland Security Secretary to the Attorney General, with no published method or data, not a finding. A registrant still on a roll is not a ballot cast. It is likely to recirculate before Nov 3.
- "identified more than 42,000 deceased registrants on Georgia's state voter rolls."

## Case file: stale voter rolls — real, lawful by design, and a roll entry is not a ballot
Tray: EXPLAINED as evidence of fraud: stale entries are real and lawful by design, and a roll entry is not a ballot.

## 147. In 2024 the rolls held about 234.5 million registrations against 158.2 million ballots counted
**CONFIRMED.** Both are the Election Assistance Commission's own figures; the same table gives 244,271,230 voting-age citizens.
- "there were 234,504,358 individuals who were registered to vote in the United States as of November 5, 2024"
- "States also reported that 158,211,780 voters cast ballots that were counted for the 2024 general election."
- https://www.eac.gov/sites/default/files/2025-06/2024_EAVS_Report_508c.pdf

## 148. 23.2 million (9.9%) registrations were inactive
**CORRECTED.** (the percentage). The count and the meaning are right (inactive voters must verify their address before they can vote), but 9.9% is a division the engine did itself. The commission's published share is 10.6%, computed without the six jurisdictions that do not separate active from inactive voters. Say *about 23 million, roughly one in ten*.
- "23,184,185 were considered inactive voters (meaning they required address verification under the provisions of the NVRA before they would be permitted to vote)"
- "2024 244,271,230 234,504,358 211,144,275 86.6% 90.0% 23,184,185 10.6%"
- "six states (Guam, Idaho, Minnesota, New Hampshire, North Dakota, and Puerto Rico) did not distinguish between active and inactive voters"
- https://www.eac.gov/sites/default/files/2025-06/2024_EAVS_Report_508c.pdf

## 149. 21.3 million records were removed in 2022–24, about one in five because the voter had died
**CONFIRMED.** Including the Feb 12, 2026 errata, which revised deaths to 4,576,275 (about 4.6 million). The most common reason for removal was the federal confirmation process itself.
- "Between the close of registration for the 2022 general election and the close of registration for the 2024 general election, states reported removing 21,298,175 records from their voter lists."
- "Indiana provided updated total number of voters removed because they were deceased" (the errata)
- "The most common reason was failing to return a confirmation notice"
- https://www.eac.gov/sites/default/files/2025-06/2024_EAVS_Report_508c.pdf · errata on https://www.eac.gov/research-and-data/datasets-codebooks-and-surveys

## 150. 38.8 million confirmation notices were sent, 68.9% unreturned
**CONFIRMED.** As the errata figures. The June 2025 report had said 39,670,903 and 69.7%; the Feb 2026 errata (a Maryland correction) changed them.
- "Nationally, 38,803,086 confirmation notices were sent between the 2022 general election and the month before the 2024 general election" … "unreturned confirmation notices accounted for 68.9% of the total confirmation notices sent."
- https://www.eac.gov/research-and-data/datasets-codebooks-and-surveys (errata) · https://www.eac.gov/sites/default/files/2025-06/2024_EAVS_Report_508c.pdf

## 151. Georgia removed 466,063 records (5.7%), 146,022 for death, against 5,297,500 ballots counted
**CONFIRMED.** (Georgia was untouched by either errata).
- "Georgia 466,063 5.7% 34,769 7.5% 146,022 31.3% 174,396 37.4%"
- "Georgia 159 7,174,961 7,917,054 5,297,500 73.8% 66.9%"
- https://www.eac.gov/sites/default/files/2025-06/2024_EAVS_Report_508c.pdf

## 152. The count already rejects mail ballots from voters who died
**CONFIRMED.** (the rate). Of 584,463 rejected mail ballots in 2024, 1.5% were rejected because the voter had died and 11.1% because the voter had already voted. Multiplying those out is not the commission's figure, so the size is APPROXIMATE: say "thousands". A ballot rejected because the voter died is usually a real voter who mailed a ballot and then died: evidence the check runs, not of fraud.
- "47,629,437 were reported as being counted and 584,463 were reported as being rejected"
- "Voter was deceased 1.5%" and "Voter already cast another ballot that was accepted 11.1%"
- https://www.eac.gov/sites/default/files/2025-06/2024_EAVS_Report_508c.pdf

## 153. Federal law: a mover can't be dropped without written confirmation, or a missed notice plus two federal general elections; removal for not voting is barred; systematic removals end 90 days before a federal election
**CONFIRMED.** (52 U.S.C. § 20507).
- "shall not result in the removal of the name of any person from the official list of voters registered to vote in an election for Federal office by reason of the person’s failure to vote"
- "A State shall complete, not later than 90 days prior to the date of a primary or general election for Federal office , any program the purpose of which is to systematically remove the names of ineligible voters"
- "conduct a general program that makes a reasonable effort to remove the names of ineligible voters from the official lists of eligible voters by reason of— (A) the death of the registrant; or (B) a change in the residence of the registrant"
- https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/52/20507

## 154. ERIC matches roll, licence, death and change-of-address data, reports to its member states, and never touches the rolls; 27 states and DC are members
**CONFIRMED.** One answer on its FAQ page still says 24 states plus DC; the home page and members list are current.
- "ERIC is never connected to any state’s voter registration system. Members retain complete control over their voter rolls."
- "Deceased Report: Identifies voters who have died using voter registration data and Social Security death data known as the Limited Access Death Master File and MVD data."
- "Possible cases of illegal voting are reviewed and, if found credible, referred to law enforcement."
- https://ericstates.org/ · https://ericstates.org/?p=987

## 155. Pew (2012): about 24 million inaccurate registrations, 1.8 million deceased people listed, 2.75 million people registered in more than one state
**CONFIRMED.** As 2012 data, labelled as old. These are roll records, not ballots.

- "More than 1.8 million deceased individuals are listed as voters."
- "Approximately 2.75 million people have registrations in more than one state."

## Case file: noncitizens on the rolls — registered is not voted
Tray: PROVEN in small numbers (Georgia 20 registered in 2024; Utah 13 who had voted; Texas 117 referred). OPEN — SAVE's error rate: (a) 28,635+ flags with no published false-positive rate; (b) the resolution of every flag would settle it; (c) Homeland Security/USCIS and the states hold it.

**CONFIRMED.** (registered). - "none of those people has cast a ballot in November’s general election but that an investigation into whether they had voted in past elections was ongoing"

## 157. Georgia, Sept 1, 2026: "432 potential noncitizens who have voted at some point since 1996"
**CONFIRMED.** As stated by the Secretary of State's office: "potential", over 30 years, with registrations going back to 1948. It is not a count of proven illegal votes.
- "the Secretary of State’s Office identified 432 potential noncitizens who have voted at some point since 1996, including records identified by DOAA."
- "Registration dates for these records go back to 1948, before Georgia required proof of citizenship to register to vote, and some records have not voted since the 1990s."
- "just 0.1% of the voter registration records would have allowed an individual to vote with an unverified identity, address, or citizenship."

## 158. Utah's citizenship review (final report May 27, 2026): 27 confirmed noncitizens registered, 13 of whom had voted, out of 2,069,640 records
**CONFIRMED.** A further 25 were "likely noncitizens", 9 of whom had voted; 5,007 records were unconfirmed, mostly old ones lacking ID numbers. About 1 in 160,000 records had a confirmed noncitizen who voted (our arithmetic).
- "In total, our office reviewed 2,069,640 voter records."
- "While we found information obtained from the DLD or SAVE to be reliable in confirming whether an individual is a citizen, it was not reliable in indicating an individual was not a citizen, without considering other factors."
- https://ltgovernor.utah.gov/wp-content/uploads/CITIZENSHIP-FULL-SUMMARY-2.pdf

## 159. The federal SAVE system, run against state rolls in 2025–26, verified 65 million voters and flagged 28,635 as potential noncitizens
**CONFIRMED.** As the government's claim in its Supreme Court filing, as NPR reported it. Its error rate is UNRESOLVED: none has been published. A district judge blocked the modified system in June 2026, and the Supreme Court revived it for now on Sept 25, 2026, with three Justices dissenting. The federal fact sheet says SAVE does not decide who may vote.
- "since May 2025, the updated version of SAVE has verified more than 65 million voters across 26 states, and has flagged 28,635 as potential noncitizens"
- "the government had not yet released the system's error rate"
- "SAVE provides verification information but does not determine an individual’s eligibility to register to vote, stay on a voting roll, or vote in an election." (USCIS)
- "In at least five different states, the system has flagged people as noncitizens who were actually citizens." (Votebeat)
- https://www.npr.org/2026/09/25/nx-s1-5976804/supreme-court-trump-save-noncitizen-voting · https://www.dhs.gov/news/2026/09/25/dhs-applauds-supreme-court-decision-permitting-citizenship-verification-voters · https://www.uscis.gov/save/current-user-agencies/guidance/voter-registration-and-voter-list-maintenance-fact-sheet · https://www.uscis.gov/newsroom/news-releases/uscis-enhances-voter-verification-systems · https://www.votebeat.org/national/2026/09/25/supreme-court-ruling-save-system-trump-dhs-noncitizens-2026-election/

## 160. Texas ran 18 million registrations through SAVE: 2,724 flagged, 578 later shown to be citizens, 117 cases referred for investigation
**CONFIRMED.** (the Texas Secretary of State, Sept 15, 2026). One flag in five fell away on review, mostly because passport data reached SAVE after the first run. 117 in 18 million is about 1 in 150,000.
- "ran the names of the state’s 18 million registered voters through the federal Systematic Alien Verification for Entitlements (SAVE) database — a process that identified 2,724 potential noncitizens on the voter rolls"
- "The additional review showed that 578 voters demonstrated citizenship. Of those, citizenship was proven for 506 because passport information was added to SAVE after the state’s initial run."
- "today referred 117 cases to the Office of the Attorney General for investigation of potential noncitizen voting"
- https://www.sos.state.tx.us/about/newsreleases/2026/091526.shtml
- Correction, 2026-10-13: Texas first announced the 2,724 on Oct 20, 2025; the Sept 15, 2026 release is the follow-up (entry 263).

**CONFIRMED.** (NPR, Dec 2025). NPR documented a naturalised citizen removed after missing a 30-day notice; he is a private person and is not named.

## Case file: the Justice Department's 2025–26 suits for states' full voter rolls
Tray: EXPLAINED for reading the dismissals as *the rolls are clean*: they rule on the department's power to demand the data.

## 162. 31 suits (30 states plus DC): 26 lost, Oklahoma resolved, 4 pending (Delaware, Hawaii, Idaho, Utah)
**CONFIRMED.** (CBS, updated Oct 1, 2026, and a university tracker updated Oct 7, 2026; re-check on script day). These are rulings on the department's legal power to demand the data, not findings that rolls are clean.
- "the Justice Department has filed lawsuits against 30 states and the District of Columbia in an effort to force them to hand over the voter data."
- "The other cases remain pending. Those are in Delaware, Hawaii, Idaho and Utah."
- "Of the Justice Department’s 31 lawsuits, 26 have been dismissed on the merits by district courts."
- "To date, no court at either the district or appellate level has ruled in favor of the Justice Department."
- https://www.cbsnews.com/news/trump-lawsuits-state-voter-rolls-justice-department/ · https://statedemocracy.law.wisc.edu/our-work/tracker-doj-lawsuits-seeking-states-sensitive-voter-data · https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/justice-department-sues-six-additional-states-failure-provide-voter-registration-rolls
- Update, 2026-10-13: on Oct 9, 2026 a federal judge vacated the Justice Department's voter-roll program itself (entry 268); the state-by-state record and the states' reasons are in entries 250–277.

## 163. The Sixth Circuit (Michigan) affirmed dismissal 2–1 on June 24, 2026
**CONFIRMED.** Two grounds: the state's voter file is internally generated, not a record that "came into" the official's possession, and the demand lacked the required statement of its basis and purpose. Rehearing by the full court was denied on Aug 14, 2026.
- "The district court dismissed the suit, concluding that Title III’s narrow text cannot withstand the weight of the government’s broad request. We agree and affirm."
- "So it is an internally generated electronic database, not a record acquired from an outside source"
- "statement of both the basis and purpose of its request"
- "less than a majority of the judges voted in favor of rehearing en banc. Therefore, the petition is denied."
- https://www.opn.ca6.uscourts.gov/opinions.pdf/26a0180p-06.pdf · https://www.opn.ca6.uscourts.gov/opinions.pdf/26a0233p-06.pdf

## 164. Georgia's refiled suit was dismissed Sept 30, 2026: the judge held the 1960 Act does not authorise the demand or preempt state privacy law, and that Georgia's public roll for a fee "would be enough"
**CORRECTED.** The order rests on one ground (the 1960 Act does not preempt Georgia's voter-privacy law) and expressly declines to decide whether the demand was sufficient. The phrase "would be enough" is not in the order. The order also records that Georgia had already handed over a redacted list. And the first Georgia suit was dismissed for lack of subject-matter jurisdiction, not for wrong venue (N.D. Ga. No. 1:26-cv-00485, Doc. 146).
- "The Court finds that the first issue raised by Defendant, that the CRA does not preempt Georgia’s voter privacy law, is dispositive, particularly given that Defendant largely complied with Plaintiff’s demand for voter records. Therefore, the Court does not need to reach the other issues raised by the Parties regarding the sufficiency of Plaintiff’s demand"
- "That case was then dismissed without prejudice on January 23, 2026, for lack of subject matter jurisdiction."
- Order of the U.S. District Court for the Northern District of Georgia, Sept 30, 2026, No. 1:26-cv-00485 (Doc. 146)

## 165. Maryland: the judge "joins every court to have addressed this issue"
**CONFIRMED.** (U.S. District Court for the District of Maryland, No. SAG-25-3934, June 18, 2026).
- "Accordingly, this Court joins every court to have addressed this issue in concluding that an SVRL is not a record or paper that a state must produce to the United States under the CRA."

## Case file: North Carolina's 9th District, 2018 — real, proven, organised fraud
Tray: PROVEN: an organised absentee-ballot operation; the election was rerun on a 5–0 bipartisan vote.

## 166. The bipartisan State Board voted 5-0 for a new election; it could "not determine who would have won", and the irregularities tainted the whole race
**CONFIRMED.** With a sourcing fix: the "could not determine who would have won" wording is from the Board's March 11, 2019 question-and-answer document, not the order. The order says the number of affected votes could not be fixed and did not need to be.
- "the 2018 General Election for North Carolina’s 9th Congressional District was corrupted by fraud, improprieties, and irregularities so pervasive that its results are tainted"
- "Tampering, obstruction and disguise have obscured the precise number of votes either unlawfully counted or excluded, but substantial evidence supports our conclusion that the absentee ballot scheme and other irregularities cast doubt on the outcome"
- "It is neither required nor possible for the State Board to determine the precise number of ballots affected in circumstances such as this."
- "Because the State Board of Elections found that the election was tainted and could not determine who would have won the election if not for absentee voting and other irregularities, it did not certify a winner."
- "The vote to hold a new election was unanimous – three Democrats and two Republicans."
- https://dl.ncsbe.gov/index.html?prefix=State_Board_Meeting_Docs/Congressional_District_9_Portal/ (the Board's order of Mar 13, 2019) · https://s3.amazonaws.com/dl.ncsbe.gov/State_Board_Meeting_Docs/Congressional_District_9_Portal/Final_QA_9thCD_03112019_.pdf

## 167. The operation submitted at least 788 absentee request forms in Bladen County and at least 231 in Robeson, and paid workers $125 per 50 ballots collected
**CONFIRMED.** It also paid $150 per 50 request forms.
- "at least 788 absentee by mail request forms in Bladen County were submitted by"
- "at least 231 absentee by mail request forms in Robeson County were submitted by"
- "though an email suggests the number may have been at least 449."
- "he would pay them $150.00 per 50 absentee ballot request forms collected and $125.00 per 50 absentee ballots collected"
- https://dl.ncsbe.gov/index.html?prefix=State_Board_Meeting_Docs/Congressional_District_9_Portal/ (the order, findings 37, 41, 42)

## 168. The margin was 905 votes; mail-in absentee votes in the two counties' parts of the district totalled 1,364
**CONFIRMED.** For the 905 and the county figures; the 1,364 total is our sum of the Board's slide figures (684 + 680). Districtwide, more than 10,500 mail ballots were counted. This is the one case in the file where the pool of suspect ballots exceeded the margin.
- "led by a margin of 905 votes, or 0.3% of the total number of votes tallied."
- "The number of returned absentee by mail ballots far exceeded the margin between"
- https://dl.ncsbe.gov/index.html?prefix=State_Board_Meeting_Docs/Congressional_District_9_Portal/ (the order) · https://s3.amazonaws.com/dl.ncsbe.gov/State_Board_Meeting_Docs/Congressional_District_9_Portal/Executive%20Director%27s%20Preview%20of%20the%20Evidence.pdf

## 169. Unreturned absentee ballots: Bladen 595 of 1,323 (44.97%); Robeson 1,493 of 2,269 (65.80%)
**CONFIRMED.** (the executive director's preview of the evidence). Unreturned is not stolen.
- https://s3.amazonaws.com/dl.ncsbe.gov/State_Board_Meeting_Docs/Congressional_District_9_Portal/Executive%20Director%27s%20Preview%20of%20the%20Evidence.pdf (slide 5)

## 170. It was caught by investigators and records, not by a viral clip; the Board refused to certify on Nov 27, 2018
**CONFIRMED.** The investigation ran 142 completed voter interviews and subpoenaed financial and phone records.
- "This team opened an investigation based on reports from Bladen County. After gathering evidence, investigators presented preliminary information to the former nine-member State Board of Elections and Ethics Enforcement on November 27. As a result, the former nine-member Board unanimously declined to certify the results"
- "142 completed voter interviews (401 total attempts)"
- "Subpoenas of documents and other records including financial records and phone records"
- https://s3.amazonaws.com/dl.ncsbe.gov/State_Board_Meeting_Docs/Congressional_District_9_Portal/Final_QA_9thCD_03112019_.pdf · https://s3.amazonaws.com/dl.ncsbe.gov/State_Board_Meeting_Docs/Congressional_District_9_Portal/Executive%20Director%27s%20Preview%20of%20the%20Evidence.pdf

## 171. The prosecutions ended in misdemeanour pleas without jail; the operative died before trial; the candidate was not charged
**CONFIRMED.** (AP via NBC, WSOC and Yahoo; secondary). Four people pleaded guilty in Sept 2022, with later pleas in Nov–Dec 2022 and community service. The operative died in April 2022 before his trial; his only conviction was federal and unrelated to ballots. The candidate, who did not run in the rerun, was not charged.
- "Four people pleaded guilty on Monday to misdemeanors for their roles in absentee ballot fraud in rural North Carolina during the 2016 and 2018 elections" … "which resulted in no active prison or jail time."
- "He rejected a plea agreement and looked forward to his day in court, according to a friend. But he died in April after being diagnosed with lung cancer."
- "pleaded guilty in June 2021 to obtaining illegal Social Security benefits while concealing payments for political work he performed."
- https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/elections/four-people-plead-guilty-north-carolina-ballot-probe-2016-2018-electio-rcna49534 · https://www.wsoctv.com/news/local/5-remaining-9th-district-election-fraud-suspects-face-judge-monday/MTZTEMIMPJE7FDAZV5ZPPL3J3I/ · https://www.yahoo.com/news/nc-election-fraud-suspect-pleads-230422677.html

## Case file: Bridgeport, Connecticut, 2023 — real ballot-stuffing on camera, proven in court
Tray: PROVEN: partisans handling other people's absentee ballots on drop-box video; the primary was voided; nine people charged.

## 172. The primary's margin was 251 votes (4,212 to 3,961); the challenger won the in-person vote and the incumbent the absentee vote
**CONFIRMED.** (Superior Court decision, Nov 1, 2023, Docket FBT-CV-23-6127336-S; an OCR of a scan). In person: 2,648 to 3,100; absentee: 1,564 to 861. The absentee pool (2,425) was nearly ten times the margin.
- "The results of the primary election as reported to the state were 4,212 votes for" [the incumbent] "and 3,961 votes for" [the challenger]
- "1,564 absentee ballot votes" … "861 absentee ballot votes"
- https://civilinquiry.jud.ct.gov/DocumentInquiry/DocumentInquiry.aspx?DocumentNo=26199518

## 173. The footage: 21 days of drop-box video, 420 approaches, several people dropping multiple ballots per trip
**CONFIRMED.**
- "The Bridgeport Police produced videos of the recordings of the activity at the four Drop Boxes for a 21 -day period that absentee ballots were cast in this primary."
- "420 discreet approaches were made to the four Drop Boxes by individuals during the relevant period of video produced."
- "The video clips from the drop boxes shows several people dropping multiple ballots into the drop box during a single approach to the drop box."
- https://civilinquiry.jud.ct.gov/DocumentInquiry/DocumentInquiry.aspx?DocumentNo=26199518 (p. 21)

## 174. Two campaign figures, the town party's vice-chair and a council candidate, took the Fifth; the court found they made multiple drops of multiple ballots
**CONFIRMED.** (CT Mirror's relay of the findings; the court text is an OCR).
- "appear multiple times in the video clips that were accepted into evidence making multiple drops of multiple ballots each time into the Drop Boxes."
- https://ctmirror.org/?p=1056745

## 175. The ruling (Nov 1, 2023) ordered a new primary, with the result "in serious doubt"
**CONFIRMED.** The legal finding is unlawful handling of real voters' absentee ballots by partisans who were not allowed to handle them, which under Connecticut law voids those ballots whether or not fraud is proven. It is not a finding of fake ballots.
- "The videos are shocking to the court and should be shocking to all the parties."
- "established substantial violations of 9-140b in the placing of absentee ballots into drop boxes by partisans who were not designated to handle such ballots and that the volume of ballots so mishandled is such that it calls the result of the primary" … "election in serious doubt and leaves the court unable to determine the legitimate result of the primary."
- "The validity of the ballot, therefore, depends not on whether there has been fraud, but on" (quoting state precedent)
- https://civilinquiry.jud.ct.gov/DocumentInquiry/DocumentInquiry.aspx?DocumentNo=26199518 (pp. 36–37)

## 176. Up to 1,253 drop-box ballots against about 420 people on 2,100 hours of video
**APPROXIMATE.** This is the plaintiff's argument as reported, not a court finding; the 1,253 is not aired as fact.
- "arguing that as many as 1,253 were submitted through drop boxes, while 2,100 hours of surveillance videos of the city's four drop boxes show only about 420 people placing ballots in them."
- https://ctmirror.org/?p=1056745

## 177. In the court-ordered rerun (Jan 23, 2024), the incumbent won again
**CONFIRMED.**
- "claimed victory over challenger" … "in a court-ordered second primary today."
- https://ctmirror.org/?p=1064553

## 178. Nine people were charged over the 2023 cycle after a state elections-commission referral; two rejected plea deals in Aug 2026; no verdict yet
**CONFIRMED.** Five were charged on Feb 21, 2025 and four more on July 30, 2025; the party vice-chair faces 42 counts of unlawful ballot possession among others. They are charged, not convicted. An earlier case, over the 2019 primary, produced a guilty plea in July 2025 from a canvasser for the other faction, so absentee abuse in the city was not one camp's. A new charge over the 2026 primary was announced on Oct 6, 2026 (a Connecticut Division of Criminal Justice press release whose web address carries the defendant's name, so it is not linked here).
- "five people have been criminally charged in connection with the misuse of absentee ballots during the 2023 Bridgeport Democratic mayoral election cycle."
- "The SEEC subsequently referred the case to the Office of the Chief State's Attorney"
- "was charged with 42 counts of Possession of Ballots and Envelopes Restricted"
- "four Bridgeport residents involved in the 2023 Bridgeport Democratic mayoral primary have been criminally charged"
- "Two Democratic officials in Bridgeport rejected plea offers in court on Tuesday and chose to put their fate into the hands of a jury"
- "One of seven defendants charged in the Bridgeport mayoral election scandals pleaded guilty to voting fraud charges Wednesday but will avoid jail time under a plea agreement."
- https://portal.ct.gov/dcj/press-releases/division-of-criminal-justice/02212025bridgeportarrests · https://portal.ct.gov/dcj/press-releases/division-of-criminal-justice/07302025bridgeportarrests · https://ctmirror.org/?p=1178726 · https://ctmirror.org/?p=1130880

## 179. Bridgeport 2023 as the strongest drop-box case (as held by hand pass C)
**UNRESOLVED.** In hand pass C, which did not fetch the Bridgeport record and so could not use it. Hand pass B fetched and settled it (entries 172–178); the episode relies on those entries, not on this one.

## Case file: Paterson, New Jersey, May 2020 — a voided all-mail council election
Tray: PROVEN as an election voided for procedural violations; the criminal fraud cases collapsed, with no conviction found.

## 180. It began when postal inspectors found hundreds of ballots bundled in mailboxes
**CONFIRMED.** The county board disqualified 347 of them.
- "The investigation started after the U.S. Postal Inspection Service tipped off state law enforcement officials to hundreds of mail-in ballots stuffed in a Paterson mailbox. Other bundled ballots were discovered in another nearby city."
- "So the Passaic County Board of Elections disqualified the 347 votes"

## 181. About 19% of the city's mail ballots were rejected, and the ward winner led by about 240
**CONFIRMED.** For the 19% (3,190 ballots). The ward margin is given as 240 by one outlet and 245 by another, so the episode says "about 240". "19% rejected" is not "19% fraud": it is unclear how many were tied to the allegations.
- "The Passaic County Board of Elections rejected 3,190 ballots, about 19% of the mail-in ballots submitted, for the May 12 election in Paterson, but it is unclear how many were connected to the fraud allegations."
- "who finished the May 12 election 240 votes ahead"

**CONFIRMED.** (CNN quoting the ruling; the written opinion was not obtained).
- "rife with mail in vote procedural violations,"
- "though he left the decision on whether there was voter fraud to the criminal courts."

**CONFIRMED.**

**CONFIRMED.** One councilman's case was dismissed on Apr 13, 2026 on speedy-trial grounds and an undisclosed immunity deal (the state is appealing); the state moved to drop the other in May 2026; the two campaign workers' charges had gone away earlier. None of the charges mention the bundled ballots.

- "wants to dismiss an indictment against" … "nearly six years after"
