## 185. "The county board decided not to count 800 ballots"
**UNRESOLVED.** The page it came from (a search summary of an advocacy group's case database) was not opened; the sources opened give 347 disqualified and 3,190 rejected citywide. The 800 is kept off air.

## Case file: the scale of proven fraud nationally — real, rare, mostly one person acting alone
Tray: PROVEN: real, rare, mostly one person acting alone, and found by records. NOT CHECKABLE: fraud nobody has detected.

## 186. Heritage's database lists 1,561 "proven instances" (about 85% convictions) across decades, says it is a sample, and says "nobody really knows the extent"
**CORRECTED.** (the count is out of date); the caveats are confirmed. The live database (last updated Dec 12, 2025) returns 1,619 results, still about 85% criminal convictions; 1,561 was a November 2024 snapshot. Heritage is an interested source that argues fraud is under-detected, and its count is a floor by its own design. A hand tally of its data file (not Heritage's own figures, so not aired as exact numbers) puts absentee-ballot fraud as the largest category and in-person impersonation as the smallest.
- "A Sampling of Proven Instances of Election Fraud"
- "The problem is that nobody really knows the extent of election fraud."
- "It does not capture all cases and certainly does not capture reported instances or allegations of election fraud, some of which may be meritorious, some not, that are not investigated or prosecuted."
- "Each and every one of the cases in this database represents an instance in which a public official, usually a prosecutor, thought it serious enough to act upon."
- https://electionfraud.heritage.org/ · https://electionfraud.heritage.org/about

## 187. AP's Dec 2021 review of six disputed 2020 states found fewer than 475 potential cases against a combined 311,257-vote margin, nearly all one person acting alone
**CONFIRMED.** (AP via GPB's reprint). These are potential cases, not convictions, and many were later declined; about 1 in 54,000 of the 25.5 million ballots.
- "An Associated Press review of every potential case of voter fraud in the six battleground states disputed by" … "has found fewer than 475"
- "by a combined 311,257 votes out of 25.5 million ballots cast for president. The disputed ballots represent just 0.15% of his victory margin in those states."
- "Virtually every case was based on an individual acting alone to cast additional ballots."
- "AP reporters in five states contacted roughly 340 election offices"
- "prosecutors declined to bring charges in 26 of those cases" (Wisconsin)
- https://www.gpb.org/news/2021/12/16/far-too-little-vote-fraud-tip-election-trump-ap-finds

## 188. Brookings: Arizona's Heritage-listed fraud rate is .0000845% of ballots over 25 years, and "no election outcome in the U.S. has ever been altered by ballot fraud"
**CORRECTED.** The rate is confirmed: 36 cases in 42,626,379 ballots, under one per million. But the same commentary's line that no outcome has ever been altered is contradicted by North Carolina's 9th District (entry 166) and by Heritage's own "election overturned" entries, so the script never borrows it.
- "in Arizona, Heritage had to go back 25 years, over which there were 36 elections held and 42,626,379 ballots cast, in order to come up with 36 cases of fraud. The percentage of fraudulent votes was a minuscule .0000845%"
- https://www.brookings.edu/articles/how-widespread-is-election-fraud-in-the-united-states-not-very/

## 189. The best evidence for "fraud is under-detected": a national estimate of double voting (Goel et al., American Political Science Review, 2020)
**CONFIRMED.** And it cuts both ways. At most about 1 in 4,000 votes in 2012 were double votes, and a 1.3% clerical error rate could explain all of them; a crude purge rule would block about 300 legitimate votes for each double vote it stopped.
- "We estimate that at most only 1 in 4,000 votes cast in 2012 were double votes, with measurement error in turnout records possibly explaining a"
- "a 1.3% clerical error rate would be sufficient to explain all of these apparent double votes"
- "could impede approximately 300 legitimate votes for each double vote prevented"
- https://doi.org/10.1017/S000305541900087X

## 190. Wisconsin's audit bureau (2021): 4 people may have voted twice by absentee ballot in 2020
**CONFIRMED.** The kind of number the audits turn up: single digits, referred for follow-up. The commission's rebuttal (that most apparent pairs were typos) was not obtained and is not aired.
- "We analyzed absentee ballot data provided by WEC’s staff and found that these data indicated 4 of the 24 individuals may have voted twice by absentee ballot during the November 2020 General Election."
- https://legis.wisconsin.gov/lab/media/rz1nj2dh/21-19full.pdf

## 191. A 2017 Brennan Center critique of Heritage's database (10 in-person impersonation cases, and so on)
**UNRESOLVED.** In this pass: the document was not obtained. The direction is supported by entry 186 (impersonation is Heritage's smallest category), but Brennan's numbers are not cited on air.
- The same 2017 report's survey of local officials (about 30 suspected noncitizen votes referred out of 23.5 million) was obtained in a top-up (entry 366); its Heritage critique figures remain unread.

## Case file: the 2020 close-out record — what the people in charge said, and what the courts decided
Tray: EXPLAINED for claims of outcome-changing fraud in 2020.

## 192. The Attorney General at the time, Dec 1, 2020: no fraud on a scale that could have changed the outcome
**CONFIRMED.** (AP, via a syndicated reprint; the AP's own site refused the fetch). The line is narrower than "no fraud".
- "To date, we have not seen fraud on a scale that could have affected a different outcome in the election"
- "U.S. attorneys and FBI agents have been working to follow up specific complaints and information they’ve received, but they’ve uncovered no evidence that would change the outcome of the election."
- https://cfjctoday.com/2020/12/01/barr-no-evidence-of-fraud-thatd-change-election-outcome/

## 193. The election infrastructure councils convened by CISA, Nov 12, 2020: "the most secure in American history"
**CONFIRMED.** The statement came from the government and private-sector councils of election officials and vendors, not from CISA alone, and it is about voting systems, not a finding that no individual committed fraud.
- "The November 3rd election was the most secure in American history."
- "There is no evidence that any voting system deleted or lost votes, changed votes, or was in any way compromised."
- https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/news/joint-statement-elections-infrastructure-government-coordinating-council-election

## 194. The Supreme Court, Dec 11, 2020 (Texas v. Pennsylvania): denied for lack of standing
**CONFIRMED.** It was not a ruling on the fraud claims. Two Justices would have let the complaint be filed but would not have granted other relief.
- "The State of Texas’s motion for leave to file a bill of complaint is denied for lack of standing under Article III of the Constitution."
- "Texas has not demonstrated a judicially cognizable interest in the manner in which another State conducts its elections."
- "I would therefore grant the motion to file the bill of complaint but would not grant other relief, and I express no view on any other issue."
- https://www.supremecourt.gov/orders/courtorders/121120zr_p860.pdf

## 195. A counted tally of the post-election lawsuits: 64 cases with 187 counts in six states, one minor win ("Lost, Not Stolen", July 2022)
**CONFIRMED.** Of the 64, 20 were dismissed before a merits hearing, 14 were voluntarily dropped and 30 had a merits hearing. The authors are a group of conservative lawyers and former officials and judges.
- "filed 64 cases containing 187 counts in the six key battleground states"
- "For this Report, we examined every count of every case brought in these six battleground states. We include both a narrative for each state and an accompanying Addendum listing each case and its disposition."
- "twenty were dismissed before a hearing on the merits"
- "and 30 cases included a hearing on the merits."
- "Only in one Pennsylvania case involving far too few votes to overturn the results"
- https://lostnotstolen.org/

## Case file: what lawful ballot handling looks like on camera
Tray: None: a reference standard for judging clips, not a claim.

## 196. Georgia's 2020 drop-box rule: video of every box, two-person sworn collection, a signed transfer form, video kept 30 days
**CONFIRMED.** As the rule in force on that date.
- "use motion detection that records one frame, or more, per minute"
- "Collection of ballots from a drop box must be made by a team of at least two people."
- "which shall include the date, time, location and number of ballots."
- "Video recordings of the drop box locations must be retained by the county registrars for 30 days after the final certification of the election"
- https://saportareport.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/11/Table-of-Contents-for-SEB-Rule-183-1-14-0.8-.14.pdf

## 197. Georgia's 2020 absentee-processing rule: scanned ballots go in sealed, labelled containers, in batches of at most 100
**APPROXIMATE.** The copy read is an advocacy group's reproduction of the rule. And this part covers scanned ballots, while the State Farm Arena carriers held opened, unscanned ballots sealed for the night, which the state's report calls a Fulton process.
- "A security seal shall be placed on the container."
- "Absentee ballots shall be processed in batches of not more than 100."
- "Fulton County Board of Elections and Registration put specific processes in place to store ballot boxes underneath tables" (the state's report of investigation)

## 198. Pennsylvania's guidance: a locked box with a numbered tamper-evident seal, video kept a year, two collectors (one a board employee), and a chain-of-custody form that travels with the ballots
**CONFIRMED.** And it is guidance ("should"), not statute.
- "All drop-boxes should be secured by a lock and sealed with a tamper-evident seal that has a unique identifying number."
- "Video surveillance should be retained securely by the county election office for one year following the deadline to certify the election"
- "The county board of elections should designate at least two election officials to collect completed ballots from a Ballot Return Site. At least one official should be a Board employee; the other may be a member of law enforcement or other peacekeeping officer."
- https://www.pa.gov/content/dam/copapwp-pagov/en/dos/resources/voting-and-elections/directives-and-guidance/2025/guidance_absentee-mail%20ballot%20person%20return%20guidance_092225.pdf

## 199. A Pennsylvania appeals court (Aug 6, 2025, non-precedential) upheld release of county election-building footage with faces blurred
**CONFIRMED.** (in outline). A viewer can request footage, but it is slow and county by county.
- "he conceded that the footage could blur or omit people and their faces"
- https://www.pacourts.us/assets/opinions/Commonwealth/out/1185CD23_8-6-25.pdf?cb=1

## Case file: how a viewer can check a clip — the method, and the research on whether people can use it
Tray: None: research on method, not a claim.

## 200. Fact-checkers ask four questions (is it original, who posted it, where, when) and reverse-search key frames, because no free tool searches whole clips; an upload date is not a film date (attributed to Bellingcat, 2017)
**CORRECTED.** (attribution). Bellingcat's guide supports the frame reverse-search, the no-whole-clip-search point, the date point and the mirroring trick, but the four-question list is not in it.
- "there are no freely available tools that allow you to reverse search an entire video clip the same way we can with image files, but we can do the next best thing by reverse image searching thumbnails and screenshots"
- "there are no indications when the video was filmed, meaning that it could have been from yesterday or five years ago"
- "Often, people use simple tricks to avoid detection from reverse image searches – mirroring a video, changing the color scheme to black and white, zooming in or out, and so on."
- https://www.bellingcat.com/resources/how-tos/2017/06/30/advanced-guide-verifying-video-content/

## 201. The professional checklist is First Draft's five pillars: provenance, source, date, location, motivation
**UNRESOLVED.** As a citation: the guide was not obtained (the organisation closed in 2022). The episode's checklist cites what was read instead: Bellingcat, InVID, and AFP's stated standard.
- "We require at least two independent sources of information to verify the central claim of a fact-check." (AFP)
- https://factcheck.afp.com/how-we-work

## 202. InVID/WeVerify splits a video into keyframes and reverse-searches them
**CONFIRMED.** (the tool's documentation; a free browser plugin used by newsrooms).
- "in order to segment it in keyframes which then can be searched with a right click on Google, Yandex, Tineye and Baidu images"
- https://www.invid-project.eu/tools-and-services/invid-verification-plugin/

## 203. Professional fact-checkers read laterally (Wineburg and McGrew, Teachers College Record, 2019)
**CONFIRMED.** (a preprint copy). A small study of 10 fact-checkers, 10 historians and 25 undergraduates: it shows that experts do it, not that lay people can learn it.
- "We sampled 45 experienced users of the Internet: 10 Ph.D. historians, 10 professional fact checkers, and 25 Stanford University undergraduates."
- "In contrast, fact checkers read laterally, leaving a site after a quick scan and opening up new browser tabs in order to judge the credibility of the original site."
- "Compared to the other groups, fact checkers arrived at more warranted conclusions in a fraction of the time."
- https://doi.org/10.1177/016146811912101102

## 204. High-school students taught lateral reading in six lessons (Wineburg et al., Journal of Educational Psychology, 2022)
**CONFIRMED.** (design and direction): a field study in one district, 271 treatment students against 228 controls. The size is APPROXIMATE: the paper's own effect size was not obtained (paywalled), and Stanford's press release says "twice as likely", from a low base. Say *about doubled, from a low base*.
- "students in treatment classrooms (n = 271) were compared to peers (n = 228) in regular classrooms."
- "were twice as likely to spot questionable websites as they were before the instruction took place"
- "Even then, however, the students earned only half the total number of possible points."
- https://mediawell.ssrc.org/citations/lateral-reading-on-the-open-internet/ · https://ed.stanford.edu/news/it-doesn-t-take-long-learn-how-spot-misinformation-online-stanford-study-finds

## 205. College students in a four-hour online module (Breakstone et al., HKS Misinformation Review, 2021)
**CONFIRMED.** A before-and-after design with no control group (n = 87).
- "At pretest, only 3 of 87 students engaged in lateral reading by leaving the original site and consulting at least one other source. At posttest, 67 of 87 did so."
- "Thirty-six students correctly raised questions about the website’s credibility"
- https://misinforeview.hks.harvard.edu/article/lateral-reading-college-students-learn-to-critically-evaluate-internet-sources-in-an-online-course/

## 206. A pop-up reminding adults to read laterally (Panizza et al., Scientific Reports, 2022, 5,387 people)
**CONFIRMED.** The abstract reads more positively than the overall result: the pop-up raised use of fact-checking strategies, but overall accuracy did not move (78.1% against 78.2% for the control group).
- "both monetary incentives and pop-up increased the use of fact-checking strategies."
- "In the control condition, 78.2% of participants correctly guessed the scientific validity of the post (incentive condition: 80.1%; pop-up condition: 78.1%)."
- https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8981191/

## 207. Prebunking videos run as YouTube ads (Roozenbeek et al., Science Advances, 2022)
**CONFIRMED.** With the size stated exactly: in the field study, recognition of a manipulation technique rose about 5% (h = 0.09), narrowly below the authors' own preregistered threshold, measured once within 24 hours. The outcome is recognising a technique in a headline, not spotting a fake video.
- "We collected a total of 22,632 responses"
- "we narrowly failed to reach our preregistered smallest effect size of interest (SESOI) of h = 0.10, with an observed effect size of h = 0.09 across all six items."
- "boosting manipulation technique recognition by about 5% on average"
- https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9401631/

## 208. Inoculation has a published critique and a counter-analysis
**CONFIRMED.** That both exist; the question is still open. A 2023 reanalysis found the gamified versions made people more sceptical of all news; a 2025 meta-analysis, co-authored by two of the original inoculation researchers, found both games and videos improve discrimination without that bias.
- "Bad News and Go Viral! did not improve discrimination, but rather elicited more “false” responses to all news items (more conservative responding)."
- "Results show that both gamified and video-based interventions consistently improve discrimination between reliable and unreliable news, without increasing response bias"
- https://doi.org/10.1037/xge0001395 · https://research-information.bris.ac.uk/en/publications/a-signal-detection-theory-meta-analysis-of-psychological-inoculat/

## 209. Short "tips to spot false news" (Guess et al., PNAS, 2020)
**CONFIRMED.** Discernment rose 26.5% in a US sample and 17.5% in a highly educated Indian sample; weeks later the US effect had shrunk by more than half.
- "the intervention improved discernment between mainstream and false news headlines among both a nationally representative sample in the United States (by 26.5%) and a highly educated online sample in India (by 17.5%)"
- "While the effect is still present weeks later, its magnitude attenuates by more than half relative to wave 1"
- https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7355018/

## 210. Do corrections of election-fraud claims work?
**CONFIRMED.** The results are mixed. Fact-check tweets did not undo the drop in confidence from fraud claims (2018 experiment); a myth-debunking article after 2020 reduced false beliefs; in 2022 prebunking with substantive security information beat corrections from credible sources; corrections work best on the specific claim they target.
- "Worryingly, exposure to fact-checks that show these claims to be unfounded does not measurably reduce the damage from these accusations."
- "The treatment also increased confidence in the 2020 election results."
- "Correction interventions are most effective at reducing belief in the specific election misinformation targeted by the correction."
- "Correction interventions are less effective at reducing belief in broad false claims about elections or in bolstering general beliefs in electoral integrity."
- https://doi.org/10.1017/xps.2021.18 · https://electionlab.mit.edu/sites/default/files/2023-01/carey-fogarty-nyhan-reifler.pdf · https://electionlab.mit.edu/sites/default/files/2024-05/11_fraud-corrections-technical-report.pdf · https://www.democratic-erosion.org/briefs/ (Democratic Erosion evidence brief, Oct 2024)

## 211. The federal "Rumor vs. Reality" page is gone
**CONFIRMED.** Fetched on 2026-10-12, the old address returns "not found", and its topic path redirects to the general election-security page, which has no rumour list.
- https://www.cisa.gov/rumor-vs-reality (returns "not found") · https://www.cisa.gov/topics/election-security/rumor-vs-reality (redirects to https://www.cisa.gov/topics/election-security)

## 212. What election offices tell voters to do: go to their own state and county offices
**CONFIRMED.** The secretaries of state's association sends voters to election officials' own sites and to CanIVote.org, and lists state rumour pages. Kentucky's page shows rumours come from every side: its 2020 example was a viral voter-suppression claim against officials. The federal tip sheet for officials has one voter-facing line and no reverse-search method.
- "#TrustedInfo2026 is NASS's public education effort to promote election officials as the trusted sources of election information."
- "Can I Vote does not capture any information, but instead links directly to state election websites"
- "dishonest and irresponsible allegations that Kentucky election officials were engaged in"
- "Encourage voters to verify the sources of articles, papers, and other resources before sharing them."
- https://www.nass.org/initiatives/trustedinfo · https://www.nass.org/can-I-vote · https://sos.ky.gov/elections/Pages/Rumor-Control.aspx · https://www.dni.gov/files/FMIC/documents/products/Securing-Election-Infrastructure-Against-The-Tactics-Of-Foreign-Malign-Influence-Operations-Apr2024.pdf


## § 4. Top-up hand passes (2026-10-13, after the host's pitch questions)

After the pitch, the host asked five questions the first research did not answer in full: whether proven fraud gets buried and whether fraud stories "disappear"; why states refuse to give the federal government their voter rolls; where the data are behind "statistically impossible" counts (overnight batches, more than 100% turnout, one-sided batches, Detroit's counting boards); what happened with Pennsylvania's undated mail ballots; and how much noncitizen and other ineligible voting there is. Five more hand passes answered them on 2026-10-13, each tested in both directions, from the primary record wherever it could be fetched. Every quotation relied on below was again checked mechanically against the saved source text. Where a top-up corrects an earlier entry, the earlier entry now points here, and the change is in the Corrections table.

## Case file: proven fraud since 2024 — the case sweep
Tray: PROVEN: real cases with their scale, three local elections voided or rerun. OPEN — Maricopa's 2026 noncitizen charges: (a) AP says charges were filed and Votebeat that none were announced; (b) the charging documents would settle it; (c) the Maricopa County Attorney holds them.

There is no national register of election-crime cases. This sweep used the Heritage Foundation's data file, the Justice Department's national releases from Sept 4 to Oct 12, 2026, and state and county prosecutors' releases found by search in thirteen states. It is a wide sample, not a census, and a search miss is a fact about the search.

## 213. The Justice Department charged at least 57 people with election crimes in six national releases between Sept 4 and Oct 12, 2026, mostly for noncitizen voting
**CONFIRMED.** (our count: 5 + 16 + 4 + 10 + 6 new + 16, the Minnesota ten counted once). Charged, not convicted. A newspaper's claim of "nearly 50" cases in September against 13 in the whole previous administration was seen only in a search summary (the page refused the fetch) and is UNRESOLVED, not aired.
- "Today, the Justice Department announced charges against 16 individuals in connection with illegal voting, illegal voter registration, and related election-fraud schemes across the country."
- "a federal grand jury indicted ten defendants separately on September 24, 2026, for voter fraud"
- "Today the Justice Department announced charges involving 16 individuals, including an illegal alien, in connection with illegal voting"
- "A complaint, indictment, and information merely contain accusations."
- https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/department-justice-charges-16-individuals-illegal-voting-and-related-election-crimes · https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/department-justice-indicts-ten-aliens-voter-fraud-minnesota · https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/justice-department-announces-charges-involving-16-individuals-election-crimes · https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/five-charged-pennsylvania-new-jersey-and-wisconsin-illegally-voting-fraudulent-registration · https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/four-aliens-charged-election-fraud · https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/justice-department-announces-charges-involving-16-aliens-election-crimes

**CONFIRMED.** (Votebeat).

- https://www.votebeat.org/national/2026/09/21/16-charged-noncitizen-voting-michigan-wisconsin-texas-georgia/

- "Of those, she said 52 were citizens, and eight were not."
- "[The county attorney] said she found no evidence that those voters were attempting to sway a specific election."
- "Though our findings today would not change the outcome of any past election, every ineligible voter dilutes the vote of a citizen"
- "The remaining 147 voters are still being investigated by her office and the Arizona Attorney General’s Office."
- https://www.votebeat.org/arizona/2026/09/28/maricopa-county-suspected-noncitizen-voters-investigation-results-save-heap-mitchell/ · https://www.kjzz.org/politics/2026-09-28/maricopa-county-attorney-mitchell-finds-8-noncitizens-voted-in-past-elections

- https://www.mysuncoast.com/2026/09/24/florida-prosecutors-hand-down-20-indictments-voting-fraud/

**CONFIRMED.** The three counted ballots could not be pulled back: about 1 in 31,500 of the county's ballots.

- "still lost the general election by 27"

- "not a conspiracy or organized effort to tip any election for any one candidate or party"

## Case file: the peg, updated to 2026-10-13
Tray: OPEN — whether Fulton's repeated scans and missing images were accident or intent: (a) the state found the sloppiness but could not say whether any repeated image was tabulated, or why; (b) the FBI's forensic findings on the 653 seized boxes, or any charging document, would settle it; (c) the FBI and the Justice Department (N.D. Ga.) hold them. Even if every repeated image had been counted, the hand-pass arithmetic puts it at a few hundred net votes against a statewide margin of about 12,000. OPEN — Homeland Security's 42,776 *deceased* on Georgia's rolls: (a) the method is unpublished and no count of ballots cast in those names exists; (b) the match method and list, and the state's own check, would settle it; (c) Homeland Security and Georgia's Secretary of State hold them. It is about rolls, not ballots.

## 231. No one has been charged in the FBI's Fulton County investigation as of Oct 13, 2026
**CONFIRMED.** To the latest dated filing: the Justice Department's Oct 5, 2026 appeals brief, which still argues over a subpoena and says it need not predict what charges, if any, might follow.
- "eventual criminal charges might result from it , if any"
- U.S. Court of Appeals for the Eleventh Circuit, No. 26-12769, the government's opening brief, Oct 5, 2026 · https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/73735904/in-re-grand-jury-subpoena/

**CONFIRMED.** The subpoena (April 2026) sought names, home addresses, phone numbers and emails of every 2020 count worker, volunteers included. The judge's view on the statute of limitations is a ruling on a subpoena, contested on appeal, and not a finding about fraud; it also sits uneasily with the May 2026 order's pointer to an image-modification date inside the period (entry 47). The script says a second judge quashed a grand-jury subpoena and the government has appealed, and does not resolve the limitations question.
- "is staggering"
- "the statute of limitations for any possible crime arising from the 2020 Election has long expired"
- "CIVIL APPEAL DOCKETED. Notice of appeal filed by Appellant USA on 08/06/2026."
- https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/73398742/in-re-grand-jury-subpoena/ (the district-court order of July 7, 2026, Doc. 37) · https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/73735904/in-re-grand-jury-subpoena/ · https://www.pbs.org/newshour/politics/judge-rejects-justice-departments-unreasonable-attempt-to-get-names-of-2020-election-workers-in-fulton-county

## Case file: do fraud stories "disappear"? Coverage, retractions and walk-backs
Tray: EXPLAINED for *proven fraud gets suppressed*: every vanished story found was false, though follow-through on convictions is under-covered. OPEN — the Texas Attorney General's removed prosecution tally: (a) removed in 2023 with no stated reason; (b) the office's records on why would settle it; (c) the Texas Attorney General holds them. It counts prosecutions, not votes.

Twelve of the largest or most relevant cases were checked against wire services, national outlets on the left and right, local outlets and the nonpartisan election press. The search tool could not reach Reuters, the New York Times or the New York Post, so those were effectively untested; "not found" means our searches did not surface it.

## 236. The Attorney General said federal election-crime charges totalled 90, 50 of them for noncitizen voting, and complained the media did not take them seriously (Sept 30, 2026)
**CONFIRMED.** As his statement, via AP. It fits the narrower count of 57 people named in national releases (entry 213); the 90 includes cases not in those releases.
- "He said those added up to 90 for various types of election misconduct, including 50 against people who are alleged to have voted as noncitizens"
- "lamenting that the media did not take seriously the charges for voter fraud his office has brought"
- https://www.wsls.com/news/politics/2026/10/01/trumps-campaign-against-noncitizen-voting-demonstrates-its-rare-and-not-enough-to-swing-elections/

## 237. Were Maricopa's eight charged?
**UNRESOLVED.** AP (Oct 1) wrote that the figure was disclosed when county prosecutors filed charges; Votebeat (Sept 28) and local outlets say no charges were announced. Use *no charges announced as of Sept 28* and re-check before air.
- "disclosed by county prosecutors when they filed charges this week, was eight"
- "[The county attorney] did not announce charges on Monday, but wouldn’t rule out doing so."
- https://www.wsls.com/news/politics/2026/10/01/trumps-campaign-against-noncitizen-voting-demonstrates-its-rare-and-not-enough-to-swing-elections/ · https://www.votebeat.org/arizona/2026/09/28/maricopa-county-suspected-noncitizen-voters-investigation-results-save-heap-mitchell/

## 238. About 70 articles accusing the two Fulton County election workers were taken down after a defamation settlement
**CONFIRMED.** One article checked is gone from the live site and intact in the Internet Archive. The claim was false, and the removal came through a legal settlement.
- "Nearly 70 articles cited as defamatory in the lawsuit were no longer available Friday on The Gateway Pundit website, The Associated Press found."
- https://www.gpb.org/news/2024/10/12/georgia-election-workers-settle-defamation-lawsuit-against-conservative-website · https://www.wabe.org/georgia-election-workers-settle-defamation-lawsuit-against-conservative-website/

## 239. Retractions of 2020 machine-fraud claims came through legal settlements, and were quieter than the claims
**CONFIRMED.**
- "Newsmax subsequently found no evidence that such allegations were true"
- "no apology on the air"
- "It was wrong for us to publish these false statements."
- https://www.npr.org/2021/04/30/992534968/newsmax-issues-retraction-and-apology-to-dominion-employee-over-election-stories · https://www.tpr.org/2023-04-19/fox-news-settles-defamation-lawsuit-with-election-tech-firm-dominion-voting-systems · https://www.coloradopolitics.com/2020-election/american-thinker-joins-conservative-outlets-walking-back-voter-fraud-claims/article_29bcd852-5782-11eb-96fe-0f94e3dae8d0.html · https://www.mediaite.com/media/tv/lou-dobbs-airs-stunning-fact-check-of-his-own-election-conspiracies-after-company-threatens-legal-action/

## 240. A 2020 federal prosecutor's release on discarded military ballots in Luzerne County, Pennsylvania overstated the ballots and was corrected the same day; the decision not to charge was kept from the public until after the election
**CONFIRMED.** (the Justice Department Inspector General's report 24-082).
- "retracted [the U.S. Attorney]’s original press release and issued a corrected statement"
- "although it failed to inform the public of that fact until well after the election"
- U.S. Department of Justice, Office of the Inspector General, report 24-082 (2024) · https://www.snopes.com/news/2020/09/25/pennsylvania-military-ballots/

## 241. The Texas Attorney General's page dropped its "155 individuals" prosecution tally between Apr 20 and Apr 30, 2023
**CONFIRMED.** (archived snapshots and the live page). The reason is UNRESOLVED: none was stated. Six weeks earlier, the state's top criminal court had ruled that the office cannot prosecute election crimes on its own, and many of its cases were being dismissed; that is the likely cause, but it is not documented.
- "Successfully prosecuted election fraud offenses against 155 individuals" (the Apr 20, 2023 snapshot; the phrase is absent from the Apr 30 snapshot and the live page)
- "investigate and prosecute, when requested, allegations of voter fraud"
- "dismissed or are awaiting dismissal due to the recent court finding"
- https://www.texasattorneygeneral.gov/initiatives/election-integrity (read live and via the Internet Archive) · https://www.texastribune.org/2023/03/15/ken-paxton-texas-election-crimes/

## 242. Big "potential noncitizen" numbers shrank on checking (Texas 2019, Alabama 2024)
**CONFIRMED.**
- "overstated the number of flagged voters by about 25,000 names"
- "resulted in about 2,000 citizens being deactivated"
- https://sanantonioreport.org/texas-will-end-its-botched-voter-citizenship-review-and-rescind-its-list-of-flagged-voters/ · https://www.denvergazette.com/2024/10/16/judge-orders-alabama-to-stop-purging-possible-noncitizens-from-voter-rolls/

## 243. Florida's 2022 voter-fraud arrests were mixed, not a clean collapse
**CONFIRMED.** As stated; the final tally is UNRESOLVED.
- "at least three other cases so far have been dismissed on procedural grounds"
- "overturned rulings that dismissed charges against a pair of defendants"
- https://www.wlrn.org/state/2022-12-21/floridas-effort-to-charge-20-people-with-voter-fraud-has-hit-some-roadblocks · https://www.wgcu.org/elections/2024-07-17/two-south-florida-appeals-courts-revive-2022-voter-fraud-cases

## 244. YouTube removed claims of widespread 2020 fraud, then stopped (June 2023)
**CONFIRMED.** This is real, systematic removal, but of claims, not of proven cases, and probably the strongest real basis for the feeling that fraud content "disappears".
- "will stop removing content that falsely claims the 2020 election"
- https://www.news4jax.com/tech/2023/06/02/youtube-changes-policy-to-allow-false-claims-about-past-us-presidential-elections/

## 245. A true, proven fraud story was taken down
**UNRESOLVED.** (none found). Searched: retractions and corrections of conviction stories 2022–24, the Texas Attorney General's page history, and suppression claims around a 2020 undercover video. The only removals found were of false claims, plus the unexplained Texas tally (entry 241).

## 246. Newspaper attention to voter fraud does not track the number of cases
**CONFIRMED.** (Fogarty et al., 2015). Coverage tracked politics, not the number of cases, which cuts both ways.
- "No evidence that newspaper attention is related to the rate of actual voter fraud cases in each state was found."
- http://eprints.gla.ac.uk/106488/

## 247. Fact-checks are a sliver of the conversation and arrive days late (2022 midterms, on X)
**CONFIRMED.**
- "fact-checks make up less than 1.2% of narrative conversations"
- "the median fact-check was released a full four days after the initial appearance of a narrative"
- https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.13280

## 248. The Pennsylvania registration-form case was not buried: national coverage, charges, a guilty plea
**CONFIRMED.**
- "were not motivated by efforts to sway any election or voter rolls for any specific party or candidate"
- https://www.attorneygeneral.gov/taking-action/state-director-pleads-guilty-to-offering-financial-incentives-to-street-canvassers-during-voter-registration-drives-in-3-central-pa-counties/ · https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/2024-election/fraudulent-voter-registration-lancaster-county-pennsylvania-rcna177322

## 249. The coverage table: which outlets carried which of twelve cases
**APPROXIMATE.** As a whole. Each "found" rests on a search hit (fetched or search-only, as marked in the hit list), each "not found" is a search miss, and Reuters, the New York Times and the New York Post were not testable. The pattern: all twelve had local coverage and nine reached AP at some stage; the outcome of a case (a plea, a sentence) got less national attention than the arrest; the gap ran in both political directions; and the nonpartisan election press tracked outcomes most consistently. Don't air *no national outlet covered X*; air *we found it only in local news*.
- https://www.live5news.com/2024/11/07/postal-carrier-her-friend-forged-stolen-ballots-test-voting-security-officials-say/ · https://www.votebeat.org/pennsylvania/2025/06/18/millbourne-2021-mayoral-race-election-fraud-sentencing/ · https://www.foxnews.com/us/two-men-convicted-pennsylvania-mayoral-race-election-fraud-case-hit-harsher-sentences-than-expected · https://dailycaller.com/2025/05/08/judge-indicted-voter-fraud-texas/ · https://www.democracydocket.com/analysis/texas-targets-latino-officials-voter-suppression/ · https://www.cnn.com/2024/10/29/politics/trump-voter-fraud-claims-pennsylvania · https://www.votebeat.org/arizona/2026/09/28/maricopa-county-suspected-noncitizen-voters-investigation-results-save-heap-mitchell/ · https://www.cbsnews.com/miami/news/desantis-florida-voting-fraud-arrests-indictments/ · https://www.washingtontimes.com/news/2026/oct/1/doj-launches-tidal-wave-prosecutions-noncitizens-who-cast-illegal/

## Case file: the states that "refuse to give the government their voter rolls"
Tray: EXPLAINED for *states are hiding their rolls*: the rolls are public, and three identifiers are withheld under state law. PROVEN: New Jersey's motor-vehicle registration error, and flag lists that proved wrong on checking. OPEN — SAVE's error rate: (a) 28,635+ flags with no published false-positive rate; (b) the resolution of every flag would settle it; (c) Homeland Security/USCIS and the states hold it. OPEN — Homeland Security's 42,776 *deceased* on Georgia's rolls: (a) the method is unpublished and no count of ballots cast in those names exists; (b) the match method and list, and the state's own check, would settle it; (c) Homeland Security and Georgia's Secretary of State hold them. It is about rolls, not ballots.

## 250. What the Justice Department demanded: the full list, with date of birth plus a driver's licence number or the last four digits of the Social Security number
**CONFIRMED.** (the demand letters themselves, signed by the Assistant Attorney General for Civil Rights). At least 48 states and DC were asked; North Dakota has no voter registration, so it was not.
- "your state’s VRL must include the registrant’s full name, date of birth, residential address, his or her state driver’s license number or the last four digits of the registrant’s social security number as required under the Help America Vote Act" (Oregon, Aug 14, 2025)
- "within seven days or by August 25, 2025"
- "At least 48 states and Washington, DC, have received requests for their complete voter registration lists." (Brennan Center tracker)
- https://www.brennancenter.org/media/14508/download/oregon_08.14.2025_doj-letter.pdf · https://www.democracydocket.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/new-hampshire_08.18.25_doj-letter.pdf · https://www.brennancenter.org/our-work/research-reports/tracker-justice-department-requests-voter-information

## 251. The statutes cited and the stated purpose: checking the state's list maintenance under federal voter-registration law
**CONFIRMED.** The letters cite three federal laws (the National Voter Registration Act, the Help America Vote Act and the Civil Rights Act of 1960) and answer privacy objections in advance.
- "The purpose of the request is to ascertain Oregon’s compliance with the list maintenance requirements of the NVRA and HAVA."
- "Any statewide prohibitions are clearly preempted by federal law."
- https://www.brennancenter.org/media/14508/download/oregon_08.14.2025_doj-letter.pdf

## 252. The purpose given privately, and later in court, was wider: run the rolls against the federal SAVE citizenship check, looking for noncitizens and dead voters
**CONFIRMED.** This gap is at the centre of the refusals.
- "we are requesting a copy of Montana's statewide voter registration list, so that we can facilitate a review for noncitizens and dead voters via DHS." (an email from the Voting Section to Montana, July 14, 2025)
- "being screened for ineligible voter entries." (a department statement, Sept 2025)
- "Based on recent verbal communications with your office, the alleged purpose of the DOJ's request is to run West Virginia's entire voter list through the Systematic Alien Verification for Entitlements" (West Virginia, Feb 11, 2026)
- The federal court's record, Oct 9, 2026, describes the department agreeing to give Homeland Security Investigations the states' voter files so it could "conduct appropriate investigation of potential violations of federal election law"
- https://www.brennancenter.org/media/15111/download/montana_07.14.2025_crd-request.pdf · https://stateline.org/2025/09/12/doj-is-sharing-state-voter-roll-lists-with-homeland-security/ · https://www.brennancenter.org/media/15226/download/west-virginia_02.11.2026_state-refusal.pdf · https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.dcd.291580/gov.uscourts.dcd.291580.51.0_1.pdf

## 253. The data-sharing agreement states were asked to sign binds them to "clean" their rolls within 45 days of the department's notice, and lets contractors see the data
**CONFIRMED.** (the executed Texas agreement, Dec 9, 2025). Some complying states pushed back on these terms: Iowa handed over its file but would not sign.
- "You agree therefore that within forty-five (45) days of receiving that notice from the Justice Department of any issues, insufficiencies, inadequacies, deficiencies, anomalies, or concerns, your state will clean its VRL/Data by removing ineligible voters and resubmit the updated VRL/Data"
- "a contractor with the Department of Justice who needs access to the VRL/Data information in order to perform duties related to the Department's list maintenance verification procedures."
- "there are potential conflicts between the terms of the MOU and the National Voter Registration Act of 1993" (Iowa)
- https://www.brennancenter.org/media/15082/download/texas_12.09.2025_executed-mou.pdf · https://www.brennancenter.org/media/15746/download/iowa_03.05.2026_state-resp_0.pdf · https://www.brennancenter.org/media/15219/download/mississippi_12.09.2025_letter-to-harmeet-dhillon-3.0.pdf

## 254. "A national voter database"
**UNRESOLVED.** As to intent. The department told a court no national database was being created; a federal judge in Maine (May 21, 2026) wrote that this assurance was "almost immediately undermined" by an executive order directing Homeland Security to compile a "State Citizenship List". On air: the department said it wasn't building a national list; a later executive order told Homeland Security to compile a State Citizenship List. Don't say a national database exists.
- "there is no national database that’s being created."
- U.S. District Court for the District of Maine, order dismissing the department's suit, May 21, 2026

## 255. How many states gave the full file: about 16 to 18
**APPROXIMATE.** The Brennan Center counts at least 16; the Oct 9 federal ruling says at least 18 "fully cooperated" without listing them. Say *about 16 to 18*.
- "At least 16 states have either provided or said they will provide their full statewide voter registration lists, including driver’s license and Social Security numbers"
- "At least 18 states fully cooperated"
- https://www.brennancenter.org/our-work/research-reports/tracker-justice-department-requests-voter-information · https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.dcd.291580/gov.uscourts.dcd.291580.51.0_1.pdf

## 256. The state-by-state record: about 16 states gave the full file; 3 gave only the public file and were not sued; 30 states and DC were sued, 26 dismissed, Oklahoma settled, 4 pending
**CONFIRMED.** (status per the trackers, current to Oct 8–13, 2026). Full file: Wyoming, Indiana, Alaska, Texas, Mississippi, Alabama, Arkansas, Louisiana, Tennessee, South Dakota, Florida, Nebraska, Ohio, Iowa, Oklahoma (after a settlement) and South Carolina. Public file only, not sued: Kansas (disputed: the department told a court Kansas gave the full list; Kansas says only the public version), Missouri and Montana. Sued and dismissed: 26, including Georgia, which sent its complete list without the protected fields. Pending: Delaware, Hawaii, Idaho and Utah. North Carolina settled the department's separate HAVA suit (entry 272).
- "Georgia law prohibits the discl osure of voters’ full da te of birth, social security number, and driver’s license number." (Georgia's letter of Dec 8, 2025; the spacing is as extracted)
- https://www.brennancenter.org/our-work/research-reports/tracker-justice-department-requests-voter-information · https://statedemocracy.law.wisc.edu/our-work/tracker-doj-lawsuits-seeking-states-sensitive-voter-data · https://sos.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/doc/news-releases/Secretary%20Evnen%20sends%20information%20from%20Nebraska%20voter%20registration%20list%20to%20the%20Department%20of%20Justice_0.pdf · https://www.brennancenter.org/media/15326/download/ohio_secretary_of_state_doj_response_2-13-2026.pdf · https://oklahoma.gov/oag/news/newsroom/2026/march/drummond-enters-settlement-with-doj-to-safeguard-voter-registration-data.html · https://www.brennancenter.org/media/15116/download/montana_12.29.2025_state-response-1.pdf

## 257. The reasons the refusing states gave
**CONFIRMED.** From the states' own letters: state law bars release of these exact fields; the federal Privacy Act requires a published notice for a new federal records system; the stated purpose kept shifting; data security; and "we already check".
- "the statute expressly prohibits the disclosure of a registrant's Social Security number or driver's license number." (West Virginia)
- "the Department must share the Department's purpose in creating that system of records, including the notice published in the Federal Register" (Maryland)
- "Given these variations in purpose and authority, we kindly request clarification on the specific objectives behind these requests and the applicable legal authority." (Utah)
- "I have significant concerns that this is not the real reason for your request." (Washington)
- "Our office partnered directly with DHS and USCIS to verify the citizenship status of all registered Idaho voters prior to the 2024 General Election." (Idaho)
- https://www.brennancenter.org/media/15226/download/west-virginia_02.11.2026_state-refusal.pdf · https://www.brennancenter.org/media/14515/download/maryland_08.13.2025_state-response.pdf · https://www.brennancenter.org/media/14867/download/utah_08.29.2025_state-response.pdf · https://www.brennancenter.org/media/14458/download/washington_09.23.2025_state-response.pdf · https://www.brennancenter.org/media/15338/download/idaho_02.26.2026_state-refusal.pdf · Kentucky State Board of Elections letter, Aug 22, 2025

## 258. Does refusal split along party lines?
**CONFIRMED.** That it leans, and that the split is not clean. Every full handover came from a state led by one party, but several states led by that same party refused too (Idaho, Utah, West Virginia, Kentucky and Georgia among them), citing the same state privacy laws, and one sued state's chief election official belongs to that party. NBC's sentence says "six" such states but names five (Corrections table). The episode does not make a party the subject; this is for the record only.
- https://stateline.org/2025/09/12/doj-is-sharing-state-voter-roll-lists-with-homeland-security/ · https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/justice-department/tracking-dojs-effort-get-us-voter-registration-data-rcna331509 · https://www.cnn.com/2026/10/07/politics/dhs-ice-voter-fraud-investigation-undercover

## 259. In most states the voter file is public or can be bought; what is withheld is the sensitive identifiers
**CONFIRMED.** Georgia's public file includes everything except month and day of birth, Social Security number, email, licence number and the place of registration; Washington's withholds full date of birth, licence and ID numbers and the Social Security number.
- "Because voter registration forms and voter lists are government documents, they are often subject to public records laws, which require their disclosure to some individuals."
- "States have varied requirements on who is eligible to request a list of voters, what information the list contains, what information is kept confidential and how the information contained in voter lists may be used."
- "Under Washington state law, full date of birth, driver’s license number, and the last four digits of an individual’s Social Security number are protected information. These are not included in the publicly available voter registration database."
- https://www.eac.gov/sites/default/files/2024-03/Voter_Roll_Privacy.pdf · https://www.ncsl.org/elections-and-campaigns/access-to-and-use-of-voter-registration-lists · https://www.sos.wa.gov/node/29953

## 260. The department could already get the public file, and many states sent it; Homeland Security bought and analysed public files itself
**CONFIRMED.** Not every state was fully open at first: Arizona and Oregon conditioned even the public file on privacy-law compliance. Say "most", not "all".
- "Most states have provided a publicly available version (which does not include Social Security numbers and driver’s license numbers) or have not provided the voter registration lists at all."
- "public voter lists from California, New Jersey, Pennsylvania and Nevada that DHS purchased and analyzed"
- https://www.brennancenter.org/our-work/research-reports/tracker-justice-department-requests-voter-information · https://www.cnn.com/2026/10/07/politics/dhs-ice-voter-fraud-investigation-undercover

## 261. Homeland Security's national SAVE totals (as of June 22, 2026): 68 million records from 25 states, "over 400,000 deceased registrants and over 28,000 non-citizens"
**CONFIRMED.** As Homeland Security's claim. As findings they are UNRESOLVED: no method, confirmation rate or count of votes was published. The department's own Supreme Court filing called a similar number "potential" noncitizens (entry 159); the script uses "flagged".
- "As of June 22, 2026, a total of 25 different states processed more than 68 million registration records through the SAVE system. DHS has enabled those states to identify over 400,000 deceased registrants and over 28,000 non-citizens who illegally registered to vote."
- https://seb.ga.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/dhs-save-system-summary-redacted.pdf (Homeland Security summary as posted by Georgia's State Election Board on Aug 4, 2026; a scan)

## 262. The "more than 42,000 deceased registrants" figure came in a Homeland Security letter to Georgia
**CORRECTED.** The Sept 2, 2026 letter went from the Homeland Security Secretary to the Attorney General, not to Georgia, and uses the number to attack the interstate data-sharing group ERIC. The exact figure (42,776 deceased and 2,549 noncitizens on Georgia's rolls, "as of June 22, 2026") is in the Homeland Security summary posted by Georgia's State Election Board on Aug 4. The method and meaning are UNRESOLVED: the summary does not say whether those registrations were active, already inactive or already being removed, or whether any of them voted. No response from Georgia's Secretary of State on the deceased figure was found. For scale, Georgia itself removed 146,022 registrations for death in 2022–24 (entry 151).
- "identified more than 42,000 deceased registrants on Georgia's state voter rolls."
- "As of June 22, 2026, the review identified 42,776 deceased registrants and 2,549 noncitizen registrants on Georgia’s rolls."
- https://seb.ga.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Homeland-Security-Letter-9-2-2026.pdf · https://seb.ga.gov/dhs-report-georgia-voter-roll-findings/ · https://seb.ga.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/dhs-save-system-summary-redacted.pdf

## 263. Texas first announced its SAVE results on Sept 15, 2026
**CORRECTED.** (the date of the first announcement). Texas announced the 2,724 flagged registrants on Oct 20, 2025; the Sept 15, 2026 release is the follow-up (578 shown to be citizens, 117 referred; entry 160). After review, 117 referrals out of 18 million registrations. Homeland Security's own table lists Texas at 2,296 noncitizens, which matches neither figure, so it is not aired (UNRESOLVED).
- "The analysis identified 2,724 Texas voters who may not be U.S. citizens."
- https://www.hppr.org/hppr-news/2025-10-22/texas-flags-more-than-2-700-potential-noncitizens-on-voter-rolls · https://www.sos.state.tx.us/about/newsreleases/2026/091526.shtml

## 264. Homeland Security: 250,000 noncitizens registered in four states (July 2026)
**CORRECTED.** By the record since. The claim came from bought public files and commercial databases. In Nevada, the department's 15,903 had produced 185 manually "confirmed" noncitizens by Aug 13, 2026, and even those were not definitive by the department's own account. California and Pennsylvania (whose chief election official belongs to the same party as the administration) asked for the method.
- "there may be up to 190,832 noncitizens registered to vote in California, 35,152 in New Jersey, 15,903 in Nevada and 14,576 in Pennsylvania."
- "DHS officials have “confirmed” just 185 Nevada registrants as noncitizens through manual review. More than 14,000 cases remained unresolved"
- "it hasn’t had a full manual review of each one."
- "Please provide a document explaining your methodology, which identifies the data sources you relied on."
- https://www.cbsnews.com/news/trump-markwayne-mullin-non-citizens-registered-voters-california-new-jersey-nevada-pennsylvania/ · https://www.cnn.com/2026/08/21/politics/markwayne-mullin-voter-fraud-nevada · https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/elections/states-push-dhs-explain-noncitizen-voter-claims-rcna589999

## 265. A real failure was found by a state that refused the federal demand: New Jersey's motor-vehicle software registered about 6,600 people who had said they were noncitizens (June 2023 – June 2024); fewer than 400 voted
**CONFIRMED.** (WHYY, July 21, 2026, quoting the governor). The error ran for a year and was made public in July 2026, and New Jersey then refused to share those registrants' details with federal investigators. It cuts both ways.
- "A software glitch in the New Jersey Motor Vehicle Commission computer system improperly registered about 6,600 noncitizens to vote in 2023, but fewer than 400 cast ballots in elections between 2023 and 2024"
- "Some individuals indicated that they weren’t citizens and weren’t eligible to vote, but the software registered them anyway."
- https://whyy.org/articles/new-jersey-motor-vehicle-commission-glitch-noncitizens-registered-vote/ · https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/elections/states-push-dhs-explain-noncitizen-voter-claims-rcna589999

## 266. The Justice Department's own published "findings" from the files it got
**UNRESOLVED.** No dataset, method or state-by-state result has been published. The department has charged dozens of noncitizens in recent weeks (64 across 17 states in about five weeks, by its figures to a news outlet), but it does not say whether any case came from the state files, and three of the states with the most cases refused the full file. ICE's internal records show many opened cases were never referred for prosecution.
- "The Justice Department has charged 64 foreign nationals across 17 states with unlawful voting, fraudulent voter registration and election-related offenses in roughly the last five weeks"
- "By late May, HSI had opened 418 voter-fraud cases and made 64 administrative arrests. But 318 of those cases had not been referred for prosecution."
- https://www.foxnews.com/politics/map-doj-illegal-voting-crackdown-sweeps-17-states-razor-thin-elections-face-scrutiny · https://www.cnn.com/2026/10/07/politics/dhs-ice-voter-fraud-investigation-undercover · https://www.allsides.com/news/2026-03-11-0700/voting-rights-and-voter-fraud-top-doj-prosecutor-says-tens-thousands-noncitizens-voter-rolls-dozens-illegally-voted

## 267. States that refused the federal demand run the checks themselves
**CONFIRMED.** Idaho ran its whole roll through SAVE (11 referrals, none of whom voted in 2024); Georgia uses SAVE, ERIC, the federal death file and licence data; West Virginia is a SAVE user; Utah's own review found 27 confirmed noncitizens (entry 158).
- "None of these referrals concerned voters who voted in the 2024 Primary or General Elections" (Idaho; an OCR of the letter)
- "the WVSOS is a registered and recently approved user of the SAVE database"
- https://www.brennancenter.org/media/15338/download/idaho_02.26.2026_state-refusal.pdf · https://www.brennancenter.org/media/15226/download/west-virginia_02.11.2026_state-refusal.pdf

## 268. Oct 9, 2026: a federal judge in Washington, D.C. vacated the Justice Department's whole "Voter List Maintenance Policy" nationwide
**CONFIRMED.** (the opinion and order). The court held that the claimed statutes do not authorise the policy, that it is arbitrary and capricious, and that it violates the Privacy Act and the Paperwork Reduction Act. The ruling also says what it does not do. It covers files the department already received. No appeal or stay request was found as of Oct 13; re-check on script day.
- "The Court sets aside and vacates the Defendants’ Voter List Maintenance Policy" … "This is an appealable final order."
- "First, the Defendants’ claimed authorities—Civil Rights Act of 1960, the National Voter Registration Act, and the Help America Vote Act—do not authorize the policy."
- "Today’s decision does not prevent the federal government from taking lawful steps to support states’ efforts to maintain accurate voter rolls."
- "The ruling applies to the voter files that the Justice Department has already received, as well as the files it is still litigating over." (CBS)
- https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.dcd.291580/gov.uscourts.dcd.291580.51.0_1.pdf (the opinion, Doc. 51; the order is Doc. 52) · https://www.cbsnews.com/news/judge-blocks-doj-state-voter-roll-policy-civil-rights/

## 269. The Supreme Court's Sept 25, 2026 SAVE stay and the Oct 9 vacatur are two different things
**CONFIRMED.** (as described in the Oct 9 opinion). States may use the modified SAVE system (the stay); the Justice Department's program of collecting the states' rolls and pressing removals is vacated (Oct 9). Keep them apart on air.
- "In September 2026, the Supreme Court stayed this Court’s Order, concluding that the Government was likely to prevail on its argument that 8 U.S.C. § 1373 authorized the modifications to SAVE."
- https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.dcd.291580/gov.uscourts.dcd.291580.51.0_1.pdf

## 270. The grounds in the dismissals, beyond the two families already logged
**CONFIRMED.** (the Oregon, California, Maine and Washington rulings, and the Brennan Center's grouping). These are rulings on the department's power to demand the data, not findings that any roll is clean.
- "The Department of Justice seeks to use civil rights legislation which was enacted for an entirely different purpose to amass and retain an unprecedented amount of confidential voter data." (California, Jan 15, 2026)
- "do not contemplate production of the unredacted computerized list to the Attorney General so that he might loom over the shoulder of the state election official" (Maine, May 21, 2026)
- "acknowledged that this case is factually indistinguishable from" (Washington, Sept 22, 2026, of the department's own counsel)
- https://www.brennancenter.org/our-work/analysis-opinion/federal-courts-reject-trump-administrations-attempts-obtain-private-voter · U.S. District Courts for the Central District of California (Jan 15, 2026), the District of Oregon (Feb 5, 2026), the District of Maine (May 21, 2026) and the Western District of Washington (Sept 22, 2026)

## 271. Since Oct 1, 2026: the Oct 9 vacatur; Delaware, Hawaii, Idaho and Utah still pending; the deadline to seek Supreme Court review of the Michigan ruling is Nov 12
**CONFIRMED.** (as of the trackers' dates). No appellate merits ruling since Oct 1 was found.
- "such an appeal would be due November 12, 2026."
- https://statedemocracy.law.wisc.edu/our-work/tracker-doj-lawsuits-seeking-states-sensitive-voter-data

## 272. North Carolina: the Justice Department's own HAVA suit led to a consent judgment to fill in missing ID numbers, with no one removed for it
**CONFIRMED.**
- "the project aims to collect identification numbers — driver’s license numbers or last four digits of Social Security numbers — from voters missing them on the state’s voter rolls."
- "no voter is being removed from the voter rolls because of it."
- https://www.ncsbe.gov/news/press-releases/2025/09/08/judge-approves-settlement-usdoj-lawsuit-about-voter-registrations

## 273. List-maintenance suits by private groups have won real changes
**CONFIRMED.** Los Angeles County and California agreed in 2019 to notify and potentially remove up to 1.5 million inactive registrations; an intervenor's counsel called the plaintiff's statistics misleading. In Kentucky, the Justice Department intervened under a consent decree that expired March 31, 2025.
- "California and Los Angeles County have agreed to purge as many as 1.5 million inactive voter registrations across the state as part of a court settlement"
- "Using misleading statistics"
- https://www.nbclosangeles.com/news/la-county-and-state-to-purge-15-million-inactive-voters-from-rolls/6866/ · https://www.demos.org/case/judicial-watch-v-logan-judicial-watch-v-mi-familia-vota

## 274. Cross-state checking has thinned
**CONFIRMED.** ERIC once had 33 member states; nine had left by 2023, citing data protection, partisanship and strategic disagreements. It now lists 27 states plus DC (entry 154).
- "At its height, 33 states were participating members"
- https://news.ballotpedia.org/2023/06/06/texas-set-to-become-ninth-state-to-withdraw-from-eric-arizonas-possible-withdrawal-vetoed-by-gov-hobbs-d/

## 275. The law the refusing states cite is real, and so far every court agrees with them on the department's power
**CONFIRMED.** State statutes bar releasing exactly these fields (entries 257, 259); no court has ruled for the department (entry 162); the Oct 9 court found the program violated the Privacy Act (entry 268).

## 276. Wrongly flagged citizens are a documented pattern
**CONFIRMED.** A 2006 Social Security audit found 7% of its "noncitizen" records were citizens; in one Texas county a quarter of noncitizen matches had already proven citizenship; Texas 2019's 95,000 list and Florida 2012's 180,000 shrank sharply; Nevada 2026 went from 15,903 claimed to 185 confirmed (entry 264).
- "A 2006 SSA audit found that 7% of the non- U.S. citizen records in SSA’s database—totaling approximately 3.3 million people—misidentified U.S. citizens as noncitizens."
- "Florida compiled a list of roughly 180,000 names. After local officials combed through it, only 85 people were removed from the rolls."
- https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.dcd.291580/gov.uscourts.dcd.291580.51.0_1.pdf · https://www.npr.org/2019/01/28/689473861/voting-rights-groups-push-back-on-texas-plan-to-purge-thousands-from-voter-rolls

## 277. Refusing the Justice Department is not refusing checks
**CONFIRMED.** Idaho, West Virginia and Georgia, all refusers, run their own rolls through SAVE (entry 267), and the federal 90-day blackout and two-election wait are federal law, not state concealment (entry 153).

## Case file: "statistically impossible" — late-night ballot batches
Tray: EXPLAINED. PROVEN as errors, caught: Shiawassee County's typo and Prince William County's miscount.

## 278. Pennsylvania and Wisconsin still cannot start processing mail ballots before Election Day
**CONFIRMED.** (NCSL's table, updated Sept 15, 2026). Six states in all wait until Election Day: Alabama, Mississippi, New Hampshire, Pennsylvania, West Virginia and Wisconsin.
- "7 a.m. on Election Day: Envelope declaration is examined and information is compared." (Pennsylvania)
- "After the polls open on Election Day." (Wisconsin)
- "Six states permit election officials to begin processing absentee/mail ballots on Election Day, but prior to the closing of the polls:"
- https://www.ncsl.org/elections-and-campaigns/table-16-when-absentee-mail-ballot-processing-and-counting-can-begin

## 279. Michigan changed its rules after 2020; Georgia lets counties start weeks early
**CONFIRMED.** In 2020 Michigan was among the states that had to wait until Election Day.
- "Jurisdictions with at least 5,000 people may begin further processing eight days before Election Day, and other jurisdictions may begin the Monday before Election Day."
- "Third Monday before Election Day: Outer oath envelope may be opened and the contents removed." (Georgia)
- "But with additional days and flexibility added into law in 2023, the option has helped to expedite results in places that choose to use it."
- https://www.ncsl.org/elections-and-campaigns/table-16-when-absentee-mail-ballot-processing-and-counting-can-begin · https://www.votebeat.org/michigan/2024/08/27/michigan-absentee-ballot-preprocessing-early-law/ · https://electionlab.mit.edu/sites/default/files/2021-04/Blue-Shift-in-2020-Election.pdf

## 280. "Central count" cities report all their absentee ballots in one block, so the overnight jump is one update, not a trickle
**CONFIRMED.**
- "results from Milwaukee’s Central Count have come in between 12 a.m. and 4 a.m. every Presidential Election due to the restrictive state laws." (Milwaukee's former election director)
- "This process cannot be completed until 100% of the absentee votes have been tabulated."
- "Those absentee votes — a total of 170,000 in Milwaukee — were reported together because Milwaukee and 38 other Wisconsin communities used a central count location."
- "Detroit had more than 174,000 absentee ballots compared with only about 83,000 votes from Election Day."
- https://politifact.com/factchecks/2024/nov/14/instagram-posts/no-an-early-morning-influx-of-milwaukee-votes-does/ · https://politifact.com/factchecks/2020/nov/20/donald-trump/trump-again-flat-wrong-claims-about-wisconsin-vote/ · https://www.votebeat.org/michigan/2024/08/27/michigan-absentee-ballot-preprocessing-early-law/

## 281. Mail voters and in-person voters split differently, so a late mail block leans one way — documented, not inferred
**CONFIRMED.** Nationally (Pew, self-reported), 46% voted by mail in 2020, and the winner's voters were far more likely to. Philadelphia's own certified 2020 division file, split by vote type: the city's winning candidate took 71.4% of the two-way vote on Election Day and 92.1% of the two-way mail vote (our count from the city's file). The MIT lab found states were slower to report where mail volume was large and pre-processing was barred. A working paper the lab also cites (Foley and Stewart) was not opened, so it is not quoted.
- "A slim majority of voters (54%) say they voted in person this November, compared with 46% who voted by absentee or mail-in ballot."
- "States were slower to report overall vote counts if they had large volumes of mail ballots, prohibited preprocessing mail ballots, and allowed mail ballots to arrive after Election Day."
- https://www.pewresearch.org/politics/2020/11/20/the-voting-experience-in-2020/ · https://phillyvotes-files.s3.amazonaws.com/raw-data/2020_general.xlsx · https://electionlab.mit.edu/sites/default/files/2021-07/curiel_stewart_williams_blue_shift_esra_final.pdf

## 282. The shift is not one-directional: where mail is pre-processed, early returns lean the other way
**CONFIRMED.** — the best balance point in this part.
- "in the first couple of hours after polls closed, there was actually a disproportionate number of" … "votes reported, which we show were likely due to preprocessed mail ballots being reported first."
- "Second, most states actually saw a _red shift_ during this period."
- https://electionlab.mit.edu/sites/default/files/2021-07/curiel_stewart_williams_blue_shift_esra_final.pdf · https://electionupdates.caltech.edu/2024/10/31/blue-shift-red-shift/

## 283. Milwaukee, Nov 4, 2020, about 3:30 a.m.: the city's absentee results, about 170,000 ballots, in one update — and not all for one side
**CONFIRMED.** The size is APPROXIMATE (170,000 per PolitiFact; about 176,000 per Milwaukee's current director). In the jump between 3:26 and 3:44 a.m., the winner gained 149,520 votes and the runner-up 31,803: about 82.5% to 17.5%, not "all for one candidate".
- "That 3:30 a.m. change simply shows the time when the City of Milwaukee reported its absentee ballot results."
- "Milwaukee County accounted for nearly all of that jump."
- "the governor's race was about 66,000 and the pandemic presidential was about 176,000" (Milwaukee's director)
- https://politifact.com/factchecks/2020/nov/20/donald-trump/trump-again-flat-wrong-claims-about-wisconsin-vote/ · https://www.therecombobulationarea.news/p/milwaukees-late-night-ballot-dump

## 284. Michigan, Wednesday morning 2020: four "spikes" cast "solely" for one candidate, per a sworn affidavit
**CORRECTED.** By the affidavit's own chart, the four "spikes" are counties (Kent, Macomb, Oakland and Wayne) and both candidates gained in them: the winner 255,856 and the runner-up 128,877, total 384,733, the affidavit's own figure. The runner-up got 33.5% of the votes the affidavit says were cast solely for the other side. The Michigan Senate's oversight committee said such spikes are expected and not evidence of fraud.
- "could easily be produced in the Dominion system by pre-loading batches of blank ballots" (the affidavit, read from page images)
- "Large spikes in the vote count are not necessarily unexplainable or unusual. They do not alone constitute evidence of fraud and can be reasonably expected."
- "This makes the spike larger than just the final count from the AVCB."
- https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.283580/gov.uscourts.gand.283580.7.1_2.pdf · https://misenategopcdn.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/99/doccuments/20210623/SMPO_2020ElectionReport.pdf (p. 28)

## 285. Milwaukee 2024, about 4 a.m.: "nearly 90%" of the absentee batch for one candidate
**CORRECTED.** Milwaukee received 108,964 absentee ballots on Election Day; the split was 80.9% to 16.2%, and the same candidate took 73.2% of the city's Election Day vote. The late block was about 8 points more one-sided than the city's in-person vote — the mail/in-person gap in entry 281, not a different electorate.
- "Milwaukee received 108,964 absentee ballots on Election Day."
- https://politifact.com/factchecks/2024/nov/15/poladmin/hovdes-claim-baldwin-won-nearly-90-of-milwaukee-ab/

## 286. Counting was much faster in 2024 where the rules changed; Wisconsin, which didn't change, was no faster
**CONFIRMED.** Hours to count 95% of the two-party presidential vote, 2020 to 2024: Michigan 17 to 9, Pennsylvania 49 to 8, Georgia 18 to 5, Wisconsin 8 to 9. Pennsylvania got faster without a pre-processing law, so lower mail volume is part of it. MIT measured the speed of counting, not the size of the shift; a measured 2024 size was not found.
- "In Wisconsin, it took a similar amount of time after the two elections."
- "allowed more than 80% of the absentee results to be reported by about 10:30 p.m." (Detroit, Aug 2024 primary)
- https://electionlab.mit.edu/articles/how-long-did-it-take-count-vote-2024 · https://www.votebeat.org/michigan/2024/08/27/michigan-absentee-ballot-preprocessing-early-law/

## 287. Real overnight problems exist, and the record shows how they were caught
**CONFIRMED.** Shiawassee County's typo (entry 310) was fixed within the hour; Milwaukee's 2024 re-run of about 31,000 ballots (entry 64) was caught in public view; and in Prince William County, Virginia, a certified 2020 count was wrong by 3,975 votes in the margin, in both directions across races, and was found when two precincts showed identical presidential totals. The criminal charges against the former registrar were dropped.
- "did not consistently favor one party or candidate but were likely due to a lack of proper planning, a difficult election environment, and human error."
- "he first discovered the mistakes when he noticed that Precincts 607 and 608 displayed identical presidential votes."
- "Other clerks, including Republicans, have acknowledged making momentary mistakes on election night with unofficial results."
- https://www.nbcwashington.com/news/local/northern-virginia/prince-william-county-admits-election-tally-in-2020-shorted-joe-biden/3514995/ · https://www.bridgemi.com/michigan-government/gop-clerks-typo-michigan-fuels-false-vote-dumping-claims

## Case file: "statistically impossible" — more than 100% turnout
Tray: EXPLAINED. OPEN — Pennsylvania's *more votes than voters* remainder: (a) whether the gap between certified ballots and the voter-history database (an advocacy figure of about 121,000) ever closed; (b) the Department of State's reconciliation of vote histories with certified totals would settle it; (c) the Department of State and the counties hold it.

## 288. A Michigan affidavit's "more than 100% turnout" precincts were in Minnesota
**CONFIRMED.** Monticello P-2, listed at 138%, had 4,202 registered voters and 3,776 presidential votes (89.9%). The same affidavit's "431,954 excess ballots" rests on the same estimated-voters base and is not aired.
- "There were at least 19 precincts where the Presidential Votes Cast compared to the Estimated Voters based on Reported Statistics exceeded 100%."
- "there were 4,202 registered voters in Monticello P-2, but only 3,776 votes were cast in the presidential race – showing turnout was actually less than 100 percent."
- https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.283580/gov.uscourts.gand.283580.7.1_2.pdf · https://fox2detroit.com/news/trump-lawyers-wrongly-cite-election-red-flags-in-michigan-that-actually-happened-in-minnesota

## 289. The corrected Michigan list, filed weeks later, was still wrong
**CONFIRMED.** Detroit's turnout was 50.88% of registered voters, not 139.29%.
- "The city’s official results show that turnout in the city was actually 50.88% of registered voters."
- "did not exceed the number of registered voters in any of the jurisdictions named."
- https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.mied.350905/gov.uscourts.mied.350905.59.1.pdf · https://misenategopcdn.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/99/doccuments/20210623/SMPO_2020ElectionReport.pdf (p. 25)

## 290. One "more than 100%" figure was in an official county report, and it was a typo in the registered-voter count, not the votes
**CONFIRMED.** The one real figure behind the claim: the county corrected it on Dec 3. A figure over 100% is usually a broken denominator.
- "initially matched the data in the county’s statement of votes cast, but that’s because there was an error in the report."
- "The county released a corrected report Dec. 3."
- https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.mied.350905/gov.uscourts.mied.350905.59.1.pdf

## 291. Wisconsin's "more votes than registered voters" used an old registration figure
**CONFIRMED.** Same-day registration makes a pre-election roll count a floor.
- "So, there were nearly 388,000 more voters registered in Wisconsin than there were votes cast"
- "which means that the voter registration numbers are likely even higher than the total reported on the state website on Nov. 1."
- https://www.factcheck.org/2020/11/viral-posts-misreport-data-on-registered-voters-in-wisconsin/

## 292. "Seven Milwaukee wards had more votes than registered voters": the county's own ward results say no
**CONFIRMED.** Whether any individual Wisconsin ward shows more than 100% because same-day registrants are missing from a pre-election count is a sound mechanism, but no named example was found: UNRESOLVED as an example.
- "In fact, the seven Milwaukee wards cited in the article had more registered voters than ballots cast."
- https://politifact.com/factchecks/2020/nov/05/facebook-posts/milwaukee-wards-did-not-have-more-votes-president/

## 293. Pennsylvania's "205,000 more votes than voters" compared certified ballots with a registration database counties had not finished updating
**CONFIRMED.** On the mechanism. Whether a gap remained after all counties uploaded is UNRESOLVED: an advocacy analysis says about 121,000 remained, and no official reconciliation was found. The script does not say the gap "closed"; it says the claim compared two different records, one still being filled in, and the certified count comes from the counties' ballots.
- "The difference of 202,377 more votes cast than voters voting"
- "At this time, there are still a few counties that have not completed uploading their vote histories to the SURE system."
- "The numbers certified by the counties, not the uploading of voter histories into the SURE system, determines the ultimate certification of an election by the secretary."
- "SURE is not designed to determine election winners, and never has been." (a county elections director)
- https://lebtown.com/2021/01/08/county-elections-chief-rebuts-alarming-discrepancy-alleged-by-reps-ryan-and-diamond-diamond-apparently-satisfied · https://levittownnow.com/2020/12/30/state-election-officials-dismiss-allegations-of-discrepancies-in-pa-election-count/ · https://joefriedcpa.substack.com/p/pennsylvanias-2020-election-was-invalid

## 294. Real over-voting exists, at a scale of dozens, and gets caught
**CONFIRMED.** Michigan, 2016: 31 people appear to have voted twice and were referred; compare Arizona's 37 possible double votes (entry 123).
- "Bureau of Elections staff discovered 31 individuals who appear to have voted twice — once by absentee ballot and again by voting in person on Election Day — who will be referred to the Michigan Attorney General’s Office for criminal investigation."
- https://content.govdelivery.com/accounts/MISOS/bulletins/186213e

## Case file: Detroit 2020 — "more votes than voters" in the absentee counting boards
Tray: EXPLAINED for *more votes than voters*. PROVEN as sloppiness: 95 of 134 absentee boards out of balance at the canvass, 14 after the audit, net 21 more names than ballots.

## 295. What "out of balance" means: the names in the poll book or absentee list do not match the ballots counted, and the gap is not explained
**CONFIRMED.** Detroit had 134 counting boards covering its 503 precincts, each handling thousands of ballots. Typical causes are mundane (overseas or damaged ballots that have to be copied, a voter who walks out, an empty envelope, two ballots in one envelope).
- "determining that the number of names in the poll book (in a polling place) or list of absentee voters (in a counting board) matched the number of ballots tabulated exactly (or that an explanation could be found for the imbalance)."
- "Other causes come from empty absentee voter ballot envelopes, or couples including both of their ballots in one envelope."
