- https://www.michigan.gov/documents/sos/BOE_2020_Post_Election_Audit_Report_04_21_21_723005_7.pdf (p. 5) · https://misenategopcdn.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/99/doccuments/20210623/SMPO_2020ElectionReport.pdf (p. 23)

## 296. Out-of-balance precincts cannot be recounted
**CONFIRMED.** With a nuance: "sometimes". An out-of-balance precinct can still be recounted if the container count matches the tabulator tape. Say *can be barred from a recount*.
- "Out-of-balance precincts sometimes cannot be recounted under the Michigan Election Law."
- https://www.michigan.gov/documents/sos/BOE_2020_Post_Election_Audit_Report_04_21_21_723005_7.pdf (p. 9)

## 297. Detroit's imbalance ran the other way from "more votes than voters"
**CONFIRMED.** Wayne County was "minus" around 475 (names without ballots) out of more than 860,000 votes; Detroit's absentee boards, after the state audit, had 21 more names than ballots out of about 174,000. Had ballots been added, ballots would exceed names.
- "there are 475 names without ballots attached to them"
- "the very close correspondence in records—between to the number of voters on each absent voter list, the number of returned AV envelopes, and the number of ballots tabulated—disproves claims that large numbers of ballots were somehow added to tabulators or improperly included in counts."
- https://www.michiganpublic.org/politics-government/2020-11-20/drama-at-the-board-of-canvassers-no-thats-not-normal-heres-whats-up · https://www.michigan.gov/documents/sos/BOE_2020_Post_Election_Audit_Report_04_21_21_723005_7.pdf (p. 10, fn. 11)

## 298. After the county canvass, about 7 in 10 of Detroit's 134 absentee counting boards were out of balance
**CONFIRMED.** With three slightly different counts in the record (94, 95 or 98); 95 is the state's corrected count. About a third of Election Day precincts were out too.
- "Following the county canvass, 95 of 134 AV counting boards were out of balance."
- "A prior version of this report listed the number of counting boards out of balance following the canvass as 98, which was inaccurate and has been corrected."
- "33% of Election Day precincts and 70% of absentee voter precincts in the city were imbalanced, according to the post-election canvass."
- https://www.michigan.gov/documents/sos/BOE_2020_Post_Election_Audit_Report_04_21_21_723005_7.pdf (p. 20) · https://www.michiganpublic.org/politics-government/2020-11-18/wayne-county-board-of-canvassers-deadlocks-then-reverses-and-votes-to-certify-election · https://www.bridgemi.com/?p=6153

## 299. How big the imbalances were at canvass: most boards off by four or fewer; a few hundred ballots in total
**APPROXIMATE.** (no primary board-by-board canvass table was found; the per-board maximum at canvass is unresolved).
- "In all, 63 Detroit counting boards were off by about four votes or fewer, creating a discrepancy of about 139 votes."
- "In most cases, the precincts were out of balance by less than three votes"
- https://www.bridgemi.com/?p=6153 · https://www.bridgemi.com/michigan-government/gop-canvassers-want-do-over-wayne-county-results-too-late-experts-say

## 300. Detroit was not unique
**CONFIRMED.**
- "In Livonia, a similar percentage of absentee counting boards were out of balance, 68 percent (30 of 44), while 47 percent were unbalanced in Dearborn (21 of 45) and 51 percent were in Canton Township (21 of 41)."
- "Precincts remained unbalanced and unreconciled in several of Michigan’s largest counties, including Saginaw, Ottawa, Ingham and Genesee, but that did not prevent canvassers from voting unanimously to certify county election results."
- https://www.bridgemi.com/?p=6153 · https://www.bridgemi.com/michigan-government/gop-canvassers-want-do-over-wayne-county-results-too-late-experts-say

## 301. The Wayne County board deadlocked 2–2, then certified 4–0 with an audit request; the stated reason for the "no" votes was the poll books
**CONFIRMED.** The same board had certified the August 2020 primary and the 2016 general unanimously despite similar imbalances.
- "The Wayne County Board of Canvassers initially deadlocked 2-2 on whether to certify the results"
- "The change in course was approved by the two Republican and two Democratic canvassers with the demand that the Secretary of State’s office conduct an audit of unbalanced absentee counting board results."
- "Importantly, this is not an indication that any votes were improperly cast or counted." (the Secretary of State that night)
- https://kvia.com/politics/2020/11/17/michigans-largest-county-certifies-election-results-after-republicans-earlier-blocked-certification/ · https://electionlawblog.org/?p=118771 · https://www.michiganpublic.org/politics-government/2020-11-18/wayne-county-board-of-canvassers-deadlocks-then-reverses-and-votes-to-certify-election

## 302. The state board certified Michigan on Nov 23, 2020, 3–0 with one abstention
**CONFIRMED.** (the board's minutes, filed in a federal suit).
- "Time of certification: 4:34 p.m."
- https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.mied.350905/gov.uscourts.mied.350905.31.4.pdf

## 303. After the state audit, 14 of 134 boards remained out of balance, each by five ballots or fewer
**CONFIRMED.** (our count from the audit's own table). The 95 rows net to minus 21 (21 more names than ballots); the 14 unresolved boards range from minus 5 to plus 5; the largest leftover gap (minus 26 on one board) was matched by plus 26 on the next. The causes named include an electronic absentee list that slowed under large boards and ballots counted in the wrong precinct.
- "so that a total of 14 counting boards remained out of balance."
- "some had more names than ballots and some had more ballots than names."
- "Some absent voter counting board imbalances could be explained but could not be corrected after the fact, such as tabulating a ballot in the wrong precinct."
- https://www.michigan.gov/documents/sos/BOE_2020_Post_Election_Audit_Report_04_21_21_723005_7.pdf (pp. 19–23)

## 304. The audit's fraud finding, in its own words
**CONFIRMED.**
- "Post-election audits conducted by the Bureau of Elections and county clerks found no examples of fraud or intentional misconduct by election officials"
- https://www.michigan.gov/documents/sos/BOE_2020_Post_Election_Audit_Report_04_21_21_723005_7.pdf (p. 5)

## 305. Detroit's audit: "17 out of 174,000" and "83% balanced"
**CORRECTED.** Those are the March 2, 2021 press-release figures. The April 2021 report revised them (95 out at canvass, 14 remaining, so about 89.6% balanced or explained; a net of 21) and says why. Use the April report: 21 out of about 174,000 (entry 77).
- "the net number of ballots out of balance for the entire board was 17 out of the 174,000 absentee ballots counted in Detroit." (March press release, as reported)
- "additional review of audit records identified adjustments from previously reported numbers of balanced precincts and net differences."
- https://www.wxyz.com/news/benson-more-than-250-election-audits-confirm-accuracy-integrity-of-michigans-general-election · https://www.michigan.gov/documents/sos/BOE_2020_Post_Election_Audit_Report_04_21_21_723005_7.pdf (fn. 13)

## 306. The Republican-led Senate committee: the imbalances were "marginal", and fewer than in earlier elections
**CONFIRMED.** It also left the door open in principle, then the canvass and audit looked and found clerical causes.
- "However, the unbalanced precincts in Michigan counties were marginal and, in no way, would have impacted the outcome of the Presidential election."
- "Technically, the imbalances that remain after the canvass could exist due to fraudulent activity."
- https://misenategopcdn.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/99/doccuments/20210623/SMPO_2020ElectionReport.pdf (pp. 23–24)

## 307. 2016: worse, audited, human error
**CONFIRMED.** The audit was ordered by a Secretary of State of the other party from 2020's. Which 2016 precinct-balance percentage is right is unresolved, so the ballot counts are used, not a percentage.
- "largely finding that human error, not illegal activity, resulted in mismatches between the number of ballots and recorded voters."
- "The total number of ballots in question in the remaining precincts was less than 600 out of 250,000 total cast citywide, and Elections staff was able to reduce that number to less than 200."
- https://content.govdelivery.com/accounts/MISOS/bulletins/186213e

## 308. The August 2020 primary: about the same share of absentee boards out (72%), which is why the state stepped in before November
**CONFIRMED.**
- "after finding that recorded ballot counts in 72 percent of absentee voting precincts didn't match the number of ballots cast."
- https://www.deadlinedetroit.com/articles/26031/ballot_counts_at_72_of_detroit_s_precincts_were_inaccurate · https://www.michiganpublic.org/politics-government/2020-08-21/wayne-county-elections-board-detroit-needs-help-counting-absentee-votes-after-primary-errors

## 309. Detroit's counting boards balanced better (or worse) in 2022 and 2024
**UNRESOLVED.** The process changed (pre-processing, early tabulation), but three targeted searches found no published count of out-of-balance Detroit boards for Nov 2022 or Nov 2024. The script does not say it "improved" or "got worse".
- "Work to pre-process absentee ballots is already underway, allowing election officials to prepare ballots for counting on Election Day."
- https://www.bridgemi.com/michigan-government/detroit-predicting-high-turnout-faster-count-presidential-race

## Case file: "statistically impossible" — one-sided batches and zero-vote precincts
Tray: EXPLAINED.

## 310. Shiawassee County, Michigan, Nov 4, 2020: "138,339 votes for one candidate, zero for the other" was a typo
**CONFIRMED.** 153,710 was entered instead of 15,371 — an extra zero, 2.8 times the county's 55,612 registered voters. The county clerk's office made the error in its report to the state, and it was corrected within about 40 minutes (posts at 5:04 and 5:43 a.m.). After the fix, the county went to the other candidate by more than 8,000 votes.
- "There was a typo in the initial report that went out to the Bureau of Elections. It was caught in a very short amount of time and quickly corrected,"
- "Once we identified the error, we cleared the erroneous data and updated it with the correct data as provided by officials."
- "kind of error is fairly common. Preliminary results are sometimes updated manually, for instance by copying and pasting from a spreadsheet."
- https://www.factcheck.org/2020/11/clerical-error-prompts-unfounded-claims-about-michigan-results/ · https://www.bridgemi.com/michigan-government/gop-clerks-typo-michigan-fuels-false-vote-dumping-claims

## 311. Philadelphia, Nov 4, 2020: "23,277 votes, all for one candidate" was one candidate's numbers entered before the other's
**CONFIRMED.** (relayed; the feed itself was not opened). The size of the offsetting update is not given.
- "Election officials and vote tabulators occasionally enter results one candidate at a time, and it looks like that's what happened here,"
- https://thedispatch.com/article/did-philadelphia-really-report-23277/

## 312. "Votes switched" and "votes deleted" in the media data feed: rounding
**CONFIRMED.** The feed carried candidates' shares rounded to three decimals for news graphics, not vote counts, so a total multiplied by a rounded share "loses" thousands of votes on paper (about 3,500 per update at Pennsylvania's size). A sworn affidavit called this feed the machine maker's "direct feed to news outlets"; the feed's own field names the media data company (Corrections table).
- "used data from a private election services company which had been formatted to automatically update Times graphics displays. It did not contain actual vote counts for the candidates."
- "There is no factual basis for this claim." (Pennsylvania's Department of State)
- "For those looking at these data, hoping to audit election results, it is important to keep in mind that data-entry errors are likely to be greater than errors made by election officials" (MIT)
- https://leadstories.com/hoax-alert/2020/11/fact-check-pedeinspector-analysis-thedonald-win.html · https://www.boomlive.in/world/trump-falsely-claims-votes-cast-for-him-in-penn-were-switched-to-biden-10646 · https://electionlab.mit.edu/sites/default/files/2021-04/Blue-Shift-in-2020-Election.pdf

## 313. Philadelphia 2012: "59 divisions" with zero votes for the runner-up, a "mathematical and statistical impossibility"
**CORRECTED.** On the count, CONFIRMED on the explanation. The city's final division file shows 50 of 1,687 divisions with zero for the runner-up (16,860 votes, 139 to 612 per division) and 460 with five or fewer; none with zero for the winner. 59 was an election-night unofficial count. The divisions are small, in neighbourhoods with very few registered members of the other party.
- "The division out of the 59 with the lowest turnout counted 139 votes in 2012; the highest was 612."
- "Votes cannot be subtracted from Philadelphia voting machines."
- https://files7.philadelphiavotes.com/election-results/2012_General_WD.csv · https://politifact.com/factchecks/2016/aug/12/viral-image/internet-philly-rigged-2012-presidential-election/ · https://www.phillymag.com/?p=713031

## 314. Philadelphia 2020: one division at zero
**CONFIRMED.** (official data): 1 of 1,706 divisions shows zero for the runner-up (131 to 0); 77 show five or fewer.
- https://phillyvotes-files.s3.amazonaws.com/raw-data/2020_general.xlsx

## 315. It happens on the other side too: a Rockland County, New York precinct gave the 2024 winner 552 votes and the runner-up zero
**CONFIRMED.** The same precinct went 528–0 in 2020, and four other county precincts showed zero for the runner-up in 2024. Experts tied it to bloc voting. In 2024 the "impossible zero" was cited as proof that the winning side had hacked the count: the same claim, used by both sides.
- "Experts said it’s not evidence of election hacking, as Ramapo has a large Orthodox and Hasidic Jewish population that often votes in blocs in support of their rabbis’ endorsed candidates."
- https://politifact.com/factchecks/2025/feb/26/social-media/why-did-kamala-harris-get-zero-votes-in-this-ny-pr/

## 316. What one-sided precincts look like statistically, and when they would be a real red flag
**CONFIRMED.** (method). A small unit in a neighbourhood that votes 95%+ one way will often show a zero for the other side. The cross-national fraud fingerprint is different: many units clustering at both near-100% turnout and near-100% for the winner (Russia 2011–12, Uganda). Philadelphia's zero divisions had ordinary turnout. A zero is suspicious when it breaks the precinct's own history, comes with near-100% turnout, isn't followed further down the ballot, comes with more ballots than poll-book names, or fails a hand count.
- "The second peak is situated in the vicinity of the 100% turnout and 100% votes point."
- "the average number of registered voters per division is 616"
- https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3478593/ · https://politifact.com/factchecks/2016/aug/12/viral-image/internet-philly-rigged-2012-presidential-election/

## Case file: what statistics can and can't prove
Tray: EXPLAINED for 2020's statistical claims. PROVEN: North Carolina's 9th, where the statistics pointed at something real.

## 317. The peer-reviewed review of 2020's statistical claims: each "anomaly" was either not a fact or not anomalous
**CONFIRMED.** (Eggers, Garro and Grimmer, PNAS 2021). The episode's on-screen rule comes from it.
- "In each case, we find that the purportedly anomalous fact is either not a fact or not anomalous."
- "First, ask whether the allegedly anomalous fact is a fact; if so, ask whether it is anomalous."
- https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8609310/

## 318. The "one in a quadrillion" claim tested whether two different elections were the same, and by that test nearly every election is "fraud"
**CONFIRMED.** The claim was an economist's report filed in a state's lawsuit at the Supreme Court.
- "we nearly always reject the null hypothesis (1,488 state–year combinations of 1,498)."
- "In fact, the test indicates simply that elections differ from each other, an unsurprising conclusion that tells us nothing about fraud."
- https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8609310/

## 319. Benford's law on precinct vote counts says nothing about fraud
**CONFIRMED.** A precinct count's first digit is set by how many people live in it.
- "distribution has nothing whatsoever to do with any kind of election fraud."
- "Its “success rate” either way is essentially equivalent to a toss of a coin"
- http://websites.umich.edu/~wmebane/inapB.pdf · https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/political-analysis/article/benfords-law-and-the-detection-of-election-fraud/3B1D64E822371C461AF3C61CE91AAF6D

## 320. What a real statistical red flag looked like: North Carolina's 9th District, 2018
**CONFIRMED.** The pattern compared like with like (a county against its neighbours, its own past and its own in-person vote), pointed to checkable records (request forms, unreturned ballots), and investigators then found the people, the payments and the forged forms (entries 166–171). In Bladen County, the party whose registrants cast only 19% of accepted mail ballots won 61% of them; a quarter of the district's requested mail ballots were not returned. The statistics started the inquiry; the records proved it.
- "had in Bladen County a mail-in absentee vote share inconsistent with his level of Election Day support."
- "rates of requested and returned mail-in absentee ballots in the period surrounding the 2018 midterm were anomalous in both Bladen and neighboring Robeson counties."
- "A quarter of the entire district's requested mail absentee ballots were not returned."
- https://electionlawblog.org/?p=107014 · https://www.oldnorthstatepolitics.com/2018/11/ncs-closest-congressional-contest-gets.html

## Case file: Pennsylvania's undated mail ballots
Tray: EXPLAINED as fraud or as deciding the race. PROVEN: county boards voted to count against a court order, and none of those ballots was counted.

## 321. Pennsylvania law requires the mail voter to "fill out, date and sign" the declaration on the return envelope
**CONFIRMED.** (the statute as quoted by the courts; the legislature's page did not render). The state's highest court read the requirement as mandatory in 2023.
- "Act 77 retained language from the Election Code which required that voters shall “fill out, date and sign” the declaration on return envelopes."
- "The Election Code commands absentee and mail-in electors to date the declaration that appears upon ballot return envelopes, and failure to comply with that command renders a ballot invalid as a matter of Pennsylvania law."
- https://www.supremecourt.gov/DocketPDF/25/25-962/396055/20260211152416315_Eakin%20Cert%20Petition%20Appendix.pdf · https://www.pacourts.us/assets/opinions/Supreme/out/J-85-2022mo%20-%20105327594212200610.pdf

## 322. The handwritten date is not used to judge timeliness: ballots are stamped on receipt, and the deadline is receipt by 8 p.m. on Election Day
**CONFIRMED.** Even the counties defending the rule concede it, and the 2024 appeals panel that upheld the rule under federal law said it serves little purpose. Pennsylvania moved to receipt-based timeliness in 1968.
- "By Pennsylvania law, a ballot is timely only if the county election board receives it before 8 p.m. on Election Day."
- "use the handwritten date to verify a mail ballot’s timeliness in any circumstance."
- "The date requirement, it turns out, serves little apparent purpose. It is not used to confirm timely receipt of the ballot or to determine when the voter completed it."
- "The date and time of receipt should be stamped on the ballot return envelope." (the Department of State's guidance, Sept 18, 2026)
- https://www.supremecourt.gov/DocketPDF/25/25-962/396055/20260211152416315_Eakin%20Cert%20Petition%20Appendix.pdf · https://www2.ca3.uscourts.gov/opinarch/233166p.pdf · https://www.pa.gov/content/dam/copapwp-pagov/en/dos/resources/voting-and-elections/directives-and-guidance/2026/2026-09-Guidance-Civil-mail-ballot-procedures.pdf

## 323. The date catches fraud: the Lancaster County ballot dated after the voter had died
**CONFIRMED.** That the case exists; how much the date mattered is contested in the record, and both readings are given. In 2022 a woman mailed her late mother's ballot, dated twelve days after the death. The defenders say the date was the only sign of fraud on the envelope, while conceding the ballot would not have been counted anyway; the appeals court says the voter database caught it, and the county's own clerk testified a missing date is no reason to suspect fraud. The one fraud case involved a ballot that had a date — a wrong one. Nothing in the record ties an undated envelope to fraud.
- "officials discovered that a ballot was fraudulent because the outer envelope had been dated twelve days after the putative elector had died."
- "the ballot was invalid and election officials would not have counted it in any event because the de- cedent passed away prior to election day." (the national party committee's petition)
- "Notably, the SURE system, and not the date on the return envelope, is what alerted the County to the fraud."
- "an outer envelope that is missing a hand -written date is no reason to suspect voter fraud." (the county's clerk, as the court records)
- https://www.pacourts.us/assets/opinions/Supreme/out/J-85-2022mo%20-%20105327594212200610.pdf · https://www.supremecourt.gov/DocketPDF/25/25-962/396055/20260211152416315_Eakin%20Cert%20Petition%20Appendix.pdf · https://www.supremecourt.gov/search.aspx?filename=/docket/docketfiles/html/public/25-962.html

## 324. A federal appeals court held the date immaterial under federal law in 2022; the U.S. Supreme Court then vacated that judgment
**CONFIRMED.** The Supreme Court did not rule on the merits: it vacated the judgment as moot because the race had been settled, so it is no longer precedent. Before that, counting 257 undated ballots in a 2021 Lehigh County judicial race flipped a 74-vote gap to a 5-vote result the other way.
- "are immaterial to a voter’s qualifications and eligibility under § 10101(a)(2)(B)."
- "Upon receipt, the LCBE timestamped the ballots, rendering whatever date was written on the ballot superfluous and meaningless."
- "Petition GRANTED. Judgment VACATED and case REMANDED with instructions to dismiss the case as moot."
- https://www2.ca3.uscourts.gov/opinarch/221499p.pdf · https://www.supremecourt.gov/search.aspx?filename=/docket/docketfiles/html/public/22-30.html · https://www.penncapital-star.com/election-2022/cohen-wins-lehigh-county-judicial-election-by-5-votes/

## 325. The state Supreme Court's Ball v. Chapman ruling on undated ballots came in Nov 2022
**CORRECTED.** (the dates). The order is dated Nov 1, 2022; the opinion was filed Feb 8, 2023 (not 2022). Cite it as *a 2022 order and a 2023 opinion*. The court split 3–3 on the federal question, so the holding rests on state law. A brief later attributed to the court a finding that the date is not used for eligibility, timeliness or fraud; that footnote reports the Acting Secretary's arguments, not a finding by the court.
- "are hereby ORDERED to refrain from counting any absentee and mail -in ballots received for the November 8, 2022 general election that are contained in undated or incorrectly dated outer envelopes."
- "The Court is evenly divided on the issue of whether failing to cou nt such ballots violates 52 U.S.C. §10101(a)(2)(B)."
- "The Acting Secretary rebuts several arguments for the date requirement’s functionality."
- https://www.pacourts.us/assets/opinions/Supreme/out/J-85-2022mo%20-%20105327594212200610.pdf · Pennsylvania Supreme Court, order of Nov 1, 2022 (J-85-2022)

## 326. A 2024 federal appeals ruling held the federal civil-rights statute does not reach rules about casting a ballot, leaving the date rule standing
**CONFIRMED.** (Third Circuit, Mar 27, 2024, 2–1). The dissent gave the comparator: 10,000 timely ballots uncounted in 2022.
- "we hold that the Materiality Provision only applies when the State is determining who may vote."
- "More than one million Pennsylvania voters mailed in their ballots in the November 2022 election . Of them, 10,000 timely-received ballots were not counted"
- https://www2.ca3.uscourts.gov/opinarch/233166p.pdf

## 327. 2024 in the state courts: the high court vacated and stayed lower-court rulings, then on Nov 18, 2024 ordered all counties not to count undated ballots
**CONFIRMED.** The order was 4–3; the dissenting statement objected to the procedure (King's Bench jurisdiction), not the merits.
- "The Commonwealth Court lacked subject matter jurisdiction to review the matter given the failure to name the county boards of elections of all 67 counties" (Sept 13, 2024)
- "DIRECT that all Respondents, including the Boards of Elections in Bucks County, Montgomery County, and Philadelphia County, SHALL COMPLY with the prior rulings of this Court"
- "SHALL NOT BE COUNTED for purposes of the election held on November 5, 2024."
- "have established grounds for this Court to exercise its sparingly invoked King’s Bench jurisdiction because there is an avenue for review" (the dissenting statement)
- https://www.pacourts.us/assets/opinions/Supreme/out/136MM2024pco%20-%20106152930288600382.pdf · Pennsylvania Supreme Court, order of Sept 13, 2024 (J-79-2024)

## 328. The state Supreme Court has since ruled on the date rule under the state constitution
**CORRECTED.** No ruling has issued. The court took the appeal (Baxter, 1 & 2 EAP 2025) in January 2025 and heard argument on Sept 10, 2025. It was still pending in the state's guidance of Sept 18, 2026 and in reporting of Oct 5, 2026; no decision was found through Oct 13. Re-check on publish day.
- "When Pennsylvania’s highest court gathered in Philadelphia on Sept. 10 to hear arguments on whether voters must write the date on their mail ballot return envelopes"
- "This issue remains a subject of continued litigation as illustrated by Eakin and a separate case pending before the Pennsylvania Supreme Court."
- https://www.votebeat.org/pennsylvania/2025/09/22/court-ruling-act-77-mail-ballot-date-requirement/ · https://www.pa.gov/content/dam/copapwp-pagov/en/dos/resources/voting-and-elections/directives-and-guidance/2026/2026-09-Guidance-Civil-mail-ballot-procedures.pdf · https://www.spotlightpa.org/news/2026/10/pennsylvania-general-election-2026-mail-ballot-how-to-request-fill-out-return-elections/

## 329. The rule for Nov 3, 2026: counties may not reject a mail ballot over a missing or wrong date
**CONFIRMED.** With a live caveat. A federal injunction (Eakin: district court Mar 31, 2025; Third Circuit Aug 26, 2025; rehearing denied Oct 14, 2025, six judges dissenting) bars rejection over the date. Petitions to the U.S. Supreme Court are pending; on June 29, 2026 the Court asked the Solicitor General for views, and that is the last docket entry. The state tells counties to keep these ballots separate and count them. Officials still recommend dating the envelope.
- "enjoined Pennsylvania’s county election boards from discarding ballots contained in return envelopes with missing or incorrect dates."
- "The Solicitor General is invited to file a brief in this case expressing the views of the United States."
- "Currently, counties cannot reject mail ballots for lacking a proper date."
- https://www.supremecourt.gov/DocketPDF/25/25-962/396055/20260211152416315_Eakin%20Cert%20Petition%20Appendix.pdf · https://www.supremecourt.gov/search.aspx?filename=/docket/docketfiles/html/public/25-962.html · https://www.supremecourt.gov/search.aspx?filename=/docket/docketfiles/html/public/25-967.html · https://www.votebeat.org/pennsylvania/2026/05/08/2026-primarymail-ballot-envelope-dating-explainer/ · https://www.votebeat.org/pennsylvania/2025/08/26/mail-ballot-dating-requirement-ruled-unconstitutional-federal-appeals-court/

## 330. "The courts keep flip-flopping"
**CONFIRMED.** (Votebeat's count, as of Sept 2025).
- "Since Act 77 was enacted, courts have made at least nine different rulings that have switched the way counties must deal with undated or misdated mail ballots, and the rules voters must follow."
- https://www.votebeat.org/pennsylvania/2025/09/22/court-ruling-act-77-mail-ballot-date-requirement/

## 331. 2022: more than 10,000 timely ballots were set aside statewide over the date
**CONFIRMED.** (10,657 in the court record), out of more than a million mail ballots.
- "county boards across the Commonwealth discarded 10,657 otherwise valid ballots in the 2022 general election solely because voters either forgot to date them or used an incorrect date."
- https://www.supremecourt.gov/DocketPDF/25/25-962/396055/20260211152416315_Eakin%20Cert%20Petition%20Appendix.pdf

## 332. 2024: about 4,500 ballots statewide
**APPROXIMATE.** The 4,500 is the court's arithmetic from Department of State percentages, not a count the Department published. The Department's "1% rejected" covers every reason (late arrival was the largest); only the 23% slice is date-related.
- "Of the returned mail ballots, 1% were rejected, which was a 57% decrease from the April primary, when 2.4% were rejected."
- "That still means that roughly 4,500 eligible Pennsylvania voters who made the effort to vote by mail in 2024 had their ballots discarded due to a missing or incorrect date."
- https://www.pa.gov/agencies/dos/newsroom/shapiro-administration-announces-57--decrease-in-mail-ballots-re · https://www.supremecourt.gov/DocketPDF/25/25-962/396055/20260211152416315_Eakin%20Cert%20Petition%20Appendix.pdf

## 333. Across the years: "tens of thousands"
**CONFIRMED.** As Votebeat's estimate; the state says no complete figure exists.
- "A spokesperson for the Pennsylvania Department of State said a complete figure for ballots rejected in the five years the law has been in effect is unavailable"
- https://www.votebeat.org/pennsylvania/2025/09/22/court-ruling-act-77-mail-ballot-date-requirement/

## 334. After the Nov 2024 election, the Bucks, Montgomery and Philadelphia boards voted to count undated and misdated ballots despite the state Supreme Court's rulings; Centre County did too
**CONFIRMED.** For the three counties (405, 501 and 607 ballots, about 1,500 in all); **corrected for Centre County**, which counted no undated ballots and judged three challenged envelopes correctly dated. Bucks voted against its own lawyers' advice.
- "At that meeting, the Board decided to count 405 undated and misdated mail ballots."
- "The Board voted 2-1 to count those ballots, with the majority taking the position that rejecting them would violate the voters’ rights under the Free and Equal Elections Clause"
- "There are roughly 1,500 or so ballots in play in the three counties, likely not enough to change the outcome of the U.S. Senate race that is spurring the litigation."
- "Yet, the Centre County Board of Elections did not count any undated ballots this election cycle."
- https://www.votebeat.org/pennsylvania/2024/11/18/ballot-dating-requirement-supreme-court-bucks-montgomery-philadelphia-counties/ · https://www.democracydocket.com/cases/pennsylvania-montgomery-county-undated-or-wrongly-dated-mail-in-ballots-challenge/ · https://www.spotlightpa.org/news/2024/11/undated-ballots-court-decision-bucks-centre-montgomery-philadelphia-county-recount/ · Pennsylvania Supreme Court, No. 136 MM 2024, the application of Nov 14, 2024 and the counties' answers of Nov 15, 2024

## 335. The court's response: two concurring statements rebuked local officials
**CONFIRMED.** One local outlet called it "7-0 on the merits"; the order itself is 4–3 and the dissent states no merits view. Air "4–3".
- "I write separately to disabuse local elections officials of the notion that they have the authority to ignore Election Code provisions that they believe are unconstitutional."
- "It is critical to the rule of law that individual counties and municipalities and their elected and appointed officials, like any other parties, obey orders of this Court."
- Pennsylvania Supreme Court, No. 136 MM 2024, concurring statements of Nov 18, 2024 · https://www.pacourts.us/assets/opinions/Supreme/out/136MM2024pco%20-%20106152930288600382.pdf

## 336. Were those ballots counted in the end? No
**CONFIRMED.** All three boards complied; the recount ended when the trailing candidate conceded.
- "The Board of Elections will of course comply with the order of the Court."
- "Ultimately, neither board of elections counted the ballots that didn't meet the date requirement."
- "In light of a recent Pennsylvania Supreme Court ruling prohibiting the counting of undated or wrongly dated mail-in ballots, the board did not count the contested ballots."
- https://levittownnow.com/2024/11/18/pa-supreme-court-rules-as-bucks-county-officials-get-blowback-from-ballot-decision/ · https://www.cbsnews.com/philadelphia/news/diane-ellis-marseglia-bucks-county-pennsylvania-voting/ · https://www.democracydocket.com/cases/pennsylvania-montgomery-county-undated-or-wrongly-dated-mail-in-ballots-challenge/ · https://penncapital-star.com/election-2024/pennsylvania-ends-u-s-senate-recount-after-casey-concession/

## 337. The comparator: the Senate race margin, about 15,000 votes
**APPROXIMATE.** (secondary; the official returns site would not render). Running margins quoted during the dispute moved from nearly 30,000 to just over 17,000. The roughly 1,500 disputed ballots were about a tenth of the final margin, and even all of 2024's date-rejected ballots statewide could not have changed the result. In Bucks, the undated mail ballots leaned toward the candidate who was behind, and the challenged provisional ballots the other way.
- "The race was decided by just"
- "These numbers make clear, as has been known by both parties, that these votes were never going to alter the outcome of this race,"
- https://penncapital-star.com/election-2024/how-mccormick-beat-casey-a-deep-dive-into-the-2024-pa-u-s-senate-race/ · https://levittownnow.com/2024/11/18/pa-supreme-court-rules-as-bucks-county-officials-get-blowback-from-ballot-decision/

## 338. A county commissioner said "precedent by a court doesn't matter anymore" about counting the undated ballots
**CORRECTED.** (the context) — the biggest script trap in this case file. The remark was real and went viral, but it was made on a motion about provisional ballots missing a signature, not on the undated mail-ballot vote, at a meeting a week after the election. She later apologised, and the state's chief election official called the comments mistaken and irresponsible.
- "I’m not going to second that, mostly because I think we all know that precedent by a court doesn’t matter anymore in this country and people violate laws any time they want,"
- "said the comment was taken out of context and that it was about provisional ballots and missing signatures. Still, she apologized at the meeting."
- "Last Thursday, when I spoke at the meeting that you're all here about, the passion in my heart got the best of me, and I apologize again for that,"
- https://penncapital-star.com/justice-the-courts/pennsylvania-casey-mccormick-supreme-court-orders-counties-not-to-count-undated-mail-ballots-senate-recount/ · https://www.cbsnews.com/philadelphia/news/diane-ellis-marseglia-bucks-county-pennsylvania-voting/

## 339. National framing on the "illegal ballots" side
**CONFIRMED.** (the headlines and posts exist). A cable network's headline said one party openly admitted counting illegal ballots, a national newspaper's editorial board said that party was thumbing its nose at the rule of law, a national party committee's chair posted that counties had "absolutely no choice" but to reject them, and a campaign manager posted that the commissioners would "go to jail". The clip led to threats against county officials.
- "Election officials in Bucks, Montgomery, Philadelphia, and other counties have absolutely no choice but to reject illegal ballots. We will hold them to it."
- "led to threats against the longtime elected official and other county officials, law enforcement sources said."
- https://www.votebeat.org/pennsylvania/2024/11/18/ballot-dating-requirement-supreme-court-bucks-montgomery-philadelphia-counties/ · https://whyy.org/articles/pennsylvania-high-court-united-states-senate-race-not-count-disputed-ballots/ · https://levittownnow.com/2024/11/18/pa-supreme-court-rules-as-bucks-county-officials-get-blowback-from-ballot-decision/

## 340. The "tossing legal votes" side
**CONFIRMED.** (the framing exists). The trailing campaign called the rule disenfranchisement; a civil-liberties lawyer called it a "disenfranchisement trap"; the Bucks board chair called it "a pretty stupid thing". (That campaign's side had argued the opposite in an earlier race; that is about candidates and stays out of the script.)
- "invalidating misdated and undated ballots disenfranchises Pennsylvania voters over a requirement that is irrelevant in determining a voters’ eligibility,"
- "we’re finally going to get to a place where this disenfranchisement trap goes away,"
- https://www.spotlightpa.org/news/2024/11/undated-ballots-court-decision-bucks-centre-montgomery-philadelphia-county-recount/ · https://www.votebeat.org/pennsylvania/2025/09/22/court-ruling-act-77-mail-ballot-date-requirement/ · https://thepublicsradio.org/npr/pennsylvanias-high-court-sides-with-republicans-on-misdated-mail-ballots/

## 341. How far the clip and the posts reached
**UNRESOLVED.** No view count, share count or poll was found. Say it "went viral", with no number.

## 342. Undated ballots are fraud, or come from ineligible or late voters
**CORRECTED.** Undated ballots are, by definition, received by the deadline (a late ballot is rejected for lateness, separately), and they come from voters whose mail-ballot applications the county had already approved against the registration database. Three courts and the county's own clerk say the date is not a fraud signal (entries 322–323). The record does not re-check every voter's eligibility, so say *timely ballots from voters the county had already approved for a mail ballot*, not *every one an eligible voter*.
- "county election boards compare the application to vote by mail against the voter’s"
- "timely-received ballots were not counted because they did not comply with the State law requirement that the voters’ declarations"
- https://www.supremecourt.gov/DocketPDF/25/25-962/396055/20260211152416315_Eakin%20Cert%20Petition%20Appendix.pdf · https://www2.ca3.uscourts.gov/opinarch/233166p.pdf

## Case file: noncitizen and other ineligible voting, and the roll-cleaning push
Tray: EXPLAINED for *millions voting illegally*. PROVEN in small numbers (Iowa 35 ballots, Michigan 16, North Carolina 41, Philadelphia 227 votes), and for flag lists that proved wrong (Texas 2019, Alabama 2024). OPEN — Virginia's ~1,600 removals: (a) how many were citizens is unpublished; (b) the removal list matched against citizenship records would settle it; (c) Virginia's Department of Elections holds it. OPEN — Arizona's 218,000 voters with no proof on file: (a) the final count of noncitizens among them; (b) the motor-vehicle and Secretary of State match results; (c) Arizona's Secretary of State and motor-vehicle division hold them.

## 343. The reading rule for every state number: registered is not voted, flagged is not confirmed
**CONFIRMED.** (the pattern holds in every primary saved). A national tracker states it as a finding; CEIR is an interested source, but its pattern matches every state record below.
- "Regardless of the state, the numbers announced after a proper investigation are invariably far smaller than any numbers announced before review."
- "Even the largest claims never allege numbers that amount to more than a few tenths of a percent of the number of eligible voters in a state."
- https://electioninnovation.org/research/claims-noncitizen-registration-voting-analysis-tracker/ · https://electioninnovation.org/research/noncitizen-analysis/

## 344. Iowa: 2,176 flagged, 277 confirmed noncitizens, 35 counted ballots in the 2024 general
**CONFIRMED.** (state primary). About 21 per million ballots (1 in about 48,000) — the highest rate found, and the honest high end. About 1,900 people on the list who were not confirmed noncitizens had to prove citizenship before their ballots counted. The release says 2,186 in one place and 2,176 in another; 2,176 is used.
- "That is approximately 12% of the 2,176 individuals."
- "35 noncitizens cast ballots that were ultimately counted in the 2024 General Election and 5 noncitizens attempted to cast ballots that were rejected."
- https://sos.iowa.gov/news-resources/iowa-secretary-states-audit-voter-registration-lists-finds-277-confirmed-noncitizens

## 345. Michigan, 2024: 15 noncitizen votes = 0.00028% of 5.7 million ballots
**CORRECTED.** (the count). The state's release counts 15 credible cases plus one from October 2024; together, 16 make up the 0.00028%, about 2.8 per million ballots. NPR's report of the review also gives 16.
- "15 credible cases identified – 0.00028% of more than 5.7 million total ballots cast in 2024 General Election; 13 referred to Attorney General for potential prosecution"
- "represent 0.00028% of the more than 5.7 million votes cast by Michiganders in the presidential election."
- "found 16 credible cases of noncitizen voting in Michigan in that election — out of 5.7 million votes cast."
- https://www.michigan.gov/sos/resources/news/2025/04/03/michigan-department-of-state-review-confirms-instances-of-noncitizen-voting-are-extremely-rare · https://www.kpbs.org/news/politics/2025/07/30/despite-grand-claims-a-new-report-shows-noncitizen-voting-hasnt-materialized

## 346. Michigan's outcomes, and the state auditor's 2026 review
**CONFIRMED.** (Votebeat, secondary). Of 38 referrals, six were charged, ten closed and 22 still open; three of the six are lawful permanent residents. The auditor found 403 registrations cancelled or rejected for citizenship reasons in 2022–March 2026, without saying whether any had voted.
- "In addition to the six who were charged, 10 of those cases have been closed without charges, and 22 remain under investigation."
- "403 registrations were canceled or rejected for reasons of citizenship during that period"
- https://www.votebeat.org/michigan/2026/08/13/attorney-general-dana-nessel-charges-5-noncitizen-voters-2024-election/ · https://www.votebeat.org/michigan/2026/09/02/auditor-general-report-voter-roll-noncitizens-2026-election/

## 347. Ohio, Aug 2024: 597 referred (138 voted, 459 registered only) of more than 8 million registered
**CONFIRMED.** (AP via WOSU).
- "The total compares to 148 noncitizen cases referred in 2022, 117 in 2021 and 354 in 2019. More than 8 million people are registered to vote in Ohio."
- "Only a handful of noncitizen-related cases are ever prosecuted."
- https://www.wosu.org/politics-government/2024-08-22/ohio-finds-597-noncitizens-who-voted-or-registered-recently-a-fraction-of-its-electorate

## 348. Ohio's earlier referrals: 521 noncitizen referrals, one noncitizen charged
**CONFIRMED.** As a newspaper's finding from a records request, not an official tally.
- "The vast majority of those incidents, 521 in all, are allegations of a noncitizen violating Ohio’s elections laws."
- "Importantly many of these incidents of suspected “voter fraud,” don’t involve anyone casting a ballot."
- https://ohiocapitaljournal.com/2023/09/27/ohio-sec-of-state-larose-flagged-more-than-520-cases-of-noncitizen-voter-fraud-only-one-was-legit/

## 349. Ohio, Oct 2025: 1,084 noncitizens referred to the Justice Department, 167 "appear to have" voted in a federal election since 2018
**CONFIRMED.** As the Secretary of State's referral; referrals, not findings of guilt.
- "This includes 167 noncitizens who appear to have also cast a ballot in a federal election, all of which occurred in the most recent four federal election cycles of 2018, 2020, 2022, and 2024."
- "These cases have encountered varying degrees of adjudication from Ohio’s 88 county prosecutors."
- https://electionlawblog.org/2025/secretary-larose-refers-more-than-1200-criminal-cases-to-doj/ · https://spectrumnews1.com/oh/columbus/news/2025/10/29/ohio-noncitizens-voting

## 350. North Carolina, 2016: 41 noncitizens voted, all lawfully present, out of nearly 4.8 million voters
**CONFIRMED.** (the State Board's audit, April 2017): about 8.5 per million. Even exact data matches turned up many citizens.
- "41 non-citizens with legal status (green card, etc.) cast ballots."
- "And 34 voters, tagged by the same audit, subsequently provided proof of citizenship, highlighting the fact that data matches alone are not sufficient to verify citizenship or to take action against the voters without follow-up investigations."
- https://s3.amazonaws.com/dl.ncsbe.gov/sboe/Post-Election%20Audit%20Report_2016%20General%20Election/Post-Election_Audit_Report.pdf

## 351. Utah: 14 of the 27 confirmed noncitizens ticked "No" on the citizenship question and were registered anyway
**CONFIRMED.** (state primary; extends entry 158). The applicants answered honestly; the failure was in processing, and most of the 27 were lawfully present. Four confirmed noncitizen votes in the 2024 general is about 2.7 per million ballots.
- "Citizenship Box* Marked No (Should not have been registered) 14"
- "SAVE Citizenship Indicator Lawful resident, employment authorized 19 No match in the database 6 DACA 2"
- https://ltgovernor.utah.gov/wp-content/uploads/CITIZENSHIP-FULL-SUMMARY-2.pdf

## 352. Philadelphia, 2006–2017: 220 noncitizens registered, 90 voted, 227 votes — most through motor-voter
**CONFIRMED.** (sworn committee testimony of the city commissioner at the time, Dec 12, 2017). The largest documented noncitizen-voting pool in this pass, and mostly a government-process failure; he warned it was a floor.
- "Of the 220 non‐citizen registrants, 90 (41%) voted in at least one election."
- "168 (76%) either initially registered to vote through PennDOT “Motor Voter,” or modified their voter registration record at some point through “Motor Voter.”"
- "Given that we only know of cases where non‐citizens have self‐reported their ineligibility, it is likely that many thousands remain registered to vote in Pennsylvania."
- https://pasenategop.com/stategovernment/wp-content/uploads/2017/12/schmidt.pdf

## 353. Pennsylvania statewide: up to 544 ballots in 93 million (2000–2017)
**APPROXIMATE.** The state did not have an exact count, and 544 was described as a figure that could drop: about one in 172,000.
- "State officials said last fall that noncitizens may have cast 544 ballots illegally out of more than 93 million ballots in 35 primary and general elections from 2000 through 2017 in Pennsylvania."
- "Pennsylvania election officials said Wednesday that there is no evidence to back up a claim that more than 100,000 noncitizen immigrants had been registered to vote in the state"
- https://whyy.org/articles/state-disputes-claim-100k-noncitizens-registered-vote · https://www.spotlightpa.org/news/2026/04/noncitizen-voter-fraud-pennsylvania-election-integrity-al-schdmidt-elections/ · https://electionlawblog.org/?p=100327

## 354. Texas 2019: the "95,000" list, about 25,000 of them citizens, rescinded in a settlement
**CONFIRMED.** The textbook case of a flag list announced as noncitizens: licence data did not reflect people who had since naturalised.
- "about 25,000 names on the list were eventually identified as naturalized citizens before the investigations were halted."
- "The state also agreed to pay $450,000 to the plaintiffs to cover legal costs and fees."
- https://www.governing.com/archive/tns-texas-voter-citizenship-settlement.html

## 355. Texas, 2021–2024: more than 6,500 "potential noncitizens" removed, about 1,930 with voting history
**CONFIRMED.** As the governor's claim; "potential" is the state's own word.
- "Of the over 6,500 potential noncitizens removed from the voter rolls, approximately 1,930 have a voter history."
- https://gov.texas.gov/news/post/governor-abbott-announces-over-1-million-ineligible-voters-removed-from-voter-rolls

## 356. Alabama 2024: 3,251 flagged; at least 2,074 of them eligible; a handful of noncitizens; the program was enjoined
**CONFIRMED.** The injunction rested on the federal 90-day quiet period, not on the merits of removing noncitizens.
- "has identified 3,251 individuals who are registered to vote in Alabama who have been issued noncitizen identification numbers by the Department of Homeland Security."
- "The secretary of state's office has so far acknowledged at least 2,074 of those individuals were eligible voters, according to court filings."
- https://www.sos.alabama.gov/newsroom/secretary-state-wes-allen-implements-process-remove-noncitizens-registered-vote-alabama · https://www.npr.org/2024/10/29/nx-s1-5169204/virginia-noncitizen-voter-purge · https://www.npr.org/2024/10/16/nx-s1-5154148/alabama-noncitizen-voter-purge-lawsuit

## 357. Virginia: 6,303 removed in 2022–24 without saying how many were noncitizens; about 1,600 removed Aug–Oct 2024, citizens among them
**CONFIRMED.** (removals; citizens among them). How many of the 1,600 were citizens is UNRESOLVED: no official or court count was found.
- "It is unknown at this point what the citizenship status is of all of the 1,600 voters. There is no database of U.S. citizens to check against."
- https://www.nbcnews.com/news/latino/virginia-governor-youngkin-voter-purge-noncitizens-errors-election-rcna167925 · https://www.npr.org/2024/10/29/nx-s1-5169204/virginia-noncitizen-voter-purge

## 358. The Supreme Court's Virginia order, Oct 30, 2024, was a stay, not a merits ruling
**CONFIRMED.** Three Justices would have denied it. The same 90-day question is now before the Court on the merits (entry 373).
- "The application for stay presented to The Chief Justice and by him referred to the Court is granted."
- https://www.supremecourt.gov/orders/courtorders/103024zr_f2ah.pdf

## 359. Arizona 2024: about 218,000 long-time voters had no proof of citizenship on file, from a motor-vehicle coding error
**CONFIRMED.** (the error and its size). How many were noncitizens is UNRESOLVED: the last figure found is two, as of Oct 3, 2024. 218,000 unverified is not 218,000 noncitizens.
- "All of the voters attested to their U.S. citizenship under penalty of perjury when they registered to vote, as required by federal law. Only two on the list have so far been identified as noncitizens."
- https://votebeat.org/arizona/2024/10/03/citizenship-proof-error-federal-only-voter-registration-drivers-license-records · https://www.democracydocket.com/news-alerts/audit-of-arizona-data-error-exposes-history-of-issues-in-tracking-voter-eligibility/

## 360. Oregon 2024–25: about 1,800 people registered by motor-vehicle error; 38 with any voting history, many of them citizens
**CONFIRMED.** (the state's DMV page and an external audit). Many were U.S. nationals from American Samoa — not citizens, but not people without legal status. Monthly checks have since found zero new errors.
- "Of the people mistakenly registered, only 38 had any kind of voting history and further investigation revealed that many were already citizens."
- "Although the number of potentially ineligible individuals being automatically registered to vote is likely too small to affect the outcome of an election, the existence of such cases poses a moderate reputational and compliance risk."
- https://www.oregon.gov/odot/DMV/Pages/News-Data-Processing-Issue.aspx · https://oregoncapitalchronicle.com/?p=12998 · https://www.salemreporter.com/?p=96082

## 361. Florida 2012, for the pattern: 180,000 suspected, then 2,600, then 198
**CONFIRMED.**
- "a revised list of 198 names of possible non-citizens was produced through the use of a federal Homeland Security Department citizenship database."
- https://whyy.org/articles/state-disputes-claim-100k-noncitizens-registered-vote

## 362. New Hampshire, 1998–2024: 8 possible noncitizen voters in about 8.3 million votes
**CONFIRMED.** As a federal judge's finding, citing an expert: about one per million.
- "During that time, only eight noncitizens may have cast ballots, she said."
- https://wtop.com/national/2026/05/federal-judge-says-new-hampshire-must-make-it-easier-to-prove-citizenship-when-registering-to-vote/

## 363. Who the noncitizen voters are: overwhelmingly lawful residents, not people without legal status
**CONFIRMED.** Across independent sources. As a description of the documented cases, "illegal" or "undocumented" people voting is corrected (Corrections table).
- "Among the 62 defendants whose immigration status could be determined, 49 were lawful permanent residents like her."
- "All cases involve documented non-citizens who were admitted into the country lawfully." (North Carolina)
- https://www.businessday.co.za/world/2026-07-19-news-analysis-trump-says-voting-by-foreigners-is-rampant-in-us-but-wheres-the-evidence/ · https://s3.amazonaws.com/dl.ncsbe.gov/sboe/Post-Election%20Audit%20Report_2016%20General%20Election/Post-Election_Audit_Report.pdf · https://www.npr.org/2025/07/30/nx-s1-5462836/noncitizen-voting-trump-ceir-review

## 364. A 2014 survey estimate that 6.4% of noncitizens voted in 2008
**CONFIRMED.** That the study exists and what it claims; its estimate was rebutted (entry 365), and its lead author says it does not support claims of millions voting illegally.
- "We find that some non-citizens participate in U.S. elections, and that this participation has been large enough to change meaningful election outcomes including Electoral College votes, and Congressional elections."
- "I published the following statement indicating that our analysis does not support his claim."
- https://digitalcommons.odu.edu/politicalscience_geography_pubs/11 · https://fs.wp.odu.edu/jrichman/2016/11/28/is-it-plausible-that-non-citizen-votes-account-for-the-entire-margin-of-trumps-popular-vote-loss-to-clinton/ · https://fs.wp.odu.edu/jrichman/2016/10/19/some-thoughts-on-non-citizen-voting/

## 365. The rebuttal: it was measurement error
**CONFIRMED.** (the survey's own principal investigators, 2015). A federal court later weighed the same evidence.
- "The results, we show, are completely accounted for by very low frequency measurement error; further, the likely percent of non-citizen voters in recent US elections is 0."
- https://cces.gov.harvard.edu/news/perils-cherry-picking-low-frequency-events-large-sample-surveys

## 366. Brennan Center, 2017: 42 jurisdictions, 23.5 million votes, about 30 suspected noncitizen votes referred
**CONFIRMED.** (an advocacy source; a survey of local officials, not an audit): about 1.3 per million. This settles, for this figure only, the 2017 Brennan critique left unresolved in entry 191.
- "Across 42 jurisdictions, election officials who oversaw the tabulation of 23.5 million votes in the 2016 general election referred only an estimated 30 incidents of suspected noncitizen voting for further investigation or prosecution."
- "local elections officials may not be aware of every incident of ineligible voting, and the tools at their disposal are imperfect"
- https://www.brennancenter.org/our-work/research-reports/noncitizen-voting-missing-millions

## 367. Federal and database counts: about a hundred Heritage entries in 25 years; 129 federal prosecutions under the noncitizen-voting statute since 1996
**APPROXIMATE.** (the Heritage tally is our own count from its data file: say *about a hundred in 25 years*); the Reuters count is confirmed as reported. None of the federal cases alleged a coordinated effort. Two other circulating counts (Cato's and the Bipartisan Policy Center's) were not located and are not aired.
- "Case records show that federal prosecutors have charged just 129 people under a federal “voting by aliens” statute created by Congress in 1996, Reuters found."
- "In none of the cases did prosecutors allege a co-ordinated effort to influence an election."
- https://www.businessday.co.za/world/2026-07-19-news-analysis-trump-says-voting-by-foreigners-is-rampant-in-us-but-wheres-the-evidence/ · https://electionfraud.heritage.org/

## 368. The federal crimes and penalties
**CONFIRMED.** (statute text). Voting by a noncitizen in a federal election is a misdemeanour; falsely claiming citizenship to register or vote in any election is a felony carrying up to five years.
- "Any person who violates this section shall be fined under this title, imprisoned not more than one year, or both."
- "Shall be fined under this title or imprisoned not more than five years, or both."
- https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/18/611 · https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/18/1015 · https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/18/911

## 369. A noncitizen who votes unlawfully is deportable and inadmissible
**CONFIRMED.** (statute text). Removal is initiated separately by immigration authorities.
- "Any alien who has voted in violation of any Federal, State, or local constitutional provision, statute, ordinance, or regulation is deportable."
- "Judges typically do not have the option of ordering deportations in criminal cases."
- https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/8/1227 · https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/8/1182 · https://www.businessday.co.za/world/2026-07-19-news-analysis-trump-says-voting-by-foreigners-is-rampant-in-us-but-wheres-the-evidence/

## 370. Where noncitizens may lawfully vote: some local elections only
**CONFIRMED.** (as of September 2026). D.C. and certain towns in California, Maryland and Vermont allow it in some local elections; New York City's law was struck down in 2025; eight states banned it in their constitutions in 2024. A clip of noncitizens voting in a local race may be lawful: check which election, which town, which ballot. The status of D.C.'s law in Congress and on remand is unresolved.
- "As of September 2026, the District of Columbia and certain municipalities in California, Maryland, and Vermont allowed noncitizens to vote in some or all local elections."
- "We hold that article II, § 1 of our Constitution limits voting to citizens, and affirm on that ground."
- https://ballotpedia.org/Laws_permitting_noncitizens_to_vote_in_the_United_States · https://nycourts.gov/reporter/3dseries/2025/2025_01668.htm

## 371. The March 2025 executive order requiring documentary proof of citizenship on the federal form, and the courts
**CONFIRMED.** Federal courts in D.C. and Massachusetts have blocked key provisions, the latter permanently in June 2026. Appeal status is unresolved.
- "the United States largely relies on self-attestation for citizenship"
- "Put simply, our Constitution does not allow the President to impose unilateral changes to federal election procedures,"
- https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2025/03/28/2025-05523/preserving-and-protecting-the-integrity-of-american-elections · https://www.clickondetroit.com/news/politics/2026/01/31/judge-blocks-additional-citizenship-provisions-in-latest-setback-to-trumps-election-executive-order/ · https://www.washingtonexaminer.com/?p=4623324

## 372. The SAVE America Act passed the House 218–213 on Feb 11, 2026, and stalled in the Senate
**CONFIRMED.** (the House vote and the Senate's failed cloture in March). Its status after August is APPROXIMATE: no enactment was found. Say *passed the House, has not become law*, and re-check on air day.
- "On passage Passed by the Yeas and Nays: 218 - 213 (Roll no. 69)."
- "Cloture on amendment SA 4732 not invoked in Senate by Yea-Nay Vote. 53 - 47."
- https://www.congress.gov/bill/119th-congress/senate-bill/1383/all-actions · https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/congress/trump-election-bill-save-america-act-50-senate-votes-democrats-block-rcna259351 · https://www.americandemocracyminute.org/wethepeople/2026/08/10/the-u-s-senate-will-leave-for-summer-recess-without-passing-the-save-america-act-but-the-restrictive-voting-bill-will-be-back-in-the-fall/

## 373. The Supreme Court will hear Arizona's proof-of-citizenship case on Dec 8, 2026
**CONFIRMED.** (docket). It asks whether federal law bars Arizona from requiring proof of citizenship on its state form, and whether it bars cancelling noncitizens' registrations within 90 days of a federal election.
- "Jun 29 2026 Petition GRANTED." … "Sep 04 2026 SET FOR ARGUMENT on Tuesday, December 8, 2026."
- https://www.supremecourt.gov/docket/docketfiles/html/public/25-1017.html

## 374. State proof-of-citizenship laws as of 2026
**CONFIRMED.** Twelve states have enacted such laws in some form; Alabama and Louisiana have not implemented theirs; Florida's takes effect Jan 1, 2027. New Hampshire is not enforcing documents-only registration for 2026: a federal court restored the sworn-affidavit route and the appeals court refused a stay. Two saved secondaries still say Utah found only one noncitizen; that was January's preliminary figure, and the final count is 27 (Corrections table).
- "Florida, Utah, and South Dakota are the 10th, 11th, and 12th states to enact laws requiring proof of citizenship to register to vote in at least some cases"
- "Defendants-Appellants have not shown that the equities and public interest justify a stay, and therefore the motion is denied." (First Circuit, Sept 4, 2026)
- https://news.ballotpedia.org/2026/04/01/florida-south-dakota-utah-enact-proof-of-citizenship-laws-for-voter-registration-2/ · https://www.brennancenter.org/our-work/research-reports/states-already-enacting-harmful-save-act-policies-requiring-proof · https://wtop.com/national/2026/05/federal-judge-says-new-hampshire-must-make-it-easier-to-prove-citizenship-when-registering-to-vote/

## 375. Kansas, 2013–2016: 31,089 applicants denied under a documentary-proof law, against at most 39 noncitizens registered in the 14 years before
**CONFIRMED.** (the district court's findings and the Tenth Circuit's opinion). More than 99% of the suspended applicants were citizens. Say *31,089 applicants were blocked*, not *31,089 citizens*.
- "These figures amount to 31,089 total applicants who were denied registration for failure to provide DPOC, 16,802 of whom applied through the DOV."
- "there are only 39 confirmed noncitizens who successfully registered to vote between 1999 and 2013 when the DPOC law became effective."
- U.S. District Court for the District of Kansas, No. 2:16-cv-02105, findings of June 18, 2018 · Tenth Circuit, 957 F.3d 1105 (Apr 29, 2020)

## 376. Blocked citizens against caught noncitizens: the ratio depends on the method
**APPROXIMATE.** (our arithmetic on sourced numbers). Blanket documents rules and stale-database purges burden hundreds of citizens per noncitizen found (Kansas about 1,100 to 1; Alabama about 200–500 to 1). Targeted, individually reviewed checks find noncitizens at far lower cost to citizens (Iowa about 7 to 1; North Carolina about 1 to 1 among those who answered).

## 377. People with felony convictions voting where barred
**CONFIRMED.** (North Carolina's scale; Florida's outcomes so far). North Carolina's 2016 audit had 441 open cases and notes felon voting is a strict-liability offence. Many of Florida's 2022 defendants said the state had issued them voter cards. Florida's final tally is UNRESOLVED.
- "441 open cases of voting by suspected active felons."
- "Most if not all had received a voter registration card from government officials ahead of the 2020 election."
- https://s3.amazonaws.com/dl.ncsbe.gov/sboe/Post-Election%20Audit%20Report_2016%20General%20Election/Post-Election_Audit_Report.pdf · https://www.npr.org/2022/12/21/1144265521/florida-voter-fraud-cases-prosecution-update · https://www.wlrn.org/state/2022-12-21/floridas-effort-to-charge-20-people-with-voter-fraud-has-hit-some-roadblocks

## 378. Double voting across states is rare and overcounted by name-and-birthdate matching
**CONFIRMED.** In the interstate Crosscheck data, an estimated 99.5% of same-name, same-birthdate vote pairs were two different people (Goel et al.).
- "In the consortium data, where we can measure this statistic more directly, we estimate this quantity to be 99.5%"
- https://doi.org/10.1017/S000305541900087X

## 379. "Dead people voted": a handful, usually relatives — and most "dead voter" counts are people who voted, then died
**CONFIRMED.** Georgia 2020: four, all by relatives (entry 143). North Carolina 2016: two. Michigan's auditor found 2,582 ballots since 2022 from voters who had died before Election Day, but 2,561 were early or absentee ballots from people who died within 40 days of the election.
- "Both involve family members voting in the place of a recently deceased loved one"
- "But 2,561 of those were early or absentee ballots cast by people who had died within 40 days of the election"
- https://s3.amazonaws.com/dl.ncsbe.gov/sboe/Post-Election%20Audit%20Report_2016%20General%20Election/Post-Election_Audit_Report.pdf · https://www.votebeat.org/michigan/2026/09/02/auditor-general-report-voter-roll-noncitizens-2026-election/


## § 5. Second top-up round (2026-10-13)

Six more hand passes ran the same day, from the host's further questions: the Postal Service mail-ballot rule and the cases the episode had not yet covered (Arizona 2022, the other side's "stolen" claims, 2024–26 viral cases); Wisconsin 2020 start to finish and whether one side's officials could change an outcome; the 2020 and 2024 vote totals, including a test of the host's own hypothesis with its method fixed in advance; the voting-machine company formerly called Dominion in 2025–26; and full case files for Nevada and Pennsylvania 2020. Each case file carries a tray on its first line, as in § 3. Where a hand pass gave no verdict word for an entry, the entry states what the record shows and carries the verdict for that statement.

## Case file: the Postal Service rule — "the post office won't deliver mail ballots to states that don't hand over voter rolls"
Tray: EXPLAINED. A real federal rule, but mis-described, not tied to the Justice Department's roll demands, and blocked for the Nov 3, 2026 election. Whether the Postal Service may do this for future elections is a pending legal question, not a gap in the evidence.

## 380. The head of the Postal Service said the Postal Service would not mail ballots for states that refuse to hand over their voter rolls
**CORRECTED.** The words "voter rolls" are the senator's and the headlines', not the answer's. Under oath on June 24, 2026, the Postmaster General answered a yes-or-no question about a **proposed** rule and a list of the people a state mails ballots to; he added that no new rule was yet in force. No social-media post or order making the statement was found.
- "Yes or no -- if a state refuses to turn their absentee voter list over to the federal government, will the Postal Service still mail their ballots under this proposed rule?" (the senator)
- "Under our proposed regulation, no. We would tell the state that we need the manifest,"
- "Remember, right now we only have a proposed rule, so there are no new rules,"
- "That list of information is less than what's included on a state's voter roll" (ABC)
- https://abcnews.com/Politics/postal-service-deliver-ballots-states-refuse-turn-voter/story?id=134185698 · https://www.pbs.org/newshour/politics/watch-under-proposed-rule-usps-wont-deliver-mail-ballots-to-states-that-dont-provide-voter-rolls-postmaster-general-says

## 381. The instrument that started it: Executive Order 14399 (signed Mar 31, 2026)
**CONFIRMED.** It directed the Postmaster General to propose a rule; it did not itself bind states. A separate list in the same order (a federal "State Citizenship List") goes from the government to the states. This is not the 2025 proof-of-citizenship order (entry 371).
- "the Postmaster General is hereby directed to initiate a proposed rulemaking pursuant to 39 U.S.C. 401 and other applicable authority within 60 days of the date of this order."
- "Proposed provisions specifying that the USPS shall not transmit mail-in or absentee ballots from any individual unless those individuals have been enrolled on a State-specific list"
- "neither requires nor forbids anything of anyone outside the Executive Branch." (the U.S. Supreme Court, Aug 24, 2026)
- https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2026/04/03/2026-06601/ensuring-citizenship-verification-and-integrity-in-federal-elections · https://www.supremecourt.gov/opinions/25pdf/26a124_hgci.pdf

## 382. The rule: proposed June 2, 2026, final Aug 26, 2026; non-compliant outgoing ballot mailings "will not be accepted"
**CONFIRMED.** A state must give the Postal Service the name, address and barcodes of each person it mails a ballot to. The rule does not cover ballots voters mail back, military and overseas ballots, primaries or state elections, and the Postal Service says it plays no role in eligibility.
- "Mailings that do not comply with 24.5.1 and 24.5.2 will not be accepted and will be returned to the authorized ballot mailer."
- "705.24.5 does not apply to Return Federal Ballot Mail."
- "the Postal Service will not play any role in determining voter eligibility, maintaining voter rolls, or counting ballots."
- https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2026/06/02/2026-10968/ballot-mail-for-federal-elections · https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2026/08/26/2026-17238/ballot-mail-for-federal-elections · https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2026/09/01/2026-17986/privacy-act-of-1974-system-of-records

## 383. The rule requires states' voter rolls
**CORRECTED.** It asks for a list of mail-ballot recipients and their barcodes, not the registration file, dates of birth or ID numbers the Justice Department's demands sought (entry 250). One real nuance: in the eight states and DC that mail every voter a ballot, that list covers nearly every active voter.
- "eight states and Washington, D.C., conduct elections by mailing all voters a ballot, meaning election officials would have to provide information on every voter."
- https://www.govexec.com/management/2026/06/postal-service-faces-backlash-over-voter-data-rule-tied-mail-ballot-delivery/414397/

## 384. The rule targets the states that refused the Justice Department
**CORRECTED.** It applies identically to every state that mails federal ballots, including those that gave the Justice Department their full files. Both programmes came out of the same push and were merged in headlines.
- "The rule adopted herein sets forth neutral, uniform, and non-discriminatory mailpiece design and data reporting standards that apply uniformly to all Federal Ballot Mail"
- https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2026/08/26/2026-17238/ballot-mail-for-federal-elections

## 385. The courts have blocked the rule for the Nov 3, 2026 election; no court has ruled on its merits for future elections
**CONFIRMED.** (to Oct 13, 2026). A district court in Massachusetts enjoined the order's key sections (June 25); the U.S. Supreme Court stayed that as premature (Aug 24); the same court then enjoined the rule's mandatory parts for elections through Nov 3 (Sept 4); the First Circuit refused a stay (Sept 10); a court in D.C. issued a second injunction (Sept 13); and the U.S. Supreme Court refused a stay (Sept 14), one Justice concurring that applying it in 2026 would be arbitrary and two dissenting. The merits are UNRESOLVED; the government's First Circuit brief is reportedly due Nov 2, 2026.
- "the Final Rule is likely a regulation of the manner of holding elections for members of Congress, which the Elections Clause of the Constitution assigns to the States and Congress, and not to an executive agency, such as the Postal Service, acting without congressional authorization." (First Circuit)
- "The Government is unlikely to succeed on the merits of its challenge to the District Court's preliminary injunction." (U.S. Supreme Court, Sept 14, 2026)
- "the Postal Service will not take actions to implement the rule specifically for the 2026 election unless and until the government obtains relief from those injunctions." (the final rule)
- U.S. District Court for the District of Massachusetts (June 25 and Sept 4, 2026); First Circuit (Sept 10, 2026); U.S. District Court for D.C. (Sept 13, 2026); U.S. Supreme Court, No. 26A305 (Sept 14, 2026) · https://www.supremecourt.gov/opinions/25pdf/26a124_hgci.pdf

## 386. What the Postal Service itself says, and the real 2026 mail risk
**CONFIRMED.** It continues to deliver ballot mail and has ordered extraordinary measures from Oct 24 through Election Day. The real risk for viewers is late postmarks on ballots mailed close to the deadline, which has nothing to do with voter rolls.
- "the Postal Service is continuing to accept and deliver ballot mail, including Federal Ballot Mail, from election officials and voters."
- "Extraordinary measures must begin Saturday, October 24, 2026, and will continue nationwide through Election Day (Tuesday, November 3, 2026)."
- "continues to recommend that voters mail back ballots at least a week before their state's deadline."
- https://about.usps.com/what/government-services/election-mail/pdf/2026-extraordinary-measures-memo.pdf · https://www.npr.org/2026/10/09/nx-s1-5984981/mail-in-ballot-usps-postmark

## 387. What state election officials said
**CONFIRMED.** Wisconsin's commission told clerks to send ballots as usual; states gave sworn ballot counts (California more than 23 million); 12 secretaries of state and 24 attorneys general opposed the rule, and 13 attorneys general supported it. Both sides of the state ledger exist.
- "The injunction prevents USPS from enforcing the rule, and all clerks can continue to send absentee ballots as usual using the post office."
- "an Alabama-led coalition of thirteen state attorneys general"
- https://elections.wi.gov/sites/default/files/documents/H.%20Clerk%20Communication%20and%20FAQ%20on%20USPS%20Rule.pdf · https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2026/09/01/2026-17986/privacy-act-of-1974-system-of-records

## 388. How many voters it would touch: about 3 in 10 voted by mail in 2024
**APPROXIMATE.** About 30% of more than 158 million ballots in 2024 were cast by mail (the EAC also says over 72% voted in person, so say "about 3 in 10"), roughly 40–47 million mail voters in a presidential year.
- "Over 72% of voters cast their votes in person at a voting location"
- https://www.eac.gov/news/2025/06/30/us-election-assistance-commission-releases-2024-election-administration-and-voting · https://www.everycrsreport.com/reports/IF13056.html

## Case file: Arizona 2022 — printer failures, "35,563 injected ballots", signatures, Cochise County
Tray: EXPLAINED for sabotage, *stolen* and unchecked signatures. PROVEN for Maricopa's flawed Election Day (equipment failure; 16,724 *Door 3* ballots, all counted), for the attorney general race's recount errors (about 500 votes, caught by the recount), and for the Cochise County certification refusal (official misconduct; one plea, one case pending).

## 389. The certified governor's margin was 17,117 votes
**CONFIRMED.** (the trial court's ruling; the state canvass pages refused the fetch).
- "The margin of victory as reported by the official canvass is 17,117 votes – beyond the scope of a statutorily required recount."
- https://statesunited.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/02/UA-Ruling-Lake-vs.-Hobbs.pdf

## 390. On Election Day, printers failed at about 70 of 223 vote centers, and 16,724 ballots went into "Door 3" to be counted centrally — all were counted
**CONFIRMED.** The county's 1% (of all voters) and the investigator's 6.7% (of Election Day ballots) use different denominators; the county's 43 sites and the investigator's ~70 troubleshooting visits are different counts. The Door 3 rate in earlier elections was 0.16–1.2%.
- "Runbeck and County technical workers travelled to approximately 70 vote centers to troubleshoot the reports of problems with the BOD printers."
- "approximately two-thirds of the vote centers did not experience printer issues"
- "All 16,724 of those ballots were counted at the Elections Department with observers present."
- "In the 2022 general election, 6.7 percent of the ballots were placed in Door 3 for secure transport to and tabulation at the MCTEC."
- https://www.maricopa.gov/DocumentCenter/View/82599/Maricopa-Election-Report-and-Attachments · https://elections.maricopa.gov/news-and-information/elections-news/board-of-supervisors-certifies-maricopa-county-election-results.html

## 391. An independent investigation by a retired state chief justice found the primary cause was equipment failure
**CONFIRMED.** (April 2023): printers could not reliably print the longer 20-inch ballots on heavy paper, and nothing showed the problem should have been anticipated. It is a finding of cause, not a ruling on intent.
- "in my judgment, the primary cause of the election day failures was equipment failure."
- "But nothing we learned in our interviews or document reviews gave any clear indication that the problems should have been anticipated."
- https://www.maricopa.gov/DocumentCenter/View/82599/Maricopa-Election-Report-and-Attachments · https://www.maricopa.gov/CivicAlerts.aspx?AID=2690

## 392. The losing candidate's election contest lost at trial, on appeal and in the state Supreme Court; a remand trial found signatures were checked
**CONFIRMED.** The trial court found no clear and convincing evidence of intent or effect, including from the plaintiff's own witnesses (Dec 24, 2022); the Court of Appeals affirmed; the state Supreme Court denied review on six of seven issues; and after a May 2023 remand trial, the court found the signature review complied with the law. The final petition was denied in November 2024.
- "Plaintiff's own witness testified before this Court that the BOD printer failures were largely the result of unforeseen mechanical failure."
- "Every single witness before the Court disclaimed any personal knowledge of such misconduct. The Court cannot accept speculation or conjecture in place of clear and convincing evidence."
- "There is clear and convincing evidence that the elections process for the November 8, 2022, General Election did comply with A.R.S. § 16- 550 and that there was no misconduct in the process"
- https://statesunited.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/02/UA-Ruling-Lake-vs.-Hobbs.pdf · https://statesunited.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/05/2023-03-22-Supreme-Court-.pdf · https://statesunited.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/05/kari-lake-election-lawsuit-cv2022-095403-5222023-re-ruling.pdf · https://azmirror.com/2024/11/07/az-supreme-court-rejects-kari-lakes-last-remaining-bid-to-overturn-her-2022-loss/

## 393. The "35,563 injected ballots" number was a gap between two exhibits, and the state Supreme Court sanctioned the claim that it was undisputed as false
**CONFIRMED.** (May 4, 2023: a $2,000 sanction).
- "The record does not reflect that 35,563 unaccounted ballots were added to the total count."
- "one exhibit that included an estimate of the number of early ballot packets based on the number of trays and a different exhibit showing a precise count."
- "is therefore unequivocally false."
- https://electionlawblog.org/wp-content/uploads/AZ-sct-order-20230504.pdf · https://statesunited.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/05/2023-03-22-Supreme-Court-.pdf

## 394. The county recorder's defamation suit ended in a default and then a confidential settlement
**CONFIRMED.** Say *the defendant stopped defending the case, which under Arizona law meant the claims stood, then settled on confidential terms* — not *admitted lying*.
