- "a defaulted party loses all rights to litigate the merits of the cause of action,"
- "an agreed upon settlement disposition has been reached as to all parties and claims."
- https://azmirror.com/2024/03/28/judge-lake-has-lost-all-rights-to-litigate-defamation-case/ · https://electionlawblog.org/2024/kari-lake-settles-election-defamation-case-brought-by-arizona-official/

## 395. The 2022 attorney general race: a recount cut the margin from 511 to 280 votes after one county found uncounted ballots
**CONFIRMED.** Human counting errors in Pinal County, found by the system's own recount; they moved votes toward the losing side and did not change the winner. This is what a real counting error looks like.
- "We conclude that human error was the cause of our election day miscounts,"
- "It seems clear that a stack of ballots from Precinct 68 was not scanned. Again, human error."
- https://votebeat.org/arizona/2022/12/29/23531652/arizona-recount-discrepancy-pinal-county

## 396. Cochise County: two supervisors refused to certify, one pleaded guilty in 2023 to a misdemeanour
**CORRECTED.** (the year). The plea was Oct 21, 2024 (Votebeat, the same day), not 2023 as a later report said; the fine was up to $500. The supervisors certified only after a court order, and the refusal rested on a false belief that the county's tabulators were not certified. The second supervisor's felony case is still pending, with no verdict or trial date found (UNRESOLVED). The one proven misconduct in Arizona 2022 was by officials acting on the stolen-election story.
- "refused to certify the election results based on the false belief that the electronic tabulators the county uses have not been certified under state and federal law."
- "she pleaded guilty to a separate misdemeanor charge of failure or refusal to perform duty by an election officer"
- "the Arizona Supreme Court on Wednesday refused to hear his appeal."
- https://azmirror.com/2022/12/01/cochise-county-certifies-election-results-after-ordered-to-by-a-judge/ · https://votebeat.org/arizona/2024/10/21/cochise-county-arizona-supervisor-takes-plea-deal-delaying-midterm-results · https://kjzz.org/content/1864268/cochise-county-supervisors-indicted-conspiracy-interference-2022-election · https://azmirror.com/?p=25636

## Case file: the other side's "stolen" claims — Florida 2000, Ohio 2004, 2016, Georgia 2018
Tray: Florida 2000, NOT CHECKABLE as to who *really* won (the review was done; the answer depends on a counting standard no one adopted), with a PROVEN thread of spoiled-ballot disenfranchisement. Ohio 2004, EXPLAINED, with a PROVEN small recount offence. 2016 *hacked tallies*, EXPLAINED, with PROVEN scanning of registration systems. Georgia 2018, EXPLAINED on the outcome, PROVEN for two administrative defects with no effect on the result shown.

## 397. Florida 2000: certified margin 537 votes; the media consortium's review found the answer depends on the counting standard, and named no winner
**CONFIRMED.** Full statewide recounts of all disputed ballots gave the other candidate a lead of 42 to 171 votes; the two partial recounts actually sought in court kept the certified winner ahead by 225 to 493. About 2,200 problem ballots were never delivered. The Civil Rights Commission found Black voters cast about 54% of 180,000 spoiled ballots, without finding a conspiracy.
- "With numbers that tiny, experts said it would be impossible to interpret the survey results as definitive."
- "the consortium said it is in no way trying to rewrite history or challenge the official result"
- "African Americans cast about 54 percent of the 180,000 spoiled ballots in Florida"
- https://www.fec.gov/resources/cms-content/documents/FederalElections2000_PresidentialGeneralElectionResultsbyState.pdf · https://www.heraldnet.com/?p=433291 · https://web.archive.org/web/20021212102029/http://www.cnn.com/SPECIALS/2001/florida.ballots/stories/main.html · https://www.usccr.gov/files/pubs/vote2000/report/exesum.htm

## 398. Ohio 2004: the losing party's own study and the exit pollsters found no evidence the machines or the count flipped the state
**CONFIRMED.** The certified margin was 118,601. A congressional minority staff report alleged serious irregularities but did not conclude the other candidate won; the exit pollsters attributed the poll gap to who answered the poll.
- "The statistical study of precinct-level data does not suggest the occurrence of widespread fraud that systematically misallocated votes from"
- "Exit polls do not support the allegations of fraud due to rigging of voting equipment."
- https://www.fec.gov/resources/cms-content/documents/federalelections2004.pdf · https://public.websites.umich.edu/~wmebane/Ohio2004/OhioReportCover2Cover.pdf · https://www.nbcnews.com/id/wbna6762638

## 399. Two Cuyahoga County workers were convicted over the 2004 recount in 2005 or 2006
**CORRECTED.** They were convicted in January 2007, of negligent misconduct (pre-selecting the "random" recount sample to avoid a full hand count), not deliberate rigging; later they took a probation plea. The special prosecutor did not claim it affected the result.
- "Two workers were convicted of a felony count of negligent misconduct and a misdemeanor of failing to perform duties."
- "By law, the sample should be chosen randomly."
- https://www.cleveland19.com/story/5984274/two-elections-workers-convicted-of-rigging-04-presidential-recount/

## 400. 2016: no evidence that any vote was changed or any voting machine manipulated
**CONFIRMED.** (Senate Intelligence Committee, 2019). Russian-affiliated actors got into registration systems in two states, not tallying systems. Recounts the losing side sought widened Wisconsin's margin by 131; federal judges in Michigan and Pennsylvania found no evidence of tampering.
- "The Committee has seen no evidence that any votes were changed or that any voting machines were manipulated."
- "However, none of these systems were involved in vote tallying."
- "Plaintiffs have not presented evidence of tampering or mistake."
- https://www.intelligence.senate.gov/sites/default/files/documents/Report_Volume1.pdf · https://urbanmilwaukee.com/pressrelease/wisconsin-recount-completed-ahead-of-schedule-with-relatively-small-changes-to-final-totals · https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/7323155/stein-v-cortes/

## 401. Georgia 2018 governor: the losing candidate said under oath that the winner "won under the rules that were in place"; a federal court found two administrative defects but ruled for the state
**CONFIRMED.** The margin was about 54,700. The court found the felon-matching process unconstitutional but caused by counties, and training materials wrong, yet rejected the claims on law and causation (Sept 30, 2022). The Secretary of State's office then overstated the ruling as denying "every allegation"; spin ran both ways.
- "won under the rules that were in place."
- "Although Georgia's election system is not perfect, the challenged practices violate neither the constitution nor the VRA."
- "Accordingly, the Court finds that the felon matching process violates the First and Fourteenth Amendments."
- https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CHRG-117shrg53977/pdf/CHRG-117shrg53977.pdf · U.S. District Court for the Northern District of Georgia, bench-trial ruling of Sept 30, 2022

## 402. Belief that an election was stolen tracks who lost, on both sides
**CONFIRMED.** (wordings differ, so not one trend): in 2000, 18% of the losing side's voters said the winner won fairly; in 2016, half of the losing candidate's voters believed Russia changed vote tallies — the thing the Senate committee found no evidence of — while six in ten of the winner's voters believed millions voted illegally; about 7 in 10 of the losing side's voters doubted the 2020 result in 2023. No credible 2004 poll was found.
- "believe there were millions of illegal votes cast on election day."
- https://news.gallup.com/poll/2188/black-americans-feel-cheated-election-2000.aspx · https://news.gallup.com/poll/4687/seven-americans-accept-bush-legitimate-president.aspx · https://yougov.com/en-us/articles/17286-belief-conspiracies-largely-depends-political-iden · https://www.cnn.com/2023/08/03/politics/cnn-poll-republicans-think-2020-election-illegitimate/index.html · https://bsos.umd.edu/featured-content/post-umd-poll-most-americans-are-unsupportive-key-trump-proposals

## Case file: real problems turned into fraud stories, 2024–2026
Tray: Lancaster County, EXPLAINED as shared, PROVEN underneath (forged registration forms caught before any reached the roll). California's 2026 primary count, EXPLAINED. Maryland's 2026 ballot mix-up, PROVEN (a vendor error, with every mail voter reissued). New Jersey, PROVEN (about 6,600 registered in error, fewer than 400 voted). Venice *vacant lot*, EXPLAINED.

## 403. Lancaster County, 2024: "2,600 fake ballots, all written by the same person" was about 2,500 suspect registration forms, from several paid canvassers, caught before anyone voted
**CONFIRMED.** The word "ballots" first came from the county's own media alert, which it then called a typo. The district attorney found 383 forms with fraud indicators; the motive was canvassing quotas, across all parties; the firm's director pleaded guilty in April 2026.
- "Approximately 2,500 voter registration forms have been contained and segregated."
- "There is no evidence in any of the counties that the forms under scrutiny resulted in any ineligible voters casting a ballot."
- "The actual issue in Lancaster was about 2,500 suspected fraudulent voter registration forms, not ballots or votes."
- https://www.politifact.com/factchecks/2024/oct/30/donald-trump/trumps-claim-that-2600-votes-were-caught-in-lancas/ · https://lancastercountypa.gov/CivicAlerts.aspx?aid=2213 · https://spotlightpa.org/news/2024/11/pennsylvania-election-fraudulent-voter-registration-investigations-update · https://votebeat.org/pennsylvania/2024/11/25/suspicious-voter-registration-forms-lancaster-monroe-lehigh-york-counties · https://www.attorneygeneral.gov/taking-action/ag-sunday-announces-charges-against-canvassers-pa-field-director-regarding-submissions-of-falsified-voter-registration-forms-in-multiple-counties/ · https://6abc.com/post/man-gets-month-jail-pennsylvania-voter-registration-quotas-2024-presidential-race/18981643/

## 404. California's June 2026 primary: a slow count and a late shift were the law working, not cheating
**CONFIRMED.** Mail ballots take longer to count, and one side's voters use them more. A separate federal charge over paying people (including homeless people) to register is real but is about registrations, not the count. Whether any charge was tied to the June count by October was not checked (UNRESOLVED).
- "Election results will change throughout the canvass period as vote-by-mail ballots, provisional ballots, and other ballots are processed."
- "The pace of counting ballots in the June 2 primary is in accordance with the state's election laws. Those policies apply to all voters regardless of party."
- https://www.politifact.com/factchecks/2026/jun/08/donald-trump/California-election-LA-governor-Spencer-Pratt/ · https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/california-woman-federally-charged-paying-individuals-including-homeless-people-las-skid-row

## 405. Maryland's 2026 primary: "500,000 fake ballots" was a vendor sending some voters the wrong party's ballot; every mail voter was reissued one
**CONFIRMED.** 500,000 is the number of mail ballots requested, not the number sent wrongly, which nobody knows; each return envelope is unique and only one ballot per voter counts.
- "It bears repeating that no fake OR illegal mail-in ballots were distributed"
- "Every return envelope/oath has a unique identifier."
- https://elections.maryland.gov/press_room/documents/2026/Mail_in_Ballot_Press_Statement.pdf

## 406. Venice, California, 2026: "26 registered voters at a vacant lot" was a former homeless shelter, which state law allows as a registration address
**CONFIRMED.** The video shows registrations, not votes. New Jersey's 2026 viral claim is the motor-vehicle error already logged (entry 265).
- "Under California law, eligible voters experiencing homelessness may register using a shelter as their physical location for voting."
- https://www.politifact.com/factchecks/2026/jan/21/benny-johnson/California-register-to-vote-toilet-no-fraud/

## Case file: Wisconsin 2020, start to finish
Tray: the overnight Milwaukee batch as fake ballots, EXPLAINED. The recount and the state Supreme Court, EXPLAINED. Care-facility guidance that broke the statute, PROVEN (one facility documented). Care-facility ballots statewide: OPEN — (a) no statewide count exists of 2020 care-facility absentee ballots requested and returned without deputies; (b) per-facility request and return records, 2016 against 2020, and the request forms would settle it; (c) municipal clerks and the state commission hold them, and the Legislative Audit Bureau could be directed to audit it (even a full count would show requests, not votes). Drop boxes, EXPLAINED (illegal 2022, legal 2024; no ballot shown ineligible). Witness-address fixes, PROVEN as a disputed practice. *Indefinitely confined* voters and the private grants' exact turnout effect, NOT CHECKABLE. The special counsel's probe as proof, EXPLAINED.

## 407. The night: one side led by more than 100,000 for much of the night; the lead flipped after 3:30 a.m. when Milwaukee reported its absentee ballots, then Green Bay and Kenosha
**CONFIRMED.** Those cities count absentee ballots centrally and could not start before Election Day (entry 278). The city batch of 169,541 absentee ballots went 84% to the winner, so about 26,000 went to the runner-up. Three different percentages describe "the batch" (82.5, 84, 85.7), each with a different denominator.
- "when the city of Milwaukee added its absentee ballots to its total."
- "Both cities have their own central count location, which processes all absentee ballots for the respective city."
- https://urbanmilwaukee.com/2020/11/04/absentee-ballots-give-biden-lead-in-wisconsin/ · https://www.wpr.org/politics/joe-biden-wins-wisconsin-thanks-late-breaking-lead-milwaukee-green-bay-kenosha · https://fox6now.com/news/ap-joe-biden-projected-winner-in-wisconsin

## 408. What moved the state was also the suburbs
**CONFIRMED.** (election-night figures; a conservative legal group and a group of conservative former officials say the same).
- "In general, the 2020 election in Wisconsin aligned with long-term statewide and national trends of Democratic gains in the cities and suburbs, coupled with increased Republican margins in rural areas."
- https://will-law.org/wp-content/uploads/2021/12/2021ElectionReviewSummary-web.pdf · https://www.wpr.org/politics/joe-biden-wins-wisconsin-thanks-late-breaking-lead-milwaukee-green-bay-kenosha

## 409. The recount the losing campaign paid for, in Milwaukee and Dane counties, moved the margin 87 votes toward the winner
**CONFIRMED.** Dane went 45 votes the other way and Milwaukee 132 toward the winner; the recount itself found the 386 uncounted ballots of entry 63.
- "We did not have yelling, screaming, shouting." (Milwaukee's election commission vice chair, the only Republican commissioner)
- https://www.wuwm.com/post/wisconsin-recount-complete-biden-gains-87-net-votes-milwaukee-dane-counties-conclude-recount

## 410. The "$3 million recount"
**CORRECTED.** $3.0 million was the losing campaign's prepayment; the actual 2020 cost was about $2.45 million ($1,719,200 in Milwaukee and $729,700 in Dane, per the Legislative Audit Bureau).
- "prepaid WEC $3.0 million."
- "the actual costs in 2020 in both counties were lower than the estimated costs"
- https://legis.wisconsin.gov/lab/media/rz1nj2dh/21-19full.pdf

## 411. The Wisconsin margin was "nearly 20,600"
**CORRECTED.** The certified margin after the recount is 20,682 (the state Supreme Court and the FEC); 20,600 was pre-recount rounding.
- https://www.wicourts.gov/sc/opinion/DisplayDocument.pdf?content=pdf&seqNo=315395

## 412. The state Supreme Court rejected the campaign's challenge to more than 220,000 Dane and Milwaukee ballots, 4–3 (Dec 14, 2020)
**CONFIRMED.** The majority called them technical issues, not improper electoral activity; the three dissenters objected to clerks' fixes of witness addresses and to the use of laches — about rules, not fraud. A federal judge appointed by the losing president also ruled against him.
- "The claims here are not of improper electoral activity. Rather, they are technical issues that arise in the administration of every election."
- "The President failed to point to even one vote cast in this election by an ineligible voter; yet he asks this court to disenfranchise over 220,000 voters."
- "This Court has allowed plaintiff the chance to make his case and he has lost on the merits."
- https://www.wicourts.gov/sc/opinion/DisplayDocument.pdf?content=pdf&seqNo=315395 · https://fox6now.com/news/will-report-no-widespread-wisconsin-election-fraud-but-illegalities

## 413. The Legislative Audit Bureau's equipment check: most differences were one vote or one ballot
**CONFIRMED.** 110 of 183 reporting units matched exactly; the largest difference was 21 ballots, from creases through the write-in fields.
- "Most of these differences involved one vote or one ballot."
- "The most-common reason for a difference was a paper jam"
- https://legis.wisconsin.gov/lab/media/rz1nj2dh/21-19full.pdf

## 414. A conservative legal group's review (December 2021): no widespread fraud; "in all likelihood" more eligible voters chose the winner — and also, unlawful-procedure votes exceeded the margin
**CONFIRMED.** Both halves in its own words. Its "unlawful" count is a legal-compliance count built mostly on drop boxes and other practices, not a count of ineligible voters (nearly 300). Its own hand count of about 20,000 ballots matched; it found no unexplained "ballot dump".
- "There was no evidence of widespread voter fraud."
- "This does not necessarily mean that" … "did not win a majority of the votes of those eligible to vote"
- "This review uncovered nearly 300 instances where ineligible voters cast ballots or attempted to cast ballots."
- "Put simply, there was no unexplained "ballot dump.""
- https://will-law.org/wp-content/uploads/2021/12/2021ElectionReviewSummary-web.pdf · https://wisconsinexaminer.com/2021/12/08/will-unveils-its-own-report-on-2020-elections-finds-no-fraud-but-attacks-election-administration/

## 415. The commission's guidance not to send special voting deputies into care facilities broke the statute; one prosecutor documented one facility
**CONFIRMED.** The commission's vote was unanimous, including its Republican appointees. A county district attorney (a Republican) declined charges for lack of jurisdiction but documented a facility where absentee returns rose from an average of 10 to 42 and some residents' ballots were requested and cast on their behalf; she did not suggest anyone tried to influence how residents voted. No statewide count exists (see the tray).
- "allowed the number of absentee ballots returned to the Village of Mt. Pleasant at this one care facility to go from an average of 10 before November of 2020, to 42 in November 2020."
- "I am in no way suggesting that the WEC or the Ridgewood Care Center staff in any way tried to influence how the residents at their facility voted."
- https://www.cbs58.com/news/racine-county-da-declines-to-issue-criminal-charges-against-state-election-commissioners · https://wisconsinexaminer.com/2021/11/03/racine-co-sheriff-refers-criminal-election-fraud/

## 416. Drop boxes: ruled illegal in 2022 and legal in 2024, by the same court
**CONFIRMED.** Drop boxes were used statewide in 2020 (528, in 66 of 72 counties by spring 2021). Each ruling was about lawful voters' return method, not fake ballots; the conservative group's correlation with about 20,000 votes came with its statement that it did not claim the voters were ineligible.
- "We hold the documents are invalid because ballot drop boxes are illegal under Wisconsin statutes." (2022)
- "we determine that the court's contrary conclusion in Teigen was unsound in principle, and as a consequence, we overrule it." (2024)
- "WILL does not claim that the voters who used drop boxes were ineligible voters or should have had their votes rejected."
- https://www.wicourts.gov/sc/opinion/DisplayDocument.pdf?content=pdf&seqNo=542617 · https://democracydocket.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/07/2024-07-05-Opinion.pdf

## 417. The private election grants: lawful at the time, lopsided, and since banned by voters
**CONFIRMED.** The five largest cities received $8.8 million of about $10 million in Wisconsin; three courts and the commission dismissed complaints; a model estimate of about 8,000 extra votes is not a precise or checkable figure; voters banned private election money in April 2024 with about 54%.
- "They were among roughly 200 communities in Wisconsin that received around $10 million as part of $350 million given out nationally."
- "We aren't saying that CTCL funding caused people to vote for"
- https://pbswisconsin.org/news-item/wisconsin-voters-approve-constitutional-amendments-on-election-funding-officials/ · https://wisconsinwatch.org/2024/04/wisconsin-voters-approve-ban-on-private-election-grants-as-biden-trump-win-primary/

## 418. "Indefinitely confined" voters: two clerks' wrong advice was corrected in March 2020; more than 265,000 voters used the status
**CONFIRMED.** No court or audit found how many were not in fact confined; the request to strike them as a class was rejected.
- "This court unanimously deemed that advice incorrect on March 31, 2020"
- https://www.wicourts.gov/sc/opinion/DisplayDocument.pdf?content=pdf&seqNo=315395

## 419. The Assembly's special counsel: no fraud finding; his own report said decertification would not change who is president; contempt; fired; a stipulated three-year suspension
**CONFIRMED.** The Republican Speaker who hired him fired him in August 2022; the cost passed $2 million. In 2025 he agreed to a three-year licence suspension, which a referee recommended; no final order was found (UNRESOLVED), so say "agreed to" and "recommended". This settles entry 71.
- "It would not, for example, change who the current President is,"
- "It's over then — can't go back. There is no mechanism, no provision, no anything that would have any practical legal effect," (his own lawyer)
- "found no evidence of widespread fraud during his inquiry,"
- https://www.wuwm.com/2022-03-01/gop-investigator-wisconsin-should-weigh-decertifying-vote · https://wisconsinexaminer.com/brief/gableman-attorney-says-2020-election-cant-be-decertified/ · https://pbswisconsin.org/news-item/vos-fires-gableman-and-ends-taxpayer-funded-2020-presidential-vote-probe/ · https://www.abajournal.com/news/article/former-wisconsin-supreme-court-justice-agrees-to-license-suspension-for-alleged-election-review-misconduct · https://wisconsinwatch.org/2025/09/supreme-court-should-suspend-michael-gablemans-law-license-for-3-years-referee-says/

## 420. The elections commission's responses
**CONFIRMED.**
- "The Special Counsel does not show his work."
- "Data allegedly showing cell phones that were tracked multiple times near absentee ballot drop boxes is, alone, not evidence of a crime,"
- https://urbanmilwaukee.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Special-Counsel-Report-Inaccuracies.pdf · https://wisconsinexaminer.com/brief/gableman-attorney-says-2020-election-cant-be-decertified/

## Case file: "so many articles can't all be fake"
Tray: EXPLAINED as proof of outcome-changing fraud. Most articles accurately reported that someone claimed or swore something; the claims then went where evidence is tested. Affidavits and expert reports, EXPLAINED.

## 421. When outlets asserted fraud as fact, several later settled or retracted
**CONFIRMED.** By example. No reliable count of 2020 "fraud" articles exists (UNRESOLVED); one allegation reported by fifty outlets is one allegation.
- "Two Georgia election workers have reached a settlement in their defamation lawsuit against a Missouri-based conservative website that falsely accused them of fraud in the 2020 presidential election"
- https://www.gpb.org/news/2024/10/12/georgia-election-workers-settle-defamation-lawsuit-against-conservative-website

## 422. What courts found when they weighed sworn affidavits
**CONFIRMED.** Nevada (Dec 4, 2020): affidavits are hearsay and largely self-serving, and the contesting side's own expert had no personal knowledge of fraud; Arizona federal court (Dec 9, 2020): "only impressive for their volume"; Arizona's Supreme Court: a duplication error rate that would net 103 votes; Pennsylvania's federal appeals court: "we have neither here", with the campaign's own lawyer saying it was not a fraud case. No total count of affidavits filed was found (UNRESOLVED). Most affiants were sincere; courts found what was sworn did not prove what was alleged.
- "Most of these declarations were self-serving statements of little or no evidentiary value."
- "Plaintiffs append over three hundred pages of attachments, which are only impressive for their volume."
- "Extrapolating this error rate to all 27,869 duplicate ballots in the county would result in a net increase of only 103 votes"
- "But calling an election unfair does not make it so. Charges require specific allegations and then proof. We have neither here."
- https://www.democracydocket.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/11/20-OC-00163-Order-Granting-Motion-to-Dismiss-Statement-of-Contest.pdf · https://www.supremecourt.gov/DocketPDF/20/20-858/164743/20201224112136771_Arizona%20Appendix.pdf · https://www2.ca3.uscourts.gov/opinarch/203371np.pdf

## 423. Expert reports were later rejected
**CONFIRMED.** By example: a Georgia consultant's claims of tens of thousands of illegal votes were called "fatally flawed and unreliable" by three political scientists; Antrim's "68% error rate" was settled by the hand count (entry 112); Nevada's court adopted the defense expert's conclusion.
- "riddled with errors and biases that render it invalid for purposes of drawing inferences about the quantities at issue."
- https://lostnotstolen.org/

## 424. Many of the officials who checked had every political reason to find the opposite
**CONFIRMED.** The Attorney General at the time; Georgia's Secretary of State and Governor; Michigan's Senate committee and its state board's vice chair; Maricopa's county board and Arizona's Attorney General; Nevada's Secretary of State (the state's only statewide Republican officeholder, later censured by her own party); and a conservative group of former officials and judges.
- "Like you, I am disappointed in the results of the 2020 Presidential Election."
- "In a unanimous vote, the Republican-controlled Maricopa County Board of Supervisors has officially certified the county's 2020 election canvass"
- "had their day in court and failed to produce substantive evidence to make their case."
- https://www.pbs.org/newshour/politics/barr-says-justice-department-found-no-evidence-of-fraud-that-would-change-election-outcome · https://www.clickondetroit.com/news/politics/2020/11/20/georgia-secretary-of-state-to-certify-election-for-biden/ · https://michiganadvance.com/2020/11/23/state-canvassers-certify-mich-election-results-during-lengthy-meeting/ · https://kjzz.org/content/1637542/republican-controlled-maricopa-county-board-supervisors-unanimously-certifies · https://ktvz.com/politics/2021/04/22/nevada-secretary-of-state-finds-no-evidentiary-support-for-gop-election-fraud-claims/

## 425. Why repeated claims feel true: the illusory truth effect is real, small per exposure, and acts on everyone
**CONFIRMED.** One prior exposure to a fake headline raised the share judging it accurate from 38 to 73 of 949 participants (d = 0.25), even when it was labelled as contested or went against the reader's politics, but not for entirely implausible statements. Knowing better did not protect people. The effect sizes of the two earlier studies were not obtained.
- "we show that even a single exposure increases subsequent perceptions of accuracy, both within the same session and after a week."
- "Contrary to prior suppositions, illusory truth effects occurred even when participants knew better."
- "For both true and false statements, there was a significant increase in the validity judgments for the repeated statements and no change in the validity judgments for the nonrepeated statements."
- https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6279465/ · https://hasherlab.psych.utoronto.ca/abstracts/hasher_etal_JVLVB_77.htm · Fazio et al., Journal of Experimental Psychology: General, 2015 (abstract)

## Case file: could one side's officials change the outcome?
Tray: EXPLAINED for 2020 (officials of one party flipping a statewide presidential count). PROVEN that insider manipulation exists at all — local, low-attention races, every case found caught (Clay County, Kentucky 2002–06; a Philadelphia judge of elections 2014–16; Cuyahoga's 2004 recount sample; North Carolina's 9th, 2018). Fraud nobody has detected, NOT CHECKABLE.

## 426. Who certified 2020 in the decisive states
**CONFIRMED.** Wisconsin's commission is bipartisan by statute; Georgia's Republican Secretary of State and Governor certified; Arizona's Democratic Secretary of State certified with the Republican Governor and Attorney General; Michigan's 2–2 board certified with the Republican vice chair voting yes; Nevada had a Republican Secretary of State. Pennsylvania's officials were not fetched in this pass.
- "One member appointed by the senate majority leader."
- "The chair of the commission rotates every two years between a Republican appointee and a Democratic appointee."
- https://docs.legis.wisconsin.gov/document/statutes/15.61 · https://www.wpr.org/justice/wisconsin-confirms-biden-presidential-win-following-recount-trump-lawsuit-imminent · https://azmirror.com/?p=8828

## 427. Poll workers are drawn from both major parties' lists and the public may observe (Wisconsin statute)
**CONFIRMED.**
- "Any member of the public may be present at any polling place, in the office of any municipal clerk"
- "One of these two electors must belong to a political party other than the county clerk's"
- https://docs.legis.wisconsin.gov/document/statutes/7.30 · https://docs.legis.wisconsin.gov/document/statutes/7.41 · https://legis.wisconsin.gov/lab/media/rz1nj2dh/21-19full.pdf

## 428. Paper: about 93% of 2020 votes had a paper record, and over 98% of jurisdictions used paper in 2024
**CONFIRMED.** (the Brennan Center, using Verified Voting data, for votes; the EAC for jurisdictions — votes are not jurisdictions).
- "Nationwide, we estimate that 93 percent of all votes cast during the 2020 election had a paper record"
- "Over 98% of election jurisdictions used voting equipment that has voters mark a paper ballot or produces an auditable paper record of voters' ballot preferences."
- https://www.brennancenter.org/our-work/analysis-opinion/paper-ballots-helped-secure-2020-election-what-will-2022-look · https://www.eac.gov/news/2025/06/30/us-election-assistance-commission-releases-2024-election-administration-and-voting

## 429. Post-election audits: 31 states and DC require them before certification
**CONFIRMED.** (NCSL, Sept 24, 2026).
- "Thirty-one states and Washington, D.C., require post-election audits to be completed before an election can be certified"
- https://www.ncsl.org/elections-and-campaigns/post-election-audits

## 430. More than 10,000 local jurisdictions run elections
**CONFIRMED.** (CISA, 2026). Wisconsin alone has 1,849 municipalities.
- "Over 10,000 local election jurisdictions manage the day-to-day administration of elections"
- https://www.cisa.gov/sites/default/files/2026-09/2026-CISA-Election-Security-Plan-FINAL-508c.pdf

## 431. The 2020 margins in the three closest states, and what flipping them would have done
**CONFIRMED.** (FEC): Georgia 11,779, Arizona 10,457, Wisconsin 20,682 (0.24%, 0.31% and 0.63% of ballots). Flipping all three would have produced a 269–269 tie, not a win (our arithmetic on the FEC's electoral table).
- "Total: 538 306 232"
- https://www.fec.gov/resources/cms-content/documents/2020presgeresults.pdf

## 432. When insiders did manipulate counts, they were caught: Clay County, Kentucky (2002–2006) and a Philadelphia judge of elections (2014–2016)
**CONFIRMED.** In Kentucky, officials on a nominally bipartisan board changed votes at the machines across three cycles and eight defendants were convicted after an FBI/IRS investigation; in Philadelphia, a judge of elections was paid to add votes in primaries and pleaded guilty. Bipartisan structure is a check, not a guarantee; how Cuyahoga's 2004 sample-rigging was first spotted was not found (UNRESOLVED).
- "According to testimony, the defendants' criminal actions controlled the outcome of the federal, local, and state primary and general elections in Clay County in 2002, 2004, and 2006."
- "All eight defendants were convicted for a racketeering and money laundering conspiracy."
- "pleaded guilty to conspiracy to deprive city voters of their civil rights by fraudulently stuffing ballot boxes in the 2014, 2015, and 2016 primary elections."
- https://www.fbi.gov/louisville/press-releases/2010/lo032510.htm · https://www.dailysignal.com/2020/05/21/election-judge-pleads-guilty-to-ballot-stuffing-for-democrats/

## 433. The pattern of real insider cases: local, low-attention races, and caught
**CONFIRMED.** Across the record: Clay County, Philadelphia's primaries, Cuyahoga's recount sample, North Carolina's 9th (entries 166–171), Harris County (entry 225) and Mesa County (entry 219) — each caught by recounts, audits, statistics, contests, signature checks or investigators. None was found at the scale of a statewide presidential count.

## 434. The symmetry: in 2024 the same systems, largely the same officials, certified the other side's wins in all six 2020-decisive states
**CONFIRMED.** Wisconsin's Democratic-appointed chair who certified 2020 certified the 2024 result the other way (margin 29,397); Arizona's Democratic Secretary of State and Governor certified the other side's 187,382-vote win; Michigan's board voted unanimously.
- "The bipartisan [Board of State Canvassers] today voted unanimously to certify Michigan's 2024 General Election results."
- https://pbswisconsin.org/news-item/wisconsin-elections-commission-chair-certifies-trumps-2024-victory-in-state/ · https://www.kawc.org/news/2024-11-25/arizona-gov-hobbs-secretary-of-state-fontes-others-certify-2024-election-results · https://michigan.gov/sos/resources/news/2024/11/22/michigan-board-of-state-canvassers-certifies-the-2024-general-election · https://www.fec.gov/resources/cms-content/documents/2024presgeresults.pdf

## Case file: 2020 against 2024 — "where did 80 million come from, and where did the votes go?"
Tray: EXPLAINED for *the 2020 total proves fraud*, *the 2024 drop proves 2020 was padded*, *20 million votes went missing*, *mail voting handed 2020 to one side*, and the host's own hypothesis (partly supported, below). NOT CHECKABLE for fake votes that leave no record. No case here meets the OPEN test: there is no specific, named gap in the official record. In our prose, *the 2020-winning side* also won 2012 and lost 2016 and 2024; *the 2024-winning side* won 2016 and lost 2012 and 2020.

## 435. The FEC's final 2020 totals: 81,283,501 and 74,223,975, of 158,429,631
**CONFIRMED.** (the FEC's Federal Elections 2020 report, October 2022). This revises the precision of entry 135, which used the FEC's January 2021 compilation (81,268,924 and 74,216,154): on the later report the gap to the 2020-winning side's 2024 total is 6,265,888, total votes fell 3,191,329, and the 2024-winning side rose 3,078,605. Why the FEC revised 2020 by 46,228 votes is not stated (UNRESOLVED; immaterial). The 2024 totals (75,017,613 and 77,302,580, of 155,238,302) are unchanged.
- "Total: 158,429,631"
- "Total: 82,644 1,144 19,625 210,381 155,238,302" (2024)
- https://www.fec.gov/resources/cms-content/documents/federalelections2020.pdf · https://www.fec.gov/resources/cms-content/documents/2020presgeresults.pdf · https://www.fec.gov/resources/cms-content/documents/2024presgeresults.pdf

## 436. The FEC has not yet published a "Federal Elections 2024" report
**CONFIRMED.** As of 2026-10-13: its index lists those reports through 2022, and for 2024 only the official results compilation of Jan 16, 2025.
- https://www.fec.gov/introduction-campaign-finance/election-results-and-voting-information/

## 437. The 2012 and 2016 totals
**CONFIRMED.** 2012: 65,915,795 and 60,933,504, of 129,085,410. 2016: 65,853,514 and 62,984,828, of 136,669,276.
- "Total: 136,669,276"
- "Total: 129,085,410"
- https://www.fec.gov/resources/cms-content/documents/federalelections2016.pdf · https://www.fec.gov/resources/cms-content/documents/federalelections2012.pdf

## 438. Both sides set records in 2020: the losing side's 74.2 million also beat every earlier total
**CONFIRMED.** The previous record was 69,498,516 (2008).
- "(Democrat) 69,498,516 52.93%"
- https://www.fec.gov/resources/cms-content/documents/federalelections2008.pdf

## 439. The 2024 winner gained 3,078,605 on his own 2020 total and stayed 3,980,921 below the 2020 winner's
**CONFIRMED.** (our arithmetic on the FEC figures in entry 435).

## 440. 2020 had the highest turnout in 120 years: 66.4% of the eligible population, the highest since 1900
**CONFIRMED.** With wording: not "highest ever" (1900 was 73.7%) and not "since 1908" (Pew's rounded wording); the Census survey's *highest of the 21st century* is a survey figure.
- "2020,0.6638,"
- "the highest voter turnout of the 21st century, with 66.8% of citizens 18 years and older voting"
- https://election.lab.ufl.edu/data-downloads/turnoutdata/US_VEP_Turnout_Rates_v1.4.csv · https://www.pewresearch.org/politics/2025/06/26/voter-turnout-2020-2024/ · https://www.census.gov/newsroom/press-releases/2021/2020-presidential-election-voting-and-registration-tables-now-available.html

## 441. Turnout and the eligible population, 2020 and 2024
**CONFIRMED.** Turnout was 66.38% in 2020 and 64.3% in 2024. The growth in the eligible population from 2020 to 2024 is APPROXIMATE (+1.7 to +3.2 million, depending on which vintage of the 2020 estimate is used); say *the eligible population barely grew*.
- https://election.lab.ufl.edu/data-downloads/turnoutdata/Turnout_2024G_v0.4.csv · https://election.lab.ufl.edu/data-downloads/turnoutdata/Turnout_1980_2022_v1.2.csv

## 442. Census survey, 2024: 73.6% of citizens of voting age registered, 65.3% voted, 29.0% by mail
**CONFIRMED.**
- "73.6% (or 174 million people) of the citizen voting-age population was registered to vote and 65.3% (or 154 million people) voted"
- https://www.census.gov/newsroom/press-releases/2025/2024-presidential-election-voting-registration-tables.html

## 443. 2020 had the largest increase in voters between two presidential elections on record
**CONFIRMED.** (survey-based; the FEC's presidential votes rose 21.76 million).
- "The 2020 election featured the largest increase in voters between two presidential elections on record with 17 million more people voting than in 2016."
- https://www.census.gov/library/stories/2021/04/record-high-turnout-in-2020-general-election.html

## 444. Mail voting: about a quarter of turnout in 2016, 43% in 2020, 30.3% in 2024
**CONFIRMED.** (the EAC's 2024 survey report): 33,140,081 mail ballots in 2016, 69,337,349 in 2020.
- "Mail voting comprised 30.3% of the turnout for the 2024 election, which is down from the high water mark for mail voting seen in 2020 (43%)"
- https://www.eac.gov/sites/default/files/2025-06/2024_EAVS_Report_508c.pdf

## 445. Universal mail voting raised turnout modestly and did not favour either party
**CONFIRMED.** (Thompson, Wu, Yoder and Hall, PNAS 2020; read via Europe PMC).
- "universal vote-by-mail does not appear to affect either party's share of turnout"
- "it increases turnout by roughly 2%, on average"
- https://www.pnas.org/doi/10.1073/pnas.2007249117

## 446. In 2020 itself, being just old enough for no-excuse absentee voting did not shift partisan turnout
**CONFIRMED.** (Yoder et al., Science Advances 2021).
- "we find that 65-year-olds voted at nearly the same rate as 64-year-olds, despite the fact that only 65-year-olds could vote absentee without an excuse."
- https://doi.org/10.1126/sciadv.abk1755

## 447. One side used mail far more in 2020: 58% of the winner's voters against 32% of the loser's
**CONFIRMED.** (Pew, validated voters). That is a choice of method; the causal studies above find it did not change the outcome.
- "By contrast, about a third of [2020 loser's] voters (32%) voted by mail."
- https://www.pewresearch.org/politics/2021/06/30/behind-bidens-2020-victory/

## 448. Where each side's 2020 gain came from (Pew, validated voters)
**CONFIRMED.** About nine in ten of each side's 2016 voters voted again; a quarter of 2020 voters had not voted in 2016; those who voted in neither 2016 nor 2018 split roughly evenly; 2016 third-party voters went 53–36 to the 2020 winner.
- "Overall, one-in-four 2020 voters (25%) had not voted in 2016."
- "The 19% of 2020 voters who did not vote in 2016 or 2018 split roughly evenly between the two candidates"
- https://www.pewresearch.org/wp-content/uploads/sites/20/2021/06/PP_2021.06.30_validated-voters_REPORT.pdf

## 449. Where each side's 2020 voters went in 2024 (Pew, validated voters)
**CONFIRMED.** 79% of the 2020 winner's voters voted for that side again, 15% stayed home and 6% voted another way; for the other side, 85%, 11% and 4%. Eligible 2024 voters who had skipped 2020 went 54–42 to the 2024 winner.
- "a larger share of [the 2024 winner's] voters (89%) than [the 2020 winner's] (85%) turned out."
- "Voters in 2024 who had not turned out in 2020, but were eligible, favored [the 2024 winner] by a margin of 54%-42%."
- https://www.pewresearch.org/politics/2025/06/26/behind-trumps-2024-victory-a-more-racially-and-ethnically-diverse-voter-coalition/

## 450. Pew's validated-voter numbers are weighted to the official result
**CONFIRMED.** (a method trap). They explain the official totals through real people's behaviour; they cannot independently prove those totals. 94% of panelists were matched to a voter file.
- "In addition, this survey is weighted to benchmarks for voter turnout and presidential vote preference."
- "Overall, 94% of panelists who we attempted to match were successfully matched to at least one of the three voter files."
- https://www.pewresearch.org/politics/2025/06/26/behind-trumps-2024-victory-a-more-racially-and-ethnically-diverse-voter-coalition/

## 451. In votes: about 12 million of the 2020 winner's voters stayed home in 2024 and about 5 million voted another way
**APPROXIMATE.** (Pew's rounded percentages multiplied by the FEC totals; about ±0.4 million per line). The other side lost about 8 million to staying home and 3 million to switching. Say *about 12 million*, never an exact figure.

## 452. Voter files: 126 million repeat voters, 30 million who voted in 2020 but not 2024, 26 million new voters
**CONFIRMED.** (Catalist). The 2020-to-2024 drop-offs had backed the 2020 winner at about 55.7%; 2016-to-2020 had 115 million repeat voters and more than 40 million new ones. Catalist counts movers as new in their new state, so its "new" is not Pew's.
- "in 2024, there were 126 million repeat voters who also cast ballots in 2020. There were 26 million new voters, who had not cast a ballot in 2020 but did cast a ballot in 2024. And finally, there were 30 million dropoff voters, who cast ballots in 2020 but not in 2024."
- https://catalist.us/whathappened2024/

## 453. First-time registrations: 40 million before 2020, 34 million before 2024
**CONFIRMED.** (Catalist).
- "40 million individuals registered for the first time"
- https://catalist.us/whathappened2024/

## 454. The 2024 drop was almost entirely outside the states where 2020 fraud was alleged
**CONFIRMED.** (FEC state rows; our sums). In the seven battlegrounds the 2020-winning side went from 15,426,689 to 15,379,253 (−0.3%) while total votes there rose 2.8%; in the other 43 states and DC it fell 6,218,452 (−9.4%). California alone fell 1.83 million. Padding removed would show up the other way round.
- "In non-battleground states, shown in black, turnout was about the same as 2020 in Republican areas, while it dropped by as much as 15% in Democratic areas."
- https://www.fec.gov/resources/cms-content/documents/federalelections2020.pdf · https://www.fec.gov/resources/cms-content/documents/2024presgeresults.pdf · https://catalist.us/whathappened2024/

## 455. The 2020 voters still exist as individual records in the 2024 voter files: about 156 million of about 159.7 million ballots
**APPROXIMATE.** (Catalist's 126 million plus 30 million, rounded, excluding Utah; vote histories run slightly below reported turnout because of deaths, moves and list maintenance).
- "individual-level vote history does not always equal 100% of reported turnout, so the vote totals are slightly lower than the full national turnout."
- https://catalist.us/whathappened2024/

## 456. The highest state turnout in 2020 was Minnesota, 79.2% of the eligible population
**CONFIRMED.** — nowhere near more votes than eligible people.
- https://election.lab.ufl.edu/data-downloads/turnoutdata/Turnout_1980_2022_v1.2.csv

## 457. The official totals 1980–1996
**CONFIRMED.** (the FEC's reports for 1992, 1996, 2000 and 2004; the Clerk of the House's statistics for 1980–88, read by eye from scans, with New York's cross-endorsement lines added to match the FEC's convention).
- "Total: 168 370 39,104,545 44,909,889 19,742,267 104,426,659" (1992)
- https://www.fec.gov/documents/1552/federalelections92.pdf · https://www.fec.gov/documents/1554/federalelections96.pdf · https://www.fec.gov/documents/1556/federalelections00.pdf · https://www.fec.gov/documents/1632/federalelections2004.pdf

## 458. What drove each side's change: shared factors against two-party swing
**CONFIRMED.** As an accounting identity on the official figures (our arithmetic: votes = eligible population × turnout × non-third-party share × two-party share). From 2016 to 2020, about 89% of the 2020-winning side's 15.4 million gain, and all of the other side's 11.2 million, came from things that lifted both (more eligible people, much higher turnout, the third-party vote coming home); only about 1.6 million was swing. From 2020 to 2024, about three-quarters of the 6.27 million drop was a two-party swing of about 4.65 million. It says how much each factor moved, not why.

## 459. A drop this size is not unusual
**CONFIRMED.** (FEC and Clerk totals, 1980–2024). The 6.27 million fall is the second largest since 1980 in raw votes; the largest was the 2024-winning side's −9.78 million in 1988→92. The same side lost 3.58 million in 2008→12 while re-electing a president. As a share of the eligible population (−2.81 points) three earlier falls were bigger.

## 460. The host's hypothesis was tested with a method fixed before the numbers were computed
**CONFIRMED.** The hypothesis, in the host's words with roles substituted: *one side's totals are at more consistent levels, but the other side's 2020 total was out of the normal range*. The method was written down and time-stamped before the calculation ran: official national totals for both major-party nominees 1980–2024; each change as an absolute number, a percentage, and a share of the eligible population; the "normal range" set by the 18 changes from 1980→84 to 2012→16 (min–max, and mean ± 2 standard deviations); and a fixed rule — supported only if the 2020-winning side's 2020 change alone falls outside on the eligible-population measure; not supported if both, neither, or the other side is equally unusual; partly if the measures disagree. It was rerun on the alternative 2020 eligible-population estimate.

## 461. Result: the 2020-winning side's 2020 jump was the largest since 1980 — narrowly
**CONFIRMED.** +15.4 million votes (the only change beyond mean + 2 standard deviations, at z = 2.1), and +5.04 points of the eligible population, above the previous maximum (the other side's +4.98 in 1980→84) by 0.06 points, about 150,000 votes' worth. Under the fixed rule this is the "supported" condition on that measure. The calls do not change on the alternative estimate.

## 462. That jump was a statistical outlier, and the other side's totals are the steady ones
**CORRECTED.** On the eligible-population measure the jump is well within the normal spread (z = 1.5), and as a percentage it ranks third, behind two gains by the other side (+28.7% in 1996→2000, +24.0% in 1980→84). Over 1980–2016 the 2024-winning side's series is the more volatile: its swings have about twice the spread (4.13 points against 1.95) and it holds the three biggest swings before 2020 in both directions. The measures disagree, so under the fixed rule the hypothesis is **partly supported**. 2020→2024 is ordinary for both sides on every measure.

## 463. AP VoteCast published a validated 2020→2024 vote-flow table
**UNRESOLVED.** None was found; not used.

## 464. Why the FEC revised the 2020 totals by 46,228 votes between January 2021 and October 2022
**UNRESOLVED.** Not stated in the documents read; immaterial to any call.

## Case file: the voting-machine company formerly called Dominion, 2025–2026
Tray: EXPLAINED for the ownership and name change, the lawsuits *forced* claim, *now it will be rigged the other way*, the 2025 executive order's machine provisions, *an order ended voting machines*, the federal grant conditions, CISA's 2026 report, the new federal standard, the July 2026 declassification as proof, foreign parts (as to outcomes), the federal access requests, Puerto Rico (as to votes changed), the defamation cases, Georgia's planned switch, Shasta County, Texas hand counts, the *deleted millions of votes* repost and Milwaukee's August 2026 delay. PROVEN for Puerto Rico's 2024 plebiscite programming error, two minor 2026 product faults, the Coffee County breach, the Mesa County clerk's offence, Cochise's plea and Newton County's 2026 programming error. OPEN, narrowly: whether Puerto Rico's 2024 machines were ever intruded upon at all — (a) the federal contractor found no evidence of exploitation but its work was stopped and its report is unpublished, and CISA could not examine the devices; (b) the forensic images and data captured from the system and the full vulnerability assessment of Sept 25, 2025 would settle it; (c) ODNI holds them, the contractor holds a working copy, a records request is reportedly pending, and Puerto Rico's election commission holds the paper ballots and its audit. Whether votes were changed is not open. NOT CHECKABLE: *a tampered foreign chip is hiding in some machine somewhere*.

## 465. Dominion Voting Systems was bought on Oct 9, 2025 and now operates as Liberty Vote
**CONFIRMED.** The federal certification record notes the purchase; the EAC approved a change order (a laptop with no wireless hardware) across the same systems in March 2026; the D.D.C. captions now name Liberty Vote. The sale price was not disclosed.
- "As of today, Dominion is gone,"
- "Dominion Voting Systems Corp (This company was purchased by Liberty Vote USA Inc. )"
- https://www.opb.org/article/2025/10/10/dominion-voting-systems-is-sold-and-is-now-liberty-vote/ · https://www.eac.gov/voting-equipment/democracy-suite-520 · https://www.eac.gov/sites/default/files/2026-03/LVU-101133_Approval_Letter_0.pdf

## 466. The buyer is a former local elections director who founded an electronic poll-book company
**CONFIRMED.** (by role).
- "I started my career in the Secretary of State's office before serving as Director of Elections for the City of St. Louis."
- https://libertyvote.com/op-ed-liberty-vote-a-new-chapter-in-american-election-technology/ · https://www.votebeat.org/2025/10/17/dominion-voting-systems-sold-to-liberty-vote-scott-leiendecker

## 467. For the 2026 machines, nothing changed but the name
**CONFIRMED.** No staff changes, contract terminations or product overhauls were expected; Georgia's contract runs to 2029. A new system has been submitted for federal testing but is not certified.
- "for now, the sale appears to be more of a rebranding than a revolution."
- https://www.votebeat.org/2025/10/17/dominion-voting-systems-sold-to-liberty-vote-scott-leiendecker · https://www.eac.gov/voting-equipment/frontier-10

## 468. A Republican bought Dominion and forced it to drop its lawsuits against conservatives
**CORRECTED.** Three cases ended by stipulated dismissal between Sept 19 and Oct 3, 2025, before the sale was announced, and the record does not say whether the sale had anything to do with it. But the new owner kept the pillow-company case going until June 2026 and is still pursuing another (a motion for default judgment, Aug 13, 2026; latest filing Oct 13, 2026).
- "making the deal contingent on dropping several remaining lawsuits against prominent conservatives and One America News Network (OANN)."
- "Aug 13, 2026 MOTION for Default Judgment by DOMINION VOTING SYSTEMS CORPORATION, LIBERTY VOTE HOLDINGS INC., LIBERTY VOTE USA INC."
- https://www.aol.com/news/republican-buys-dominion-voting-systems-190053825.html · U.S. District Court for D.C., No. 1:21-cv-02131 (docket)

## 469. A new owner changes nothing about how votes are checked: the same equipment, certifications and contracts, and the same audits
**CONFIRMED.** The fear on the other side — that the machines will now be rigged the other way — is documented, and the answer to it is the same as to 2020's "who owns it" claims: the paper audit.
- "sparked full-throated fears that the company was now an arm of the Republican Party"
- "We have to look at performance. So that's why we have voting system certification, that's why we have pre-election testing. That's why we have post-election audits, right?"

## 470. The March 2025 executive order banned QR-code ballots and required paper
**CORRECTED.** It directed the EAC to amend its standards and rescind prior certifications. A federal court in Washington state permanently enjoined those provisions as to Washington and Oregon (Jan 9, 2026), the government appealed, and no machine has been decertified. Whether the order's separate DHS security review has been issued is UNRESOLVED (entry 476).
- "§ 4(b)(i) directs the EAC to amend the guidelines to reach a particular outcome, in violation of the statutory process prescribed by Congress."
- "No voting system will be decertified by the EAC as part of VVSG migration."
- https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2025/03/28/2025-05523/preserving-and-protecting-the-integrity-of-american-elections · https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.wawd.346612/gov.uscourts.wawd.346612.126.0.pdf · https://www.eac.gov/election-officials/voluntary-voting-system-guidelines-vvsg-migration

## 471. The President pledged (August 2025) an executive order to get rid of voting machines
**CONFIRMED.** As a pledge; no such order followed. The only later election order (14399, March 2026) has no provision on machines, tabulation or the EAC. Grant conditions took its place (entry 472).
- "Paper ballots still have to be counted — either by hand (which is slow and error-prone) or by machine."
- https://www.votebeat.org/2025/08/18/fact-checking-trumps-latest-claims-about-mail-ballots-and-voting-machines/

## 472. The federal government now requires hand-marked paper ballots
**CORRECTED.** As a condition of voluntary grants (July 10, 2026), states must submit a plan to move away from barcode and QR-code counting and run a manual audit of at least 5% of ballots, with 20% of the award withheld until they comply. The federal fix is the paper audit the episode teaches. Whether any state has lost the 20% was not checked.
- "after each federal election, states must conduct a manual audit of at least 5% of all ballots cast."
- "FEMA's grant programs are voluntary."
- https://www.dhs.gov/news/2026/07/10/dhs-requires-states-adopt-common-sense-election-security-measures-receiving-federal

## 473. CISA's 2026 plan and report document real vulnerabilities and patching gaps, and recommend paper ballots and manual audits; neither says any vote was changed
**CONFIRMED.** "Without physical access" in the report means through the county's election-management computer or files, not over the internet (entry 54). The plan's "confirmed success in at least 20 states" is unsupported by the documents it cites, which say "at least two states" for 2016, and is about registration databases, not machines: UNRESOLVED and off air.
- "Election-related software, like all complex software, contains vulnerabilities that require timely remediation."
- "Conduct post-election manual audits of paper ballots to verify that voting systems function properly"
- https://www.cisa.gov/sites/default/files/2026-09/2026-CISA-Election-Security-Plan-FINAL-508c.pdf · https://www.cisa.gov/sites/default/files/2026-09/CISA-Election-Report-FINAL.pdf

**CONFIRMED.** (context, not a fraud claim).
- "CISA confirmed this week that it had cut $10 million in federal funding for activities under its cooperative agreement with the Center for Internet Security"

## 475. The company's machines are certified to the new federal standard — or no machine meets it
**CORRECTED.** Both ways. Four systems from three other makers are certified to the new standard (VVSG 2.0); none from Liberty Vote, whose new system is still under test. Its current system, certified to the older standard in February 2025, remains certified and usable.
- "Election officials may continue to use or procure voting systems that have been certified to VVSG 1.0 and 1.1"
- https://www.eac.gov/election-officials/voluntary-voting-system-guidelines-vvsg-migration · https://www.eac.gov/voting-equipment/democracy-suite-520

## 476. The Department of Homeland Security's security review of voting systems ordered in March 2025 has been delivered
**UNRESOLVED.** No document labelled as that report was found; CISA's July 2026 report may be it, but that is not established. Say *a federal security report is pending*.

## 477. Declassified documents (July 2026) prove the machines are exposed and that votes can be changed undetectably (a Venezuelan plot)
**CORRECTED.** The documents say less, and some say the opposite. The intelligence memo the White House quotes says registration systems, not tabulators, are the weak point, that tabulators would be difficult to manipulate at scale, and that audits and paper trails "very likely would uncover" it. The CIA note is about Venezuela's own elections and a different company, with heavy caveats. (OCR of scanned documents.)
- "Systems that tabulate, transmit, or display election results are vulnerable to localized exploitation but would be difficult to manipulate on a wide enough scale to alter the election outcome."
- "it did not definitively confirm that large-scale electronic fraud was successfully executed in specific Venezuelan elections"
- https://www.whitehouse.gov/election-integrity/ · https://www.whitehouse.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/Vulnerabilities-in-Electronic-Voting-and-Ballot-Counting-Systems.zip · https://www.nextgov.com/cybersecurity/2026/07/trump-stretches-declassified-china-intelligence-broader-2020-election-claims/414837/

## 478. Foreign-made parts in voting machines: real, but no tampered component has been named
**CONFIRMED.** Vendors told Congress in 2020 they depend on Chinese-made parts; a move to have Commerce ban the company's components collapsed for lack of evidence (per unnamed sources; denied by the White House); the federal teardown found one Intel chip packaged in China.
- "The team found one chip packaged in China by U.S. company Intel. Such chips are not generally considered a threat to U.S. national security."

**CONFIRMED.** No finding came out of either request.

**CONFIRMED.** At least 12 known vulnerabilities, 5 exploited in the lab, fixes already available; no Venezuelan-origin code. The contractor's work was stopped and its report is unpublished (see the tray).

- "found some known-about vulnerabilities, but no Venezuelan-origin code or evidence of hacking."

## 481. Puerto Rico 2024: a real Dominion programming error swapped two options on the status plebiscite in machine-counted precincts; the candidate races were confirmed
**CONFIRMED.** (the election commission's own resolution and audit). One non-binding ballot, two options swapped; the number of votes affected was not found (UNRESOLVED). A ballot-definition error, like Antrim 2020; no hack shown.

## 482. The vendor's own 2026 advisories: two real, minor product faults
**CONFIRMED.** A 3–5 ballot batch discrepancy if a scan is stopped mid-batch and accepted, caught by the standard batch sheet; and an English write-in label on translated ballots that does not affect the selections.
