## Case file: Nevada 2020 — "thousands of dead, illegal and out-of-state votes"
Tray: dead voters and double votes, PROVEN at small scale (10 dead-voter referrals and one conviction; 10 double-vote referrals, 6 found at the Clark canvass, one cross-state conviction). Movers list, fake and commercial addresses, the *70% rejected* signature claim, machines, the registrar's *discrepancies* and observers, EXPLAINED. Universal mail ballots, PROVEN for the detected cases and NOT CHECKABLE for undetected fraud. Five narrow OPEN items, each bounded far below the 33,596 margin — (1) the signature machine's false-accept rate: no blind human re-check of the roughly 30% of Clark envelopes it approved is on the record; a blind re-review of a random sample of those envelope signatures would settle it; Clark County holds them. (2) The 4,057 voters on a motor-vehicle list of people who once showed immigration papers: the state said it could not check their citizenship at the time of voting; their status as of Nov 3, 2020 would settle it; the Secretary of State (which has the list) and USCIS hold it. (3) Out-of-state voters who may also have voted elsewhere: the state declined to verify residency for about 16,700 and published no 2020 cross-state double-vote count; a cross-state participation match would settle it; the Secretary of State and ERIC hold it (residency intent itself is NOT CHECKABLE). (4) The voting-drive giveaway referral: referred to law enforcement in April 2021, no outcome found; the agency's decision would settle it; the Secretary of State's office can name the agency. (5) The other 2020 prosecution referrals: 9 referrals, 2 public outcomes; the Attorney General's office and county prosecutors hold the dispositions.

## 493. The court filing's numbers (Nov 17, 2020)
**CONFIRMED.** As filed: no less than 15,000 votes by people "known to have voted in other states", 1,000 residency, 500 dead (an OCR of the scan misreads 500 as 300), machine and giveaway claims, and a net total of 40,000 or more.
- "there were no less than 15,000 of these illegal and improper votes"
- "the reduction in votes for Defendant, however, is 40,000 or more than the reduction in votes for the Contestant"
- https://www.democracydocket.com/cases/nevada-election-law-contest/

## 494. The hearing's numbers (Dec 3, 2020) grew from the filing's
**CONFIRMED.** As said (a commercial transcript, not an official one): dead 500 became 1,506; other-state 15,000 became 19,218; 42,284 "double voters" appeared that the filing did not plead; about 4,000 noncitizens.
- "1,506 votes from dead voters. 19,218 votes from non-Nevadans. And particularly striking is 42,284 votes from double voters."
- "about 4,000 non-citizens voted this election"
- https://www.rev.com/transcripts/nevada-election-fraud-hearing-transcript-december-3

## 495. The list of 3,062 people who allegedly voted after moving away, sent to the U.S. Attorney General
**CONFIRMED.** More than 100 of the addresses were military post codes; the state's own elections office agreed people who moved within 30 days may vote in the old state; the list later shrank to 2,479.
- "Of those 3,062 addresses, more than 100 listed military APO and FPO zip codes; and at least 14 specific U.S. military bases were even listed as the current address for multiple voters"
- https://www.militarytimes.com/pay-benefits/2020/11/13/hundreds-of-military-absentee-ballots-are-likely-on-gops-list-of-alleged-criminal-voter-fraud-votes/ · https://www.politifact.com/factchecks/2020/nov/06/nevada-republican-party/fact-checking-republican-claim-illegal-votes-nevad/

## 496. Clark County's signature machine "rejected 70%" and was set below the maker's recommendation
**CONFIRMED.** As to the facts, corrected as to their meaning. The machine was set at 40 (the factory preset is 50, which the vendor does not recommend as such); about 30% of Clark mail ballots passed it with no human look, and the other 70% went to human review, not rejection. Clark's pre-cure mismatch rate (1.51%) matched Washoe's, which used no machine; the court credited the vendor's testimony that poor reference images cannot cause false accepts. No blind re-check of the machine-approved 30% entered the record (see the tray).
- "roughly 30 percent of signatures were verified by the Agilis machine, while roughly 70 percent were flagged for human verification."
- "a ballot envelope bearing a signature that was scored 40 or better by the Agilis machine was accepted without further review."
- "Clark County's pre-cure signature mismatch rate of 1.51 percent is nearly equivalent to that of Washoe County"
- https://www.democracydocket.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/11/20-OC-00163-Order-Granting-Motion-to-Dismiss-Statement-of-Contest.pdf

## 497. The registrar admitted "discrepancies that we cannot explain"
**CONFIRMED.** As a quote, corrected as to what it was about: 936 discrepancies county-wide, which mattered only to one county commission race decided by 10 votes (re-run); not the presidential race. Six people were found to have voted twice.
- "The relatively small number of discrepancies identified did not affect the outcome of any other race in Clark County, Nev., including the presidential race."
- "In the Las Vegas area, officials identified six people who voted twice"
- https://politifact.com/?p=283509 · https://www.wsls.com/news/politics/2020/11/16/trump-attacks-vegas-area-certification-of-biden-election-win/

## 498. Observers were kept at a distance and in set positions — but not denied access
**CONFIRMED.** As to distance and positions; corrected on "denied". The state later said it would fold the complaints into future rules.
- "The record does not support a finding that members of the public were denied the right to observe the processing and tabulation of mail ballots."
- https://www.democracydocket.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/11/20-OC-00163-Order-Granting-Motion-to-Dismiss-Statement-of-Contest.pdf

## 499. The 2020 law mailed a ballot to every active registered voter
**CONFIRMED.** (AB 4, August 2020), with a two-employee signature-review rule; the pre-election challenge failed for lack of standing.
- "shall prepare and distribute to each active registered voter in the county or city, as applicable, a sample ballot and a mail ballot for the election."
- https://www.leg.state.nv.us/Session/32nd2020Special/Bills/AB/AB4_EN.pdf

## 500. The state court's ruling (Dec 4, 2020), category by category
**CONFIRMED.** No credible evidence of fraud; the record supported none of the dead-voter, double-voting, other-state, residency, machine or late-ballot claims; the post-election paper audit in Clark found no discrepancies; the contestants' experts were "of little to no value". The phrase *fell far short* is a reporter's summary, not the judge's words.
- "the Court finds that there is no credible or reliable evidence that the 2020 General Election in Nevada was affected by fraud."
- "there were no discrepancies between the paper audit trail created by the printer and the data from the voting machine."
- https://www.democracydocket.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/11/20-OC-00163-Order-Granting-Motion-to-Dismiss-Statement-of-Contest.pdf

## 501. The Nevada Supreme Court affirmed unanimously (Dec 8, 2020)
**CONFIRMED.**
- "appellants have not pointed to any unsupported factual findings, and we have identified none."
- https://www.archives.gov/files/electoral-college/2020/ascertainment-nevada.pdf · https://www.democracydocket.com/cases/nevada-election-law-contest/

## 502. Every other 2020 Nevada suit failed
**CONFIRMED.** (as listed by the court): two pre-election challenges to the mail-ballot law (standing; relief denied), two challenges to the signature machine (denied; one dropped), three suits against the Clark registrar (dismissed), and the election contest.
- https://www.democracydocket.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/11/20-OC-00163-Order-Granting-Motion-to-Dismiss-Statement-of-Contest.pdf

## 503. The state party's "122,000 instances" of fraud
**CORRECTED.** The Secretary of State's investigation (a Republican, April 2021) found 3,963 unique reports; the larger number counted spreadsheet lines, and 3,950 of the reports were one complaint printed with different voter records. The report's verdict is not *fall short* (that is a reporter's phrase).
- "Although the NVGOP’s initial reports indicated that there were 122,918 records to support the accompanying allegations, the agency inventory identified 3,963 unique Elections Integrity Violation Reports (EIVRs)."
- "these concerns do not amount to evidentiary support for the contention that the 2020 general election was plagued by widespread voter fraud."
- https://www.nvsos.gov/home/showpublisheddocument/9428/637745479663670000 (read via the Internet Archive) · https://www.nvsos.gov/Home/Components/News/News/2931

## 504. What each pile became under the state's check
**CONFIRMED.** 1,506 "dead voters" → 10 dead per Vital Statistics, all referred. 42,284 "double votes" → 21,142 pairs counted twice, mostly different people; the state's own check referred 10, none on the party's list. 8,111 "non-existent addresses" → about 95% of a sample were real homes without street delivery. 81,971 Clark voters wrongly inactivated inside the federal blackout were reactivated — a real error, admitted. The 4,057 noncitizen-list voters and the out-of-state voters were not checked (see the tray). One concession, kept: mail voting "can make it easier for people to skirt durational residency requirements".
- "only 10 of the 1,506 records were identified as being deceased according to their records"
- "approximately 95% were quickly identified as being valid residences, although they do not receive street delivery from the USPS"
- "there is no federal data base that can be accessed for the purpose of evaluating bulk data relative to current immigration status"
- https://www.nvsos.gov/home/showpublisheddocument/9428/637745479663670000 (read via the Internet Archive)

## 505. The man who said on TV that his dead wife's vote had been stolen had cast it himself
**CONFIRMED.** (described by role only). He was charged in October 2021, pleaded guilty to a felony that became a misdemeanour a year later under the deal, and paid a $2,000 fine with no jail. His was the dead-voter declaration in the contestants' own court evidence. Whether a machine or a person approved that envelope is unknown; a signature check of any kind is weakest against a spouse.
- "on or between Oct. 26, 2020 and Oct. 30, 2020, the defendant voted twice"
- "How did the forged signature pass Clark County’s signature verification machine?" (the state party, November 2020)
- https://ag.nv.gov/News/PR/2021/Attorney_General_Ford_Announces_Voter_Fraud_Charges · https://www.8newsnow.com/investigators/las-vegas-man-who-voted-twice-using-dead-wifes-ballot-pleads-guilty-pays-2000-fine/amp · https://thenevadaindependent.com/article/clark-county-man-will-plead-guilty-to-voting-more-than-once-in-2020-election

## 506. A voter who tried to vote in both Nevada and Arizona was convicted
**CONFIRMED.** Sentenced to probation in January 2025 (the Attorney General's release says "Jan. 8, 2024", a typo).
- "pled guilty to attempting to vote both in Las Vegas and in Kingman, Arizona."
- https://ag.nv.gov/News/PR/2025/Attorney_General_Ford,_Secretary_of_State_Aguilar_Announce_Sentencing_of_Las_Vegas_Resident_for_Attempting_to_Vote_Twice_in_2020_Presidential_Election · https://www.fox5vegas.com/2025/01/13/las-vegas-man-sentenced-attempting-vote-twice-2020-election

## 507. AP's 93–98 potential Nevada cases, and what became of them
**CONFIRMED.** (AP, 2021). These are potential cases flagged by local officials, many stopped before counting. The state reported 9 prosecution referrals from 2020 as of April 2024; public outcomes for the rest are APPROXIMATE (two convictions found).
- "Between 93 and 98 potential cases were identified by election officials in six counties and Carson City."
- "was rejected and not counted, ensuring the voter was unable to vote twice."
- https://www.wxyz.com/news/read-michigan-other-state-details-of-aps-review-of-potential-voter-fraud-cases · https://thenevadaindependent.com/article/sos-most-election-fraud-reports-in-nevada-dont-warrant-criminal-prosecution

## 508. The certified margin was 33,596 votes, of about 1.4 million
**CONFIRMED.** (the Governor's certificate: 703,486 to 669,890). The exact statewide ballot total was not opened from a state document (APPROXIMATE: *about 1.4 million*).
- "Turnout among the state’s more than 1.8 million active registered voters was almost 77.3%"
- https://www.archives.gov/files/electoral-college/2020/ascertainment-nevada.pdf · https://lasvegassun.com/news/2020/nov/24/biden-win-over-trump-in-nevada-made-official-by-co/

## 509. The Clark canvass, and the 81,971 voters wrongly inactivated and then reactivated
**CONFIRMED.** 977,185 presidential ballots in Clark; 936 discrepancies; one county race re-run; the inactivation inside the federal blackout was reversed so those voters received the mailed ballots the law required.
- "the Elections Deputy coordinated with the Clark County Registrar of Voters to reactivate all 81,971 voters"
- https://www.nvsos.gov/home/showpublisheddocument/9428/637745479663670000 (read via the Internet Archive)

## 510. Recounts and audits
**CONFIRMED.** For the 2020 paper-trail audit (no discrepancies in Clark). Whether any presidential recount was demanded in 2020 is UNRESOLVED (a losing candidate may demand one at his own cost), and when Nevada's risk-limiting-audit law took effect was not checked; don't say Nevada ran one in 2020.
- https://nevada.public.law/statutes/nrs_293.403 · https://nevada.public.law/statutes/nrs_293.394

## Case file: Pennsylvania 2020 — the whole case file
Tray: EXPLAINED for Philadelphia's *8,021 dead voters*, *1.8 million sent, 2.5 million counted*, the three-day extension, Act 77, notice-and-cure, the observers claim, Erie *backdating*, Delaware County's USB drives, the Fulton County *audit* and the state Senate's investigation. PROVEN for 3 illegal ballots proven in court (a dead mother's, a son's, a two-state vote), Luzerne's 9 discarded ballots (a mistake), the Philadelphia warehouse theft (no vote effect shown), and Fulton County's breach of a court order (a legal wrong, not vote fraud). NOT CHECKABLE for *ballots mailed to people who never applied* and *188 dead voters in Montgomery County* (no names or lists produced) and for undetected fraud. OPEN, narrowly, as a records question: Delaware County's 2021 records-handling allegation — (a) the county said in November 2021 that the district attorney was investigating, and no public disposition was found; (b) the district attorney's closing or charging decision and the final court orders would settle it; (c) the district attorney's office, the county and state court dockets, and the county board of elections hold them. It is about documents produced in 2021, not the vote count.

## 511. The certified Pennsylvania margin was 80,555 votes
**CONFIRMED.** (the Governor's certificate: 3,458,229 to 3,377,674; the Third Circuit used the same figure).
- "the certified margin of victory of 80,555 votes."
- https://www.archives.gov/files/electoral-college/2020/ascertainment-pennsylvania.pdf · https://www2.ca3.uscourts.gov/opinarch/203371np.pdf

## 512. The losing campaign's federal suit was dismissed with prejudice (Nov 21, 2020)
**CONFIRMED.** It had already dropped five of its seven counts.
- "Plaintiffs ask this Court to disenfranchise almost seven million voters."
- "That has not happened. Instead, this Court has been presented with strained legal arguments without merit and speculative accusations, unpled in the operative complaint and unsupported by evidence."
- https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.pamd.127057/gov.uscourts.pamd.127057.202.0.pdf

## 513. The Third Circuit affirmed (Nov 27, 2020): the campaign's own lawyer said "this is not a fraud case"
**CONFIRMED.**
- "Charges require specific allegations and then proof. We have neither here."
- "[T]his is not a fraud case."
- "The Campaign never alleges that any ballot was fraudulent or cast by an illegal voter."
- https://www2.ca3.uscourts.gov/opinarch/203371np.pdf

## 514. The challenge to Act 77's no-excuse mail voting was dismissed as filed more than a year too late; the U.S. Supreme Court denied relief
**CONFIRMED.** Two state justices wanted the constitutional question heard, though not to undo 2020; it was heard later, and in August 2022 the state Supreme Court upheld Act 77.
- "Petitioners filed this facial challenge to the mail-in voting statutory provisions more than one year after the enactment of Act 77."
- "we find no constitutional violation, and so we reverse the order of the Commonwealth Court."
- https://www.pacourts.us/assets/opinions/Supreme/out/68%20MAP%202020%20Per%20Curiam%20Order.pdf · https://www.supremecourt.gov/search.aspx?filename=/docket/docketfiles/html/public/20-810.html · https://www.pacourts.us/assets/opinions/Supreme/out/J-18A-E-2022mo.pdf

## 515. The state Supreme Court created the three-day extension (Sept 17, 2020) and declined to require notice-and-cure
**CONFIRMED.**
- "we adopt the Secretary's informed recommendation of a three-day extension of the absentee and mail-in ballot received-by deadline"
- https://www.pacourts.us/assets/opinions/Supreme/out/J-96-2020mo%20-%20104548450113066639.pdf

## 516. Ballots with handwritten-envelope defects were counted; the campaign conceded there was no fraud
**CONFIRMED.** (Nov 23, 2020; an opinion announcing the judgment, so *the state's highest court ruled they counted*, not *a majority held*): 8,329 Philadelphia and 2,349 Allegheny ballots.
- "that they were cast and signed by qualified electors; and that there is no evidence of fraud"
- https://www.pacourts.us/assets/opinions/Supreme/out/J-118A-2020oajc%20-%20104614187120563725.pdf

## 517. "8,021 dead people voted in Philadelphia" (also "30,000")
**CORRECTED.** A New York appellate court found the statement false when it suspended the lawyer who made it, and Philadelphia's only Republican election commissioner testified under oath there was no evidence of even eight. His office ran Philadelphia; statewide, prosecutors proved one dead-relative ballot (entry 531).
- "The public records submitted on this motion unequivocally show that respondent's statement is false."
- "Not only was there not evidence of 8,000 dead voters voting in Pennsylvania, there wasn't evidence of 8."
- https://www.nycourts.gov/reporter/3dseries/2021/2021_04086.htm · https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CHRG-117hhrg48999/html/CHRG-117hhrg48999.htm

## 518. "Pennsylvania sent 1.8 million mail ballots but counted 2.5 million"
**CORRECTED.** 3.08 million were sent; the 1.8 million came from setting a primary-election number against the general election. The exact source series is APPROXIMATE.
- "This factual statement regarding the number of ballots mailed out before the election was simply untrue."
- "But once you actually go and look and compare apples to apples, there's no discrepancy at all."
- https://www.nycourts.gov/reporter/3dseries/2021/2021_04086.htm · https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CHRG-117hhrg48999/html/CHRG-117hhrg48999.htm

## 519. 9,428 ballots arrived in the three-day window, were set aside, and changed no federal race — even the dissenting Justices said so
**CONFIRMED.** That is 11.7% of the margin even if all went one way. Whether they were later added to the certified totals is UNRESOLVED (minor).
- "Counties reported only 9,428 ballots were received within the three-day extension at issue, and only 669 of those lacked a legible postmark."
- "we know that the State Supreme Court's decision had no effect on the outcome of any election for federal office in Pennsylvania."
- https://www.supremecourt.gov/DocketPDF/20/20-574/162064/20201130140745119_Scarnati%20Opp%20Cert%20v.FINAL.pdf · https://www.supremecourt.gov/opinions/20pdf/20-542_2c83.pdf

## 520. The three-day extension was a real legal fight
**CONFIRMED.** Three Justices dissented from the 2021 denial; in June 2026 the U.S. Supreme Court held that federal law does not forbid counting timely-mailed late arrivals — which does not decide whether a state court may extend a legislature's deadline, the 2020 Pennsylvania objection.
- "That decision to rewrite the rules seems to have affected too few ballots to change the outcome of any federal election."
- "The federal election-day statutes do not preempt Mississippi's law."
- https://www.supremecourt.gov/opinions/20pdf/20-542_2c83.pdf · https://www.supremecourt.gov/opinions/25pdf/24-1260_g3cn.pdf

## 521. "682,479 mail ballots were processed without observers"
**CORRECTED.** Observers were present at a distance the state Supreme Court held lawful (entry 81), and the claim was dropped from the suit. A real, small error: one poll watcher was wrongly kept out of one polling place.
- "No federal law requires poll watchers or specifies where they must live or how close they may stand when votes are counted."
- "Poll workers mistakenly believed his poll watcher certificate did not grant him access to that specific location."
- https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.pamd.127057/gov.uscourts.pamd.127057.1.0.pdf · https://www2.ca3.uscourts.gov/opinarch/203371np.pdf · https://witf.org/2023/08/02/an-annotated-guide-to-the-distortions-half-truths-and-outright-lies-about-the-2020-election-in-pennsylvania

## 522. Counties handled ballot "curing" unevenly
**CONFIRMED.** — real, lawful under the court's 2020 ruling, and small: about 6,500 notices in four counties.
- "Even if 10,000 voters got notice and cured their defective ballots"
- https://www2.ca3.uscourts.gov/opinarch/203371np.pdf

## 523. The Erie postal worker's "backdating" claim collapsed
**CONFIRMED.** He told federal agents he had not heard ballots discussed; no backdated ballots were found; in 2024, in a settlement, he said he was wrong.
- "Both the interview of the Erie County Election Supervisor and the physical examination of ballots produced no evidence of any backdated presidential election ballots at the Erie, PA Post Office."
- "As I have now learned, I was wrong."
- https://www.uspsoig.gov/sites/default/files/reports/2023-01/21INV00469.pdf · https://news.yahoo.com/project-veritas-settles-libel-suit-152850050.html

## 524. Delaware County's "47 missing USB drives / up to 120,000 votes"
**CONFIRMED.** That the claim failed: the drives were programming copies, all accounted for; fewer than 129,000 mail votes were cast for president in the county; the county was in the 2020 audit pilot; the challenge reached the state Supreme Court, which declined it.
- "All V-drives have been accounted for… The V-drives are copies of the information on the ballot scanners used in the precincts."
- "While the county has a pristine 15-0 record in the courtroom"
- https://witf.org/2023/08/02/an-annotated-guide-to-the-distortions-half-truths-and-outright-lies-about-the-2020-election-in-pennsylvania · https://whyy.org/articles/delaware-county-2020-election-fraud-lawsuits/

## 525. Mail ballots were sent to people who never applied
**UNRESOLVED.** Anecdotes in the original complaint, never put on the record with names or counts, dropped from the amended complaint; a related "reactivated registrations" claim was rated false.
- "Voters who've been removed from the voter roll cannot be "reactivated" unless they re-register."
- https://witf.org/2023/08/02/an-annotated-guide-to-the-distortions-half-truths-and-outright-lies-about-the-2020-election-in-pennsylvania

## 526. An outside "audit" of Fulton County, Pennsylvania found the machines compromised
**CORRECTED.** The outside firm itself called the county's election "well run" and found no fraud; the state decertified the machines because the outside inspection compromised them; the county then let another firm copy them in breach of a court order and was sanctioned (2023); it lost on the merits (Dec 2024, affirmed Dec 2025) and faces more than $1 million in court-ordered fees — fees, not fines (APPROXIMATE as a total).
- "that the [2020] election [in Fulton County] was well run, was conducted in a diligent and effective manner[,] and followed the directions of the Commonwealth."
- "Our close review makes clear that Fulton County willfully violated an order of this Court."
- https://www.pacourts.us/assets/opinions/Supreme/out/J-46-2022mo%20-%20105503198221664463.pdf · https://penncapital-star.com/campaigns-elections/five-years-after-trying-to-aid-trump-pennsylvanias-fulton-county-faces-steep-penalty/

## 527. The state Senate's 2021 "forensic investigation" ended with no report
**CONFIRMED.** (AP's account of the 2024 order): its voter-data subpoena became unenforceable when the session ended.
- "Senate Republicans never issued any sort of public report stemming from their investigation or subpoena"
- https://www.witf.org/2024/02/22/pennsylvanias-high-court-throws-out-gop-lawmakers-subpoena-in-2020-presidential-election-case/

## 528. The Gettysburg hearing (Nov 25, 2020) was mostly false, misleading or mistaken testimony, with some true accounts of real problems
**CONFIRMED.** (a line-by-line review). The true items were small: one poll watcher wrongly barred, uneven curing, and results updated in batches.
- "What followed was three hours of mostly false, misleading and mistaken testimony about the 2020 presidential election in Pennsylvania."
- "In still other cases, the complaints were true accounts of legitimate problems in the 2020 election"
- https://witf.org/2023/08/02/an-annotated-guide-to-the-distortions-half-truths-and-outright-lies-about-the-2020-election-in-pennsylvania

## 529. "188 dead people voted in Montgomery County"
**UNRESOLVED.** No list was ever produced; kept off air.
- https://witf.org/2023/08/02/an-annotated-guide-to-the-distortions-half-truths-and-outright-lies-about-the-2020-election-in-pennsylvania

## 530. Delaware County's 2021 records-handling allegation
**UNRESOLVED.** — the one OPEN item in this case file (see the tray). It is a chain-of-custody allegation about documents produced in 2021, not a vote-count claim.
- "allegations of impropriety regarding the 2020 general election are being investigated by the District Attorney's Office."
- https://patch.com/pennsylvania/haverford/da-investigating-2020-general-election-impropriety-allegations · https://whyy.org/articles/delaware-county-2020-election-fraud-lawsuits/

## 531. A Delaware County man cast a mail ballot in his dead mother's name; guilty plea, five years' probation
**CONFIRMED.** The district attorney called it the only known case of a dead person voting in the county; his request for his dead mother-in-law's ballot was flagged and stopped. A private person: his name, party and vote stay off air.
- "Officials said this is the only known case of a dead person voting in the county."
- https://www.fox29.com/news/delaware-county-man-sentenced-for-casting-dead-mothers-ballot

## 532. A Chester County man voted twice, once in his son's name; guilty plea
**CONFIRMED.**
- "a Pennsylvania man who went twice to the polls -- voting once on his own behalf and once for his son."
- https://www.pbs.org/newshour/politics/ap-review-finds-far-too-little-vote-fraud-to-tip-2020-election-to-trump

## 533. Statewide, AP counted 26 possible 2020 fraud cases in 11 of 67 counties
**APPROXIMATE.** — possible cases, not a census of convictions.
- "Election officials in 11 of the state's 67 counties have identified 26 possible cases of voter fraud"
- https://www.pbs.org/newshour/politics/ap-review-finds-far-too-little-vote-fraud-to-tip-2020-election-to-trump

## 534. A man who had moved to Florida voted by mail in Pennsylvania and again in person in Florida; convicted in 2026
**CONFIRMED.** (federal jury, March 2026; probation, July 2026). A pardon claim was rejected before trial. Double voting across states gets caught, slowly.
- "He then went back to Florida and voted at a polling place there on or around Election Day."
- https://www.nbcphiladelphia.com/news/local/bethlehem-man-receives-sentence-for-election-fraud-charges-prosecutors-say/4433875/

## 535. Proven illegal 2020 ballots in Pennsylvania, tallied against the margin
**CONFIRMED.** As a floor, not a census: 3 proven in court in this record (entries 531, 532, 534), at least 2 attempts caught before a ballot issued, 26 possible cases (AP) — 0.004% to 0.03% of the 80,555 margin (our arithmetic).

## 536. Philadelphia, September 2020: a laptop and USB sticks were stolen from the voting-machine warehouse
**CONFIRMED.** As a real theft with no vote effect shown (secondary source only).
- "The theft happened in Philadelphia"
- https://witf.org/2023/08/02/an-annotated-guide-to-the-distortions-half-truths-and-outright-lies-about-the-2020-election-in-pennsylvania

## 537. Luzerne County, September 2020: 9 ballots were discarded by a county worker by mistake; no charges
**CONFIRMED.** (extends entry 240): seven military absentee ballots discarded and two envelopes mishandled.
- "The FBI and the DA's Office determined that seven military absentee ballots were discarded"
- "appeared to have discarded the ballots by mistake, and would likely not be criminally charged."
- https://oig.justice.gov/sites/default/files/reports/24-082.pdf

## 538. The 2020 audits: a 63-county pilot matched within a fraction of a point, plus the required 2% sample
**CONFIRMED.** With its limits: four counties did not take part, and a risk-limiting audit checks the winner, not every precinct. The *six discrepancies in about 37,000 ballots* figure (entry 119) is not a 2020 figure found here and stays off air.
- "confirms the election's accuracy."
- "That's different from showing that any equipment worked properly, that the count was accurate"
- https://www.spotlightpa.org/news/2021/03/pa-election-biden-trump-risk-limiting-audit-limitations/

## 539. Every Pennsylvania lawsuit over 2020 in this record lost, including at the U.S. Supreme Court
**CONFIRMED.** For the cases listed (entries 512–516, 519–520, 524; and the Texas suit, entry 194). Don't say *sixty lawsuits* without the national source (entry 195).

## Corrections applied to source
<!-- One row per CORRECTED claim. "Caught by" and "came from" are how the research method gets
     measured: without them nobody can say whether the engine, the hand pass or the script review is
     doing the catching. Use these words, and add a few words after a dash if it helps.
     Caught by:  engine (a research-engine verifier refuted or flagged it) · hand pass · peg re-hunt ·
                 scripting (while writing or checking the script) · review (Lucas watching a cut) ·
                 after publish
     Came from:  engine (a finder's claim or the synthesis) · scout brief · prior episode · peg hunt ·
                 draft line (the script's own wording) · source (the source itself changed or erred)
     ⛔ Keep the correction text in the FIRST column: the site's verdict reader skips column one, so
        this table can never be mistaken for a claim table. -->
| correction | caught by | came from |
|---|---|---|
| FBI seized the ballots Jan 29, 2026 → Jan 28, 2026: warrant signed 2:31 p.m., executed 3:00 p.m. (entry 35) | hand pass | source — CBS, relayed by the peg hunt |
| "656 boxes" → 653 boxes, by the FBI's own inventory; the order's "over 600" and the county's "over 650" both agree (entry 36) | hand pass | source — a press estimate, relayed by the peg hunt and the engine |

## Fairness audit
- **The case list leans where the record leans, and does not pad either side.** Most of the famous clips are EXPLAINED: ordinary procedure misread (State Farm Arena's carriers, the rescan, the Detroit van, families at a drop box, a poll worker initialling ballots), or fabricated outright (Bucks County, the "Haitian voters", the Arizona interview). The episode says so plainly, and it does not trim that list to look balanced.
- **"Something real underneath" is stated as plainly as the debunks.** Fulton 2020 was badly run: about 200 ballots double-scanned in the first count, 3,075 repeated images in the recount, missing images, chain-of-custody lapses, a state monitor out of the room, observers who left believing counting was over. Real machine vulnerabilities exist (CISA's nine; a federal judge's "substantial concerns"; an unauthorised access to equipment in Coffee County). Detroit's windows were papered over, challengers of both parties were held at the door, and about 7 in 10 of its absentee counting boards were out of balance at the canvass. Arizona's Attorney General flagged chain-of-custody paperwork. None of these is evidence of a stolen election, and the episode says that too.
- **Proven fraud is stated plainly.** North Carolina's 9th District (2018): an organised absentee-ballot operation, a 5-0 bipartisan board vote to rerun the election, and the one case where the suspect pool exceeded the margin. Bridgeport (2023): party workers on police drop-box video handling other people's ballots, a judge who voided the primary, and nine people charged. Paterson (2020) is PROVEN as an all-mail election voided for procedural violations, while the criminal cases collapsed. Since 2024, organised schemes were proven or charged in Atlantic City, Millbourne, Frisco City, Hamtramck and Frio County, and three local elections were voided or rerun. Small, real cases (four dead-voter ballots in Georgia; eleven in Wisconsin; Iowa's 35 noncitizen ballots in 2024; Michigan's 16; Utah's 13 noncitizens who voted) are given with their denominators, not waved away.
- **The host's own hypotheses were tested in both directions.**
  - *What holds:* the follow-through on proven cases (the plea, the sentence) gets far less national coverage than the arrest, and the nonpartisan election press is where the follow-ups live (entries 236, 249). Walk-backs are quieter than the claims, on every side: settlement retractions with no on-air apology, a federal prosecutor's overstated release corrected quietly, big "potential noncitizen" numbers that shrank on checking with little attention (entries 239–242). Some officials really were slow (Hamtramck, Luzerne County). Refusing states did withhold data, and one of them (New Jersey) had a real registration failure and kept the names from federal investigators (entry 265). Noncitizens do get onto rolls and sometimes vote, often because a government system put them there (entries 344–360).
  - *What does not hold:* every vanished news story found was one that turned out to be false, removed through a settlement, a court record or the outlet's own correction; no true, proven fraud story was found taken down (entry 245). The one unexplained removal of official figures (Texas, 2023) followed a court ruling that ended many of those cases. The "refused to hand over the rolls" states mostly sent or offered the public roll and withheld three identifiers their own laws protect, and every court so far has agreed with them on the federal government's power (entries 256–259, 268). The "statistically impossible" counts were, case by case, known batches, typos, the wrong state, or a lopsided neighbourhood — on both sides (entries 278–316). The documented noncitizen voters are mostly lawful permanent residents, not people without legal status (entry 363).
- **The "other side" cases are pinned to the same standard.** The USPS claim (2020), the 2024 claims ("20 million missing votes", satellite internet, "statistical anomalies") and the 2024 "impossible zero" precinct are checked against the same kind of primary record as the 2020 clips. Where they had something real underneath (machine removals that spiked; pollbooks that do connect), it is said. The Pennsylvania undated-ballot fight is reported as what it is: not fraud, one real episode of officials voting against a court's instruction (reversed before anything was counted), and a genuine, unresolved rule-of-law versus disenfranchisement question.
- **Candidates and parties are not the subject.** Sides are described by role ("the losing candidate", "the then-President" only for his own recorded call). Officials appear by office. Private people (the two Fulton election workers, poll watchers, voters, relatives, defendants, wrongly flagged citizens) are never named. A party is named as an actor only where its own primary is the case (Bridgeport) or where a bipartisan board's seats are the point.
- **What the government says is attributed, not adopted.** The 2024 attributions to Russian actors are "the agencies said": the physical tells and the local officials' statements are what a viewer can check. The board director's letters, Homeland Security's deceased and noncitizen figures, the SAVE counts and the Justice Department's prosecution totals are claims by parties, labelled as such, with the shrink ratios wherever they were checked.

- **Second round (2026-10-13), the same test on the remaining ground.** The other side's "stolen" claims (Florida 2000, Ohio 2004, 2016's "hacked tallies", Georgia 2018) were checked against their official records exactly as 2020's were: two had a real thread (Florida's spoiled ballots; two administrative defects in Georgia) and two did not. Belief that an election was stolen tracks who lost, on both sides (entry 402). The vote-total question was answered with a test of the host's own hypothesis whose method was fixed before the numbers were computed, and the result is reported as it came out — partly supported (entries 460–462). The officials who checked 2020 in the closest states were often from the losing side's own party, and the same systems certified the other side's wins in 2024 (entries 424, 434). Insider manipulation is real and proven where it happened, in small local races, and was caught (entries 432–433). Proven wrongs in this round are stated at their true scale: Arizona's equipment failure and Cochise's misconduct; Puerto Rico's plebiscite programming error; Coffee and Mesa counties' breaches; Fulton County, Pennsylvania's breach of a court order; Nevada's and Pennsylvania's handful of illegal ballots.

## Open questions this episode could not settle
- Re-check on the day of the script: whether anyone has been charged in the FBI's Fulton County investigation, and where the government's Eleventh Circuit appeal over the quashed grand-jury subpoena stands (No. 26-12769).
- Re-check: whether the Justice Department has appealed or sought a stay of the Oct 9, 2026 ruling vacating its voter-roll program, and whether it seeks Supreme Court review of the Sixth Circuit's Michigan ruling (due Nov 12, 2026).
- Re-check: Pennsylvania's undated-ballot rule — the state Supreme Court's pending Baxter appeal, and the U.S. Supreme Court cert petitions in Eakin (Nos. 25-962 and 25-967), where the Solicitor General's views were requested on June 29, 2026.
- Re-check: the SAVE America Act's status in the Senate, and the Supreme Court argument in Arizona's proof-of-citizenship case, set for Dec 8, 2026.
- Were Maricopa County's eight noncitizen voters charged? AP and local outlets disagree (entry 237).
- Did a separate Wisconsin "ballots under a table" clip ever exist? Searches found none, but an absence of results is not proof. The episode says the clip people remember was filmed in Georgia, and stops there.
- The State Farm Arena footage itself was never frame-checked by this research. The containers are described only in the words of sworn and official accounts.
- The court-ordered monitor's report on Fulton 2020 was not read (it sits behind an account sign-up); its findings come through the documents that quote it.
- Homeland Security's deceased and noncitizen figures for Georgia and nationally have no published method or confirmation rate, and SAVE has no published error rate.
- How many of the 3,075 repeated recount images were counted? The state's lawyers said it cannot be decided conclusively.
- The written State Election Board report on the recount case, the Attorney General's August 2024 letter, the September 28, 2026 board minutes, and the state rule on pausing the count when watchers leave were not found or not opened.
- The Paterson judge's written opinion, the Wisconsin special counsel's report and Pennsylvania's 2024 audits were not opened, so nothing is cited from them.
- How many of Georgia's 20 noncitizen registrants (2024) had ever voted; how many of Virginia's 1,600 removed in 2024 were citizens; how many of Arizona's 218,000 voters without proof on file were noncitizens; how Florida's 2022 felony-voting cases ended.
- Why the Texas Attorney General's office removed its prosecution tally in 2023: no reason was stated.
- Pennsylvania's "205,000 more votes than voters": no official reconciliation of the voter database against the certified count was found, so the episode does not say the gap closed.
- Undetected fraud is, by definition, in none of these numbers; prosecution counts depend on how hard anyone looks.
- Whether lay viewers can use the full clip-checking method on election videos: no study has tested it. The episode presents it as what investigators in these cases did, not as a proven skill.
- Re-check on script day: the Postal Service rule's merits appeal (the government's First Circuit brief is reportedly due Nov 2, 2026), and any stay application before Nov 3.
- Re-check: the appeal dockets in the remaining defamation suit by the company formerly called Dominion (D.D.C. No. 1:21-cv-02131, where a default-judgment motion is pending), the Cochise County supervisor's felony case, and the appeals of the injunctions against the March 2025 executive order (including Ninth Circuit No. 26-1429).
- Puerto Rico 2024: whether the machines were ever intruded upon at all (not whether votes changed) — the federal contractor's report is unpublished and a records request is reportedly pending; the number of plebiscite votes affected by the programming error was not found.
- Nevada 2020's five narrow OPEN items (the signature machine's false-accept rate, the 4,057 noncitizen-list voters, out-of-state double voting, the voting-drive referral, and the other prosecution referrals), each bounded far below the 33,596 margin.
- Wisconsin 2020: a statewide count of care-facility absentee ballots requested and returned without special voting deputies; and the special counsel's final discipline order.
- Pennsylvania 2020: the Delaware County district attorney's disposition of the 2021 records-handling allegation; whether the 9,428 late-window ballots were added to the certified totals.
- Whether the federal security review of voting systems ordered in March 2025 has been delivered; whether any state has lost part of its grant over the new audit conditions.
- Why the FEC revised its 2020 totals by 46,228 votes; whether any presidential recount was demanded in Nevada in 2020.


## Re-check 2026-10-19 (before scripting): the affidavit wording and two viral Fulton numbers
_Folded from the 2026-10-19 re-check; every source is linked by its public URL. The quotes were spot-checked against the saved text by the main session._

### Item #19: what the affidavit and the warrant actually say

#### R19-1. The statutes: two federal laws, one a misdemeanor, one a felony
**CONFIRMED.** The application, the warrant and Attachment B all name 52 U.S.C. §§ 20701 and 20511.
- "there is probable cause to believe that violations of Title 52, United States Code, Sections 20701 (Retention and Preservation of Records of Elections); and 20511 (Deprivation of a Fair Election) have been committed by unknown persons." (affidavit para 4, ECF p. 6 [2])
- "Title 52, United States Code, Section 20701 requires election officers to retain election records for 22 months following an election for a federal office. … Violation of this statute is a misdemeanor." (para 7, ECF p. 6 [2])
- "Title 52, United States Code, Section 20511(2) makes it a crime to 'knowingly and willfully deprive[ ] … the residents of a State of a fair and impartially conducted election process, by … (b) the procurement, casting, or tabulation of ballots that are known by the person to be materially false, fictitious, or fraudulent under the laws of the State in which the election is held.' Violation of this statute is a felony." (para 8, ECF pp. 6–7 [2–3])
- "All records relating to violations of Title 52, United States Code, §§ 20701 and 20511 those violations occurring after October 12, 2020, including:" (Attachment B, items to be seized: all physical 2020 ballots including absentee envelopes; all tabulator tapes; all ballot images from the original count and the recount; all 2020 voter rolls) (Doc. 22-2, ECF p. 3)
- The application cover (sworn by telephone, Jan 28, 2026): the property "constitutes evidence of the commission of a criminal offense and property which has been used as the means of committing a criminal offense, concerning violations of Title 52, United States Code, Sections 20701 and 20511" (Doc. 22-2, ECF p. 1; read from the page image)
- https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.355087/gov.uscourts.gand.355087.22.2.pdf
- https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.355087/gov.uscourts.gand.355087.22.1_2.pdf

#### R19-2. "Unknown persons" belongs to the probable-cause sentence, not to the description of the investigation
**CORRECTED (wording).** The brief says the affidavit "describes a criminal investigation into whether any of
the errors were intentional, by 'unknown persons.'" The two halves come from different paragraphs. Para 6
describes the investigation, into whether any "improprieties" were intentional. Para 4 is the sworn
probable-cause statement, and that is where "unknown persons" appears: the agent says there is probable
cause that violations of both laws "have been committed by unknown persons."
- "Some of those allegations have been disproven while some of those allegations have been substantiated, including through admissions by Fulton County. This warrant application is part of an FBI criminal investigation into whether any of the improprieties were intentional acts that violated federal criminal laws." (para 6, ECF p. 6 [2])
- "have been committed by unknown persons." (para 4, ECF p. 6 [2])
- Same source and path as R19-1.

#### R19-3. The theory is conditional: "if" the defects were intentional
**CONFIRMED.** The affidavit's own framing of the defects is conditional throughout. Its conclusion names the
"false" votes it means.
- "If these deficiencies were the result of intentional action, it would be a violation of federal law regardless of whether the failure to retain records or the deprivation of a fair tabulation of a vote was outcome determinative for any particular election or race." (para 10, ECF p. 8 [4])
- "If these deficiencies were the result of intentional action, the election records identified in Attachment B are evidence of violations of Title 52 U.S.C. §§ 20511 and 20701. Seizure of the election records would corroborate the analysis that evinces that election records were destroyed and or the tabulation of votes included materially false votes, either through duplicated scanning of specific ballots, interjection of pristine ballots, or other methods described above." (conclusion, para 85 in Doc. 22-2, ECF p. 22 [18]; para 84 in Doc. 22-1, ECF p. 22 [18], read from the page image)
- "Such action would be an intentional tabulation of ballots in a false manner in violation of 52 U.S.C. § 20511." (para 35, the agent's gloss on an analyst's view that the duplicates "could be intentional but was not partisan", ECF p. 12 [9])
- Same source and path as R19-1.

#### R19-4. "Fictitious ballots" is the statute's word, not a finding
**CONFIRMED, with the base stated.** "Fictitious" appears twice in the affidavit: once in the quoted statute
(para 8), and once in a witness's hypothetical (para 65, spelled "fictious" in the original). The agent's own
named theory is "materially false votes" through duplicated scans or "pristine" ballots (para 85). News lines
like "probable cause … tabulating fictitious ballots" paraphrase the statute that was cited. They do not
report anything the affidavit says it found.
- "materially false, fictitious, or fraudulent" (the statute, as quoted in para 8, ECF p. 7 [3])
- "there is no way to determine if someone within the process of counting votes substituted fictious ballots for real ballots if the ballot totals matched the number of voters accounted for in absentee ballots." (para 65, the state's 2020 elections director, ECF p. 18 [14]). The next sentence: "if 100 extra ballots were introduced without removing real ballots, that would be caught during the reconciliation process" (same para). Para 66: he "was not aware of any discrepancies between voters counted and ballots counted."
- The judge's summary: "In [the agent's] view, the above irregularities, if intentionally caused, demonstrate violations of 52 U.S.C. § 20701 and 52 U.S.C. § 20511(2)(B)." (order of May 6, 2026, ECF p. 21)
- Same source as R19-1 · order: https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.355452/gov.uscourts.gand.355452.108.0.pdf

#### R19-5. What the magistrate judge found: probable cause to search, nothing more
**CONFIRMED.** The warrant records a finding that the affidavit "establishes probable cause to search and
seize". That is the standard for a search, not a finding that a crime happened.
- "I find that the affidavit establishes probable cause to search and seize the person or property" (warrant, case 1:26-MC-0177, signed 2:31 p.m., January 28, 2026; OCR of a scan)
- https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.355452/gov.uscourts.gand.355452.43.2.pdf

### RULING on the brief's sentence (brief.md, lines 28–29)
**Too soft, and it fuses two separate statements.** Every quote in the brief is real. But the sentence turns a
sworn probable-cause claim that two named federal crimes, one of them a felony, "have been committed by
unknown persons" into an investigation "by 'unknown persons.'" A viewer who later reads "probable cause …
fictitious ballots" in the news would think we left that out. The opposite overstatement is the trap too:
"the FBI found fictitious ballots" is false. The affidavit is the agent's sworn belief, its theory is
conditional ("If these deficiencies were the result of intentional action"), and the only judicial finding
is probable cause to search.

**Corrected sentence for the script** (≤ 2 sentences):
> In a sworn affidavit, the FBI agent said there was probable cause to believe "unknown persons" had broken
> two federal election laws: one on keeping election records, and a felony against counting ballots known to
> be false, fictitious or fraudulent. That is the agent's sworn claim, enough for a judge to approve a search,
> and the affidavit itself says the open question is whether any of the problems were intentional.

(If the script adds "no one has been charged", re-check it on the day: the brief's last check was Oct 13.)

**Corrected sentence for the brief** (same facts, brief register): "The affidavit swears there is probable
cause that 'unknown persons' violated two federal laws, a records-retention misdemeanor (52 U.S.C. § 20701)
and a felony against tabulating ballots 'known by the person to be materially false, fictitious, or
fraudulent' (§ 20511). It describes the investigation as into 'whether any of the improprieties were
intentional acts,' and its own conclusion is conditional: 'If these deficiencies were the result of
intentional action…'" Cite Doc. 22-2 (the executed warrant's affidavit).

---

### Item #20(a): "Fulton admitted 315,000 ballots / votes without poll-worker signatures"

#### R20-1. What was unsigned: tabulator tapes from early voting, not ballots
**CORRECTED.** The signatures at issue go on **tabulator tapes**, the paper printouts a scanner produces at
poll close. They are not on ballots. Georgia's rule says the poll manager and two witnesses sign each tape.
The case before the board (2022-015) was about early-voting closing tapes produced to a citizen's
open-records request.
- "Georgia Rules and Regulations require that tabulation tapes be printed from each machine at poll closing. These tapes must be signed by the poll manager and two witnesses. See Ga. Comp. R. & Regs. 183-1-12-.12." (affidavit para 42, ECF p. 14 [10])
- "Fulton County produced zero signed tabulator tapes and early voting, as you just heard. Not one for early voting." (the complainant, a private citizen, SEB transcript p. 225, Dec 9, 2025)
- https://seb.ga.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/2025-12-09__transcript.pdf

#### R20-2. The county conceded the tapes were unsigned; the "315,000" was the complainant's figure
**CORRECTED (attribution).** At the Dec 9, 2025 board meeting, the county's attorney conceded the rule
violation. In the whole two-day transcript the number 315,000 is spoken only by the complainant (the
transcript's word index lists three uses, all in his remarks). The county's attorney said she had "not seen
the tapes myself." Headlines saying "Fulton County admits … 315,000" and the FBI affidavit's para 51 ("Fulton
County stated that tabulator tapes accounting for 315,000 ballots were not properly signed") attach the
complainant's number to the county's concession. The judge's May 2026 order repeats the affidavit's sentence
without testing it.
- "I've not seen the -- the tapes myself, but I -- we do not dispute that the tapes were not signed. It was a violation of the rule." (the county's attorney, SEB transcript pp. 223–224 [PDF pp. 224–225])
- "Every tabulator tape represented 315,000 votes." … "those 315,000 votes are, by definition, uncertified. Fulton County's deliberate or grossly negligent destruction of the audit trail for more than 315,000 votes is a National disgrace." (the complainant, SEB transcript pp. 224, 226)
- "This case is about tapes that are not signed, which is a violation of a rule not a statute" (the county's attorney, SEB transcript p. 234)
- "During a December 9, 2025, meeting before the State Election Board, Fulton County stated that tabulator tapes accounting for 315,000 ballots were not properly signed." (affidavit para 51, ECF p. 16 [12])
- SEB transcript as in R20-1 · affidavit as in R19-1

#### R20-3. The number itself is about right: it matches Fulton's 2020 advance-voting total
**APPROXIMATE (CONFIRMED in size).** Fulton's official 2020 results put 314,985 presidential votes in the
"Advanced Voting" category, out of 528,777 ballots cast. So "about 315,000" is roughly every early in-person
vote in the county, the set whose closing tapes were at issue. The count of unsigned tapes varies by speaker:
the complainant said 134 unsigned; the board's vice chair said 138 closing tapes, with ten missing, against
148 memory cards.
- Fulton County 2020 General, official results feed (detail XML, timestamp 11/18/2020): President, "Advanced Voting Votes" 314,985; "Election Day Votes" 59,143; "Absentee by Mail Votes" 146,994; "Provisional Votes" 3,537; ballotsCast 528,777 (summed by this pass from the per-choice VoteType rows)
- "We received 134 that were unsigned." (complainant) … "148. … 138 there were ten missing." (the board's vice chair, SEB transcript p. 228)
- https://results.enr.clarityelections.com/GA/Fulton/105430/271723/reports/detailxml.zip

#### R20-4. What the board did: a 3–0 referral to the Attorney General, plus a notice to the Justice Department
**CONFIRMED.** The board referred the case to the state Attorney General, asking for a $5,000 civil penalty per
tape. On Dec 10 it amended the motion to notify the U.S. Justice Department as well. Two of the five members
were absent. This pass found no report of an Attorney General decision; the search was secondary only.
- "[A board member] amended her motion to refer the case to the Attorney General with a request that the Attorney General seek the missing tapes and a request to issue a civil penalty fine of $5,000 for each of the unsigned tabulator tapes that were produced as evidence" … "Vote: Passed unanimously (3-0)" (minutes, Dec 9, 2025, p. 10)
- "The Board reaffirms its decision on this case by adding a notification to the DOJ, due to concerns over missing tabulator tapes and unsecured memory cards." (minutes, Dec 10, 2025 entry, p. 17)
- Two of the five members, the chairman among them, are listed as absent (minutes, p. 1).
- https://seb.ga.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/2025-12-09__minutes.pdf

#### R20-5. Do unsigned tapes mean the votes were unverified? No: the paper was counted by hand
**CONFIRMED.** The tapes are a check on the machines. The votes are the paper ballots, and in 2020 every Fulton
ballot was counted by hand in the audit (statewide, the hand count matched the machines to within 0.1053%;
brief case 1). The affidavit itself records both the full hand count and the state's matching count. The
Secretary of State called the lapse clerical (his X post, quoted by two outlets). On the related memory-card
theory, the county's expert testified at the March 2026 hearing that early-voting memory cards were read on
other scanners by design, so results were not calculated before polls closed.
- "in the 2020 election all ballots were audited due to the closeness of the race." (affidavit para 50, ECF p. 16 [12]) · "the Georgia Secretary of State's Office also counted Fulton County's ballots and the number they got matched what was given by Fulton County." (para 75, ECF p. 20 [16])
- "all voters were verified with photo ID and lawfully cast their ballots." … "A clerical error at the end of the day does not erase valid, legal votes." (the Secretary of State on X, Dec 20, 2025, as quoted by Newsweek, Dec 24, 2025, and Atlanta News First, Dec 22, 2025; the post itself is the second in a thread whose first post could not be retrieved)
- "[The county's expert] explained that this was done specifically with tabulator machines used during early voting so that election officials did not have to send personnel back to each early voting location after the close of voting to retrieve the votes from those machines." … "nothing in this process indicates misconduct." (the order's summary of the county's expert, order ECF p. 24)
- https://www.newsweek.com/georgia-official-responds-to-315000-ballots-not-properly-signed-in-2020-11267106 · https://www.walb.com/2025/12/23/fulton-county-admits-verifying-315000-votes-2020-without-poll-worker-signatures/ · the post: https://x.com/GaSecofState/status/2002435035660722589 · order as in R19-4

#### R20-6. How it spread
**CONFIRMED (reach unmeasured).** A Dec 18, 2025 post on X: "Fulton County admits certifying 315,000
fraudulent early votes without poll worker signatures." (as quoted by Lead Stories, which rated the fraud
framing unsupported). TV headlines ran it as "Fulton County admits to verifying 315,000 votes in 2020 without
poll worker signatures" (Atlanta News First, syndicated to WALB, WTOC and WRDW).
- https://leadstories.com/hoax-alert/2025/12/fact-check-fulton-county-315000-unsigned-early-votes-are-not-proof-of-electoral-fraud.html

**Tray: PROVEN as a broken rule; EXPLAINED as "315,000 illegal / uncertified votes".** OPEN test: (a) there is
a named gap: ten closing tapes are missing, and the board found no opening ("zero") tapes for early voting;
(b) the evidence that would settle *whether the early votes were counted right* already exists and has been
used: the paper ballots, counted in full by hand, matched; (c) the FBI now holds those ballots. Because (b)
is already in the record, the gap is in the paperwork, not in the count. It fails the OPEN test for the
claim as shared.

**Recommendation: CASE-FILE IT.** It is the most-shared Fulton claim of the past year, it is in the
affidavit, and it teaches a clean skill: read what the number counts (tapes, i.e. receipts, not ballots) and
who said it (a complainant, not the county). Draft, in the brief's style:

> ### "Fulton admitted 315,000 votes had no poll-worker signatures" — December 2025 — **PROVEN as a broken rule; EXPLAINED as illegal votes**
> - **As shared:** the county "admits" 315,000 early votes were counted without poll-worker signatures, so
>   they were illegal or uncertified.
> - **Real:** the signatures go on tabulator tapes, the printouts a scanner makes at poll close, and Georgia's
>   rule says a poll manager and two witnesses sign each one. At the State Election Board on December 9,
>   2025, the county's attorney said "we do not dispute that the tapes were not signed. It was a violation of
>   the rule." The board voted 3–0 to refer it to the Attorney General and asked for $5,000 per tape. Ten
>   closing tapes are still missing.
> - **The number:** 315,000 was the complainant's figure, not the county's (the FBI affidavit later repeated
>   it as the county's). It is about right: Fulton's official results show 314,985 early in-person votes.
> - **Not real:** that those votes went unchecked. The tapes are receipts; the votes are the paper ballots,
>   and every Fulton ballot was counted again by hand in November 2020. The hand count matched.

---

### Item #20(b): "380,000 ballot images are missing"

#### R20-7. Where the number came from: an activist group in 2021–22, then a board member in May 2024
**CONFIRMED (origin).** The claim began with an activist group, VoterGA: at a Nov 9, 2021 news conference it said
Fulton had no images of in-person ballots, and in 2022 it said "374,128 original images" were "completely
missing." The 380,761 version came from a State Election Board member's questions at the May 7, 2024
meeting. A viral X post the same day said the board "CONFIRMS" it.
- "In an email to Snopes, [the group's co-founder] stated that Fulton County informed VoterGA that it did not have digital images of in-person voter ballots in response to a public records request submitted by VoterGA." (Snopes, Nov 10, 2021; name bracketed out here)
- "with 374,128 original images completely missing" (a March 2022 report of VoterGA's 15-point analysis, CPI / the Georgia Record)
- "Do you know why 380, 761 ballot images from election day machine count are not available?" (a board member, SEB transcript May 7, 2024, p. 177; OCR)
- "BREAKING: Georgia Election Board CONFIRMS Over 300,000 Ballot Images From 2020 ELECTION DAY Are MISSING" (X post, May 7, 2024, as quoted by Lead Stories, May 9, 2024)
- https://www.snopes.com/fact-check/georgia-verify-2020-election/ (read via r.jina.ai) · https://cpi.org/2022/03/the-georgia-record-irrefutable-evidence-of-electronic-manipulation-in-fulton-countys-2020-results/ · https://seb.ga.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/2024-05-07__transcript.pdf · https://leadstories.com/hoax-alert/2024/05/fact-check-no-evidence-over-300000-ballot-images-are-missing-from-2020-election-in-fulton-county-georgia-results-are-not-invalid.html

#### R20-8. The 374,128 is exactly Fulton's in-person presidential vote, not a count of missing files
**CONFIRMED (arithmetic, this pass).** Advance-voting votes (314,985) plus Election Day votes (59,143) for president =
374,128, VoterGA's figure to the digit. So the number is the size of the in-person count, restated as missing
images. This pass found no official source for 380,761. The Secretary of State's spokesperson told PolitiFact
he did not know where it came from.
- Fulton 2020 official results, as in R20-3: "Advanced Voting Votes" 314,985 + "Election Day Votes" 59,143 = 374,128
- The Secretary of State's spokesperson did not know where the figure came from (PolitiFact's paraphrase); PolitiFact: "State and local officials did not substantiate this claim." (PolitiFact, May 24, 2024)
- https://www.politifact.com/article/2024/may/24/fact-checking-misleading-claims-of-2020-election-f/ · results file as in R20-3

#### R20-9. In May 2024 nobody confirmed it: investigators had the recount images, the county had not heard of it
**CONFIRMED.**
- "We subpoenaed Fulton County for all of their ballot images. We received approximately 518, 000 ballot images." … "What was subpoenaed was the ballot images for the recount because that's what this case was about, not the election day." (the state investigator, SEB transcript May 7, 2024, p. 177; OCR, line order reconstructed from the OCR)
- "We've not been apprised of this allegation. This is the first time I'm hearing about. And I have no idea what's the basis of that allegation." (the county's attorney, p. 178; OCR)
- Same transcript as R20-7.

#### R20-10. But the substance was later conceded: most in-person original-count images were not kept
**APPROXIMATE (substantiated in substance).** By January 2026 the FBI affidavit reported that the county had
admitted, in a federal civil action, that it did not preserve most in-person images from the original count.
The AJC confirmed the county admits images are missing from both counts; about 3% of the recount images are
missing. So "hundreds of thousands of original-count images are gone" is roughly right. This pass did not
find the civil-action filing itself; the affidavit does not name the case. What is not right is everything
the claim adds. The images are pictures; the ballots exist, were counted by hand, and are now held by the FBI.
- "Fulton County has admitted that it does not have scanned images of all the 528,777 ballots counted during the Original Count or the 527,925 ballots counted during the Recount." (affidavit para 9a, ECF p. 7 [3])
- "During a Civil Action in the United States District Court for the Northern District of Georgia, Fulton County Board of Registration and Elections admitted it has not preserved the majority of ballot images from in-person voting for the November 3, 2020, Original Count. This is another impediment to ruling out non-criminal explanations for the activities during the election." (para 28, ECF p. 11 [7])
- "This is true — the county has admitted it's missing images from both counts." … "About 3% of the 524,000 images from the presidential recount are missing." … "the county has said it still has the physical paper ballots cast in the 2020 election." (AJC, Feb 25, 2026)
- "images are not used to tally votes." (the Secretary of State's spokesperson, PolitiFact, May 24, 2024)
- Affidavit as in R19-1 · https://www.ajc.com/politics/2026/02/the-fbis-investigation-of-fulton-records-is-a-trip-down-memory-lane/ · PolitiFact as in R20-8

#### R20-11. ⚠ A discrepancy to settle before the script: was keeping images required in 2020?
**UNRESOLVED.** The fact-check log (entry 43) and the brief say Georgia "did not require counties to keep ballot
images" in 2020 (the state investigator, May 2024; PolitiFact quotes the Secretary of State's spokesperson
saying the same). The AJC (Feb 25, 2026) agrees on state and federal **law** but adds: "But a State Election
Board rule required them to be kept." This pass did not open the rule. Until someone reads the 2020 text of
the board's rules, the script should say "state law did not require it", not "nothing required it".
- "At the time, state and federal law did not require counties to preserve ballot images." … "But a State Election Board rule required them to be kept." (AJC, Feb 25, 2026)

**Tray: PROVEN as a record-keeping failure (most in-person original-count images not kept; about 3% of recount
images missing); EXPLAINED as "missing ballots" or "the result cannot be verified".** OPEN test: (a) a named
gap exists: the images; (b) the settling evidence for the count is the paper ballots, which were counted in
full by hand and matched; (c) the FBI holds them now. The gap is real, but it does not leave the count
unverifiable, so it is not OPEN for the claim as shared. (Whether any image was lost *on purpose* is already
inside the brief's existing OPEN item, "Fulton's repeated scans and missing images, accident or intent".)

**Recommendation: FOLD INTO CASE FILE 4, not a case file of its own.** The brief already covers missing images
under case 4 ("Fulton was badly run, not shown to be rigged"). The viral number adds one teachable line, not
a new case: the 374,128 is the in-person vote count relabelled as missing images, and the paper behind it was
counted by hand. A separate case file would repeat case 4's facts. Draft insert for case 4:

> - **The viral number, "380,000 ballot images missing":** an activist group said in 2021–22 that all of
>   Fulton's in-person ballot images were gone, and put the figure at 374,128, which is exactly the county's
>   in-person vote for president. In 2024 a board member asked about 380,761; no official source for that
>   number has been found. The core turned out to be partly true: the county has since admitted it did not
>   keep most original-count images of in-person ballots, and about 3% of the recount images are missing. But
>   an image is a picture of a ballot, not the vote. The paper ballots exist, every one was counted by hand in
>   2020, and the FBI now holds them.

**If the build wants one Short instead:** pair the two numbers in one "big Fulton numbers" Short. Both are true
counts relabelled: tapes (receipts) read as ballots, and pictures read as votes.

### Rows for the corrections table

| Claim as it stood → what the record shows | Found by | Source |
|---|---|---|
| The affidavit "describes a criminal investigation into whether any of the errors were intentional, by 'unknown persons'" → the agent swore probable cause that "unknown persons" violated two federal laws, a records misdemeanor (§ 20701) and a felony against tabulating ballots known to be "materially false, fictitious, or fraudulent" (§ 20511); the investigation is "into whether any of the improprieties were intentional acts"; the conclusion is conditional (R19-1 to R19-3) | re-check 10-19 | source — the affidavit, Doc. 22-2 |
| The affidavit linked (Doc. 22-1) is the executed one → 22-1 is the first (Election Hub) warrant's affidavit; the executed warrant's is Doc. 22-2, same text plus para 84 (notice) | re-check 10-19 | source — Doc. 22 notice |
| "Fulton admitted 315,000 ballots without poll-worker signatures" → unsigned **tabulator tapes** from early voting; the county conceded unsigned tapes; 315,000 was the complainant's number (≈ the 314,985 early in-person votes); the paper ballots were hand counted (R20-1 to R20-5) | re-check 10-19 | source — SEB transcript + minutes, Dec 9–10, 2025; Fulton results |
| The affidavit says "Fulton County stated" the 315,000 → in the transcript the figure is said only by the complainant (R20-2) | re-check 10-19 | source — SEB transcript |
| "380,000 ballot images missing" → origin an activist group's 374,128 (= Fulton's in-person presidential vote) and a board member's 380,761 (no official source); later substantiated in part: the county admits most in-person original-count images were not kept and about 3% of recount images are missing; the ballots exist and were hand counted (R20-7 to R20-10) | re-check 10-19 | source — SEB transcript May 7, 2024; affidavit; AJC |
| Images "were not required" in 2020 → state law did not require them; AJC says an SEB rule did; unresolved (R20-11) | re-check 10-19 | AJC, Feb 25, 2026 |

## Script and screen figures (added at scripting, 2026-10-19)
Rounded on screen from figures confirmed above (case file 26, top-up T8): **81.3 million** = 81,283,501 (the 2020 winner's
total); **74.2 million** = 74,223,975 (the runner-up's); **69.5 million** = 69,498,516 (the old record, 2008). Spoken as
"eighty-one million", "seventy-four million", "about sixty-nine and a half million". **6.25 million** is the certified gap
between the two elections (case file 19). **About 315,000** unsigned early-voting tape votes: the complainant's figure,
≈ the 314,985 early in-person votes (re-check R20-3).
